Products not saved since then have null, shown as "—".
  - **`CurrentPrice`** (`decimal?`, per sellable unit, VAT included; added 2026-09-19 at the user's request, per piece until Quantity Required on 2026-09-20): what the shop actually charges now, entered in the Products header beside the computed Selling Price and saved with the product (null until entered; `ProductValidator` refuses a negative one). A clone copies it. It prices nothing: the header shows the **Markup %** it earns, `CostingRules.CalculateMarkupPercentFromPrice` — VAT taken out first, then the markup on the base cost, `(CurrentPrice ÷ (1 + VAT%) − BaseCost) ÷ BaseCost` — the same definition as the costing's Margin, so the computed Selling Price gives back exactly the Margin % (the user's choice, over a markup on the material cost alone).
  - **Each usage has its own piece size, as total weight, yield and weight per piece** (2026-09-14, at the user's request). The user types either the **weight per piece** (grams) or the **yield** (pieces per batch), and the other is worked out from the batch's total weight. A usage stores only what was typed: **`AuthoredField`** (`UsageAuthoredField.WeightPerPiece` or `Yield`) and **`AuthoredValue`** (≥ 0, `CK_ProductSubAssemblyUsages_AuthoredValue`). Until then it stored `WeightPerPiece` only; the migration carried every one over as a typed weight.
    - **Neither figure is required** (2026-09-20, at the user's request): a section goes into the recipe, and saves, before how much of it a piece takes is known, stored as `AuthoredValue` 0. `ProductRules.EnsureValidUsages` and the check constraint both take 0 and refuse only a negative (migration `OptionalUsagePieceSize`); `ResolvePieceSize` already had no other figure to work out from 0, so an unsized section has a batch but costs the product nothing per piece, and the form's caption says so. It was refused on Apply (`EnsurePieceSizeEntered`, now gone) and at the repository before that.
    - **Each usage has its own shrinkage too** (2026-09-22, at the user's request: "Add Shrinkage % on top of the Weight per Piece (g) in the Recipe Structure. Final Weight (g) = Total Weight (g) * (1 - Shrinkage %)"). **`ShrinkagePercent`** is what the batch loses being made — a dough baked off, a filling reduced — at least 0 and under 100 (`CK_ProductSubAssemblyUsages_ShrinkagePercent`, migration `UsageShrinkagePercent`, which carried every existing usage over at 0). It belongs to the *usage*, not the sub-assembly: two products can bake the same dough to different weights. **`ProductRules.CalculateFinalWeight(totalWeight, shrinkagePercent)`** is the formula, the same one `RawMaterial.WeightLossPercent` uses one layer down (`IngredientBomRules.CalculateCost`). **The final weight is what the pieces are cut from** (the user's choice, over showing it and feeding nothing): the weight per piece is the *finished* piece, the yield is the final weight over it, and the batch keeps its whole cost, so shrinking a batch leaves fewer, dearer pieces. 1,700 g at 10% → 1,530 g; a typed 60 g piece yields 25.5, not 28.33.
  - **`ProductRules.ResolvePieceSize(authoredField, authoredValue, finalWeight)`** is the one formula: weight typed → `Yield = FinalWeight / WeightPerPiece`; yield typed → `WeightPerPiece = FinalWeight / Yield`. It's worked out on every read, never stored, which is what makes **the typed figure stay put when the batch or the shrinkage changes**: a 60 g piece stays 60 g and the yield moves; a 24-piece yield stays 24 and each piece gets heavier or lighter. Being computed on read, an owner's line edit never has to update other products' rows. A batch that weighs nothing has no other figure (0). Every caller works the final weight out first — `ProductUsageEdit`'s constructor, `ProductsViewModel.RecomputeUsageForm` and `UpdateOtherPieceSizeFigure`, and `ReportsViewModel` — so the rule itself stays one line.
  - Usage cost per piece = `SubAssembly.TotalCost × WeightPerPiece / FinalWeight`, with the resolved weight per piece, which comes to the same thing as `TotalCost ÷ Yield`. For example, 100g/₱24 filling used at 10g/piece gives ₱2.40/piece, yield 10; reduce that filling 20% and the same 10 g finished piece carries ₱3.00, yield 8.
  - **Per-piece line breakdown** (2026-09-14): a piece takes `WeightPerPiece / TotalWeight` of the batch, so it takes that share of every line. `ProductRules.CalculateLineGramsPerPiece` scales a line's grams and `CalculateLineCostPerPiece` its cost (grams × buffered cost per gram), with the same share. A usage's lines add up to its cost per piece and its weight per piece. The Recipe Structure showed this per line until 2026-09-16, when a sub-assembly's tab became its form. **Nothing calls these two rules now** — the Costing tab's Cost Breakdown works off `ProductCostSummary`, a piece at a time, not line by line. They're kept, with their tests, because they're the costing rule for a line's share of a piece and a per-line view is the obvious thing to want back. **If one returns, it must divide by the usage's *effective* batch after shrinkage** (`ProductRules.CalculateEffectiveBatch`, then `CalculateFinalWeight`), not the sub-assembly's stored totals, or a linked usage's add-ins would be left out of every line's share and a shrinking batch would under-price every line; their third parameter is named `finalWeight` for that reason.
  - **UsageAddInLine: a product's own extras on top of a sub-assembly it links** (2026-09-16, at the user's request: "the first datagrid has headers containing the linked recipe and the bottom one contains some additional recipe like food colors, flavoring etc"). The same dough goes into several products, but one wants ube flavouring and another pandan colour; until then the only way was **Make a Copy**, which breaks the link, so the dough stopped being shared and the owner's changes stopped reaching it.
    - `UsageAddInLine` (`UsageId` Cascade, `IngredientId` Restrict, `Grams`; unique per usage + ingredient) hangs off the **usage**, not the sub-assembly: two products linking the same dough each keep their own set, and neither moves `SubAssembly.TotalWeight`/`TotalCost`, which stay the owner's. The only cascade path in is `Products → ProductSubAssemblyUsages → UsageAddInLines`, a chain rather than a second path, which SQL Server allows because a usage's own sub-assembly FK is Restrict.
    - **Add-ins go into the batch** (the user's choice, over "a fixed amount per piece"): the usage's **effective batch** is the sub-assembly's plus its add-ins (`ProductRules.CalculateEffectiveBatch`, over `SubAssemblyRules.CalculateTotals`), so 1,700 g of dough with 20 g of flavouring is a 1,720 g batch and a piece takes its weight-per-piece share of the whole thing. `ResolvePieceSize` and `CalculateUsageCostPerPiece` were **unchanged**: they just receive the effective figures. The typed figure still stays put, so adding a flavouring moves the other one.
    - **Linked usages only.** A new section (a sub-assembly of the product's own, until 2026-09-23) has no add-ins — its extras just go in its lines, and a second table there would be two ways to do one thing.
    - **An ingredient the linked recipe already has is refused** (`ProductRules.EnsureValidAddIns`, the user's choice over merging the grams): "Water is already in Variety Bread Dough. Add-ins are only for what its recipe doesn't have — change how much it uses where Variety Bread Dough is kept, or make this product a copy of it." Checked in the form the moment Apply is pressed, and again in `SetRecipeAsync`'s pre-flight and `UsageAddInLineRepository.SetLinesAsync` as it writes. An empty set is valid, unlike a sub-assembly's own lines: most linked usages take the recipe as it is.
      - That guard is load-bearing beyond validation: since an add-in can never duplicate a base ingredient, **Make a Copy (and `ProductCloner`'s owned branch, until Clone started linking on 2026-09-23) concatenate the base lines and the add-ins** into the new sub-assembly's lines without a merge step, and the result always passes `EnsureValidLines`. The batch it was showing is the batch it keeps. A clone of a *standalone* link stays linked, so its add-ins stay add-ins.
    - **Written with the rest of the recipe**: `LinkedUsageDraft.AddIns` (an `init` property, so the existing positional call sites are untouched). `SetRecipeAsync` adds the linked usages together, saves once so every one has the id its add-ins need, then writes each one's lines — the same "save for the id, then reline" shape the owned loop already uses. `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.GetAddInsForProductAsync` reads them back, on the interface Products and Reports already inject, so no ViewModel constructor changed.
    - **`ReportsViewModel.CalculateMarginRowsAsync` had to learn about them**: it was the only place that costed a product without going through `ProductUsageEdit`, reading a sub-assembly's stored totals as the usage's batch, so without `CalculateEffectiveBatch` the margin chart silently understated every product with add-ins. **Since 2026-09-27 it has no pricing of its own**: it prices each product through `ViewModels/SavedProductReader` (see the Products screen's Print), as the Products screen prices the product it opens, and `ReportsViewModelTests` kept their figures, which shows the two agree. (The chart became the Product Cost Summary the same day, `ReportsViewModel.ReadProductCostsAsync`.)
  - A product has **no yield of its own**. Each usage row has its own yield, and those will usually differ between a product's sub-assemblies. That's expected, not an error.
  - Product material cost per piece = the sum of all its usages' cost-per-piece, plus its packaging lines' cost-per-piece.
  - Sub-assembly and product weights are raw input weights, and baking/moisture loss is not modeled for them. Made ingredients are the only place weight loss is modeled (`WeightLossPercent`).
- **PackagingLine**: one packaging material used by one whole sellable unit of a product — its wrapper, its box, its label, its ribbon. "Unit" throughout this bullet means that sellable unit, which is one finished piece unless the product's `QuantityRequired` says otherwise.
  - FK to a `RawMaterial` of the Packaging type (`PackagingLineRepository` rejects an ingredient), so packaging price changes cascade like ingredient price changes.
  - **`UnitId?`** (`PackagingMaterialUnitId` before the raw materials merge): the unit the line's quantities are entered in — one of the material's own units, or its base unit when empty. Quantities are stored as entered, in that unit. (A length-only `EntryUnitId` existed until the length units were removed on 2026-09-13; this general unit replaced it the same day.)
  - **`PackId?` is gone** (2026-09-20, with `ProductPack`; see Quantity Required below). It pointed at the `ProductPack` a line packed, null meaning a per-piece line. Its FK had to be `Restrict` rather than `Cascade`, since `PackagingLine` already cascades from `Product` and `ProductPack` did too, and a second cascade path `Products -> PackagingLines` is something SQL Server refuses — so `ProductPackRepository.SetPacksAsync` and `ProductRepository.DeleteAsync` deleted a pack's lines by hand, lines before packs. All of that went with it: `ProductRepository.DeleteAsync` now just deletes the product and lets its cascades do the rest.
  - **`QtyPerUnit`** (`QtyPerPiece` until 2026-09-18): amount of the packaging material used by one sellable unit, in the line's unit (12.7 for 12.7 cm of ribbon, `0.1667` for a 6-piece box, 1 for a label). **Typed, and never rescaled**: changing a product's `QuantityRequired` from 8 to 12 doesn't touch it, so the packaging is revisited by hand. The Packaging tab's caption says which unit the figures are for (`ProductsViewModel.CostUnitCaption`), so that stays visible rather than silent.
  - **`UnitsPerPackagingUnit`** (`PiecesPerPackagingUnit` until 2026-09-18): sellable units served by one of the line's unit of the packaging material (6 for that box). **Both were renamed when `PackId` arrived** (2026-09-18), since "piece" stopped being true for a pack's line; the names outlived packs, because "unit" is now the sellable unit. The columns were renamed by a generated migration (`PackagingLinesServeUnits`) with no data loss.
  - **`AuthoredField`**: records which of the two the user typed. The other is recalculated as its reciprocal on every edit. Ribbon is naturally entered as `QtyPerUnit`, a box as `UnitsPerPackagingUnit`. Its members are stored as ints, so they may be renamed but **never reordered**.
  - The unique index is **`{ProductId, PackagingMaterialId}`**: one line per material per product, a material a unit takes twice over belonging on a single line at the combined quantity. It was `{ProductId, PackId, PackagingMaterialId}` with `HasFilter(null)` while packs existed — the filter being required, since EF's SQL Server convention would otherwise have added `[PackId] IS NOT NULL` and dropped every per-piece line out of the index (the same trap `SubAssemblyConfiguration` documents). `ProductQuantityRequiredReplacesPacks` put the two-column index back.
  - Cost per unit = `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit × QtyPerUnit` converted to base units (`ProductRules.CalculatePackagingLineCost(costPerBaseUnit, qtyPerUnit, baseUnitsPerEntryUnit?)`, passed the buffered cost), computed on read. It is a cost per *sellable unit*, so `CalculateMaterialCost` adds it once however many pieces that unit holds.
  - Example: a ribbon costs ₱45.00 per roll. Its base unit is cm and a roll holds 1000 cm, so it costs ₱0.045 per cm. One product uses 12.7 cm per piece (₱0.5715); another is entered as 0.08 m per piece (8 cm → ₱0.36).
  - Storing both directions differs from `ProductSubAssemblyUsage` on purpose. People describe packaging quantities both ways, but they describe recipe weights only one way.
- **Quantity Required** (`Product.QuantityRequired`, 2026-09-20, at the user's request: "first let's have the product a quantity required. then we can set the necessary packaging in the subassembly"): how many finished pieces make one sellable unit. 1 for a product sold by the piece, which is nearly all of them; 8 for "Ensaymada Box of 8". `int`, defaulting to 1 in the CLR and in the database, `CK_Products_QuantityRequired` keeping it positive, and `ProductValidator` refusing anything below 1 on the screen.
  - **The one rule it establishes: the recipe is authored per piece, costing and pricing are per sellable unit, and this is the bridge.** `ProductRules.CalculateMaterialCost(usageCostsPerPiece, quantityRequired, packagingCosts)` = `usageCostsPerPiece.Sum() × quantityRequired + packagingCosts.Sum()` — only the usages are multiplied, since a packaging line is already for one whole unit. It replaced `CalculateMaterialCostPerPiece` and `CalculatePackMaterialCost`, whose bodies it merges. At a quantity of 1 it is exactly the old per-piece figure, so nothing that existed changed value.
  - **`ProductConfiguration` sets `HasDefaultValue(1).HasSentinel(-1)`.** The database default is what let the migration add the column to existing rows without failing the check constraint. The sentinel is what moves "leave it to the database" off 0, which EF would otherwise take it to be: without it a product explicitly saved as 0 pieces would be quietly stored as 1 on an insert while being refused on an update.
  - **It replaced `ProductPack`** (2026-09-18 to 2026-09-20), which was one sellable pack of a product — "Pack of 8", "Pack of 12" — with its own `PiecesPerPack` and its own packaging lines, shown on a **Packs** tab. Create Variant (2026-09-19) made it redundant, and a pack had turned out to be a second-class SKU: it never reached Reports (`ReportsViewModel` costs per piece, and `PackagingLineRepository` filtered `PackId IS NULL`), it had no `CurrentPrice`, Markup %, category, description or `UpdatedAt`, it had no row in the Products list, it was never linkable so two products sharing a box format each kept their own copy, and it cost the schema the `PackId` complications above. **A pack is now a product**: a variant of the single, with a Quantity Required and a packaging set of its own.
  - **The worked example**, which `ProductQuantityRequiredPersistenceTests` pins: Ensaymada takes 10 g of a 1,000 g / ₱50 dough, so ₱0.50 of recipe a piece. "Ensaymada Box of 8" is **Create Variant** of it — the recipe linked, so still 10 g a piece — with Quantity Required 8 and its packaging linked to a standalone **packaging set** holding the 8 bags (`QtyPerSet` 8) and the one box (`QtyPerSet` 1). Its material cost is `8 × ₱0.50 + 8 × ₱0.25 + ₱12 = ₱18`.
  - **The per-piece wrapper is written down twice**, which was the user's choice (2026-09-20) over giving a product a second packaging slot. A product's packaging is its own lines **or** one `PackagingLink`, never both (`CK_PackagingLinks_OneTarget`), so a boxed variant can't read the single's wrapper *and* link a box set; the set carries both instead. Prices still cascade on their own — both point at the same `RawMaterial` — and only the composition is duplicated. The other consequence of that choice was that `SubAssemblyPackagingLine` had no unit column, so a ribbon a product entered in metres had to go into a set in cm; `UnitId` (2026-09-20) put that right, and the two now hold a quantity the same way.
  - **Pricing is linear in the sub-assembly cost** (labor/overhead/administrative are percentages of it, packaging passes straight through, margin and VAT are multiplicative), so **a unit of 8 with no packaging of its own prices at exactly 8 times the single's price** — its own packaging is the whole of the difference. That's pinned by a test in `CostingRulesTests`, as it was for packs.
  - **Every figure on the Products screen is per sellable unit**: `ProductHeaderCard`'s Material Cost, Base Cost, Current Price and Markup %, and the whole of `CostingCard`'s breakdown. `ProductsViewModel.CostUnitCaption` ("Per piece", or "Per unit of 8 pieces") is bound into their tooltips, the breakdown's column header and the Packaging tab's caption, so which unit is never in doubt. The recipe tabs stay per piece: a usage's weight per piece, yield and cost per piece are untouched by this.
  - **Typed in on `ProductDetailsCard`**, beside Current Price (a Syncfusion `IntegerTextBox`, `MinValue` 1), since that card is where the product's own typed fields live and the header holds only read-only figures. Undo covers it (`ProductFormSnapshot.QuantityRequired`), a clone and a variant both copy it, and Reports' Product Cost Summary is per unit too (through `SavedProductReader`), with a Pieces column saying how many.
  - Gone with the Packs tab: `ProductPack`, `IProductPackRepository`/`ProductPackRepository`, `ProductPackValidator`, `ProductPackRow`, `PacksCard`, `PackCostSummary`, `ProductRules.EnsureValidPacks` and `CalculatePackMaterialCost`, the `packs` parameter of `IProductRepository.SaveAsync`, and `PackState` in the form snapshot. `ProductsViewModel.LinksTab` went back from 3 to 2.
  - **Migration `ProductQuantityRequiredReplacesPacks`**, which has **one hand-added statement** — the second in the project, after `DropLengthUnitsAndPackagingLineEntryUnit` — `DELETE FROM PackagingLines WHERE PackId IS NOT NULL;` before the column is dropped. Without it a pack's own lines would be left behind as ordinary packaging lines of their product (a box charged on every sellable unit), and a material that wrapped the piece and packed a pack, or two packs sharing one, would break the two-column unique index as it is created. The user's call was that the few packs that existed are re-entered as variants rather than converted.
- **Composite products** (`Product.IsComposite`, 2026-09-29, at the user's request: "A composite product is a product composed of two or more products"): a product made of other products and its own packaging, such as a Pasalubong Box of 6 Ensaymada and 4 Cheese Rolls. The user took the recommended option on each of four questions:
  - **A kind of product holding products and packaging only, fixed once saved** (over letting any product take in whole products beside its own sub-assemblies).
    - It's picked with New ▸ New composite product, or New Composite Product (Here) in the list.
    - `CompositeProductRules.EnsureKindUnchanged`, in `ProductRepository.UpdateAsync`, refuses a change, as `RawMaterialRules.EnsureTypeUnchanged` does for a raw material.
    - `ProductComponentRepository.SetComponentsAsync` refuses products for a product of sub-assemblies.
    - **Its own sections are only in product-owned roles** (2026-09-29, the user's follow-up: "Besides packaging a composite can have another subassembly such as topping, filling… we can reuse the Role settings"). The user chose reusing `SubAssemblyRole.IsProductOwned` (over a second per-role checkbox, and over any role), and refusing to untick it while a composite uses the role (over letting existing sections stay).
      - `CompositeProductRules.EnsureSectionRoles`, run by `SaveAsync` through `ProductComponentRepository.EnsureSectionRolesAsync`, refuses a section, new or linked, in any other role, naming it.
      - `SubAssemblyRoleRepository.UpdateAsync` refuses unticking Product-owned while a composite has a section in the role, and `SubAssemblyRepository`'s standalone save refuses moving a sub-assembly a composite links into a role that isn't product-owned. Both name the composites (`CompositesUsingRoleAsync`).
      - A composite is one unit, so a section's piece size is per whole composite, and its cost per piece joins the products' lines in the recipe cost. A composite held in another brings that whole recipe cost, sections included.
      - Clone and Create Variant copy or link its sections as any product's (`DerivedRecipe`). Raw Materials' Used By needed nothing new: `SectionUsers` already takes any product's sections, and `RecipeUsers` climbs from there.
    - It's always **one sellable unit**, `QuantityRequired` 1. `CompositeProductRules.EnsureOneUnit` (in the static Add and Update), `ProductValidator` and `CK_Products_CompositeIsOneUnit` all enforce it.
  - **Each product brings its recipe cost, as a Box of 8 does** (over bringing its recipe and its packaging, and over its full base cost with only the composite's markup and VAT on top).
    - A line brings the product's Sub-assemblies (ingredients) figure for one sellable unit, × the line's quantity (`CompositeProductRules.CalculateComponentCost`).
    - Its packaging stays out, so the composite's packaging covers the box and anything wrapping the pieces. This is the same choice as the per-piece wrapper written into a Box of 8's set (2026-09-20).
    - The composite's own labor, overhead, admin, markup and VAT apply to that recipe cost as for any product. So `ProductCostSummary` takes the lines, then its sections, as its "usages" at a quantity of 1, and every formula is unchanged; `isComposite` and `hasSections` only rename the rows (`PartsTitle` "Products (ingredients)", or "Products and sub-assemblies (ingredients)" with sections; `PartsBase` "products", or "products and sub-assemblies").
    - Pricing stays linear: products at the same percentages and a composite with no packaging price at exactly the sum of their prices. `SavedProductReaderCompositeTests` pins it: 2.50 → 5.096 and 5.00 → 10.192 make 12.50 → 25.48.
  - **Include each product's packaging** (`Product.IncludesComponentPackaging`, 2026-09-30, at the user's request: "a checkbox option … to include individual product packaging when the Product is composite"). Off by default, so the rule above holds unless it's ticked, for pieces that go into the box already wrapped.
    - **It joins the composite's packaging, not its recipe cost** (the user's choice, over folding it into each line's Recipe Cost): labor, overhead and administrative, percentages of the recipe alone, don't go on it, as for any packaging. So products at the same percentages still price at exactly the sum of their prices with their packaging in, which `SavedProductReaderCompositeTests` pins.
    - A line brings its product's Packaging figure for one sellable unit × its quantity (`CompositeProductRules.CalculateComponentPackagingCost`). A composite held in another brings all of its Packaging figure, its own products' included when it has the box ticked, so it nests the way the recipe cost does.
    - `SavedProductReader.ReadComponentCostsAsync` (was `ReadRecipeCostsAsync`) returns `ComponentCosts` (was `RecipeCosts`): recipe and packaging, buffered and unbuffered, from the one read. `ProductCostSummary` takes it as `componentPackaging` (null when not ticked), keeps `OwnPackaging` apart for the Packaging tab's caption, and names the row `PackagingTitle`, "Packaging (own and products')" when ticked, on screen and in print.
    - Only a composite can have it: `CompositeProductRules.EnsureComponentPackagingOnComposite` in the static Add and Update, and `CK_Products_ComponentPackagingOnComposite`. Clone and Create Variant copy it. Migration `CompositeComponentPackaging` is additive (false for every product), applied to the dev database after a `COPY_ONLY` backup to the instance's Backup folder (`TinapayanRMS_before_CompositeComponentPackaging_20260930.bak`).
    - Raw Materials' Used By climbs for it: `PackagingCosts` in `UsedBySql` goes from every product whose packaging cost the material is in to each composite with the box ticked holding it, and on up. It's a CTE of its own because a packaging link takes only the linked product's lines, so `PackagingUsers` mustn't climb through composites.
  - **Composites nest, with a loop check** (over single products only).
    - `CompositeProductRules.EnsureNoCycle`/`Contains` check every other composite's lines, in `SetComponentsAsync`. The message names both: "Party Package can't go into Pasalubong Box: it already contains Pasalubong Box, so …".
    - The picker leaves out the composite and every composite holding it.
    - `SavedProductReader` guards against a loop that data changed outside the app could still hold.
  - **At least two different products** (`CompositeProductRules.MinimumProducts`; over no minimum): six of one product is a Box of 6. A new composite saved with a null recipe (the form's unchanged-recipe shortcut sends none) counts as holding none, so the rule can't be skipped. The public `AddAsync` refuses a composite: only `SaveAsync` writes one with its products.
  - **Quantity**: a whole number, 1 or more, counting the component's sellable units. 1 of "Ensaymada Box of 8" is the whole box and brings 8 pieces' recipe. Every variant is a product of its own, so each is a line of its own.
  - **Storage**: `ProductComponent`.
    - `ProductId` cascades. `ComponentProductId` is Restrict: a product in a composite can't be deleted, and `ProductRepository.DeleteAsync` names the composites before the key would refuse: "Pasalubong Box holds this product. Take it out there first."
    - `Quantity` is an int above 0, and `SortOrder` records the dragged order. It's unique per composite and product, with `CK_ProductComponents_Quantity` and `CK_ProductComponents_NotItself`.
    - Migration `CompositeProducts` is additive (`IsComposite` defaults to false). It was applied to the dev database after a `COPY_ONLY` backup to the instance's own Backup folder (`C:\Program Files\Microsoft SQL Server\MSSQL17.SQLEXPRESS\MSSQL\Backup\TinapayanRMS_before_CompositeProducts_20260929.bak`), since the SQL Express service account can't write to the user's profile, where the LocalDB backups went.
  - **Written as the recipe**: `RecipeDraft.Components` is an init property, the `LinkedUsageDraft.AddIns` precedent, so `IProductRepository.SaveAsync`'s call sites didn't change.
    - `ProductComponentRepository` is static, like `UsageAddInLineRepository`. Its reads are `IProductRepository.GetComponentsAsync` and `GetAllComponentsAsync`, so no ViewModel constructor changed.
    - `SavedProductReader` gained `IProductRepository`, which `ProductsViewModel` passes from its own.
  - **Worked out on read, nothing stored**: `SavedProductReader.ReadDetailAsync` reads a composite's lines and prices each product through itself, recursively (`ProductDetail.Components`, `SavedComponent`). So a composite's line always matches its product's own Costing tab, in Reports and Print as on the screen. `ReadRecipeCostsAsync(ids)` serves the screen.
  - **Clone and Create Variant** give the copy the same products at the same quantities (`SetComponentsAsync` in place of `SetRecipeAsync`). Create Variant still links the packaging.
  - **Raw Materials' Used By** climbs `ProductComponents` for an ingredient, however deep. For packaging it climbs only into composites that include their products' packaging (2026-09-30, above).
  - Tests:
    - ApplicationCore: `CompositeProductRulesTests` and `ProductValidatorTests`.
    - Infrastructure: `CompositeProductPersistenceTests` and one case in `RawMaterialUsedByPersistenceTests`.
    - UI: `SavedProductReaderCompositeTests`, `ProductsViewModelCompositeTests`, one case in `PrintTabViewModelTests` (a saved composite reads as the screen shows it), two in `ProductPrintLayoutTests`, and the tree-menu tests.
- **Line order: every line table stores each line's position** (2026-09-23, at the user's request: "Sub-Assembly Lines, Product Recipe Lines, Packaging Lines, and BOM should allow reordering of rows through dragging"). `SortOrder` (an `int`, from 0) is on `SubAssemblyLine`, `SubAssemblyPackagingLine`, `IngredientBomLine`, `PackagingLine` and `UsageAddInLine`, the five tables whose lines the user types in and now drags into order (see Application Architecture: rows are dragged into order).
  - **Written from the list's order, read in it.** Every `SetLinesAsync` (and `SaveStandalonePackagingAsync`) already deletes a set's lines and inserts them again, so each line's `SortOrder` is simply its index in the list it's handed; no signature changed. Every read that returns lines in order (`SubAssemblyLineRepository.GetForSubAssembliesAsync`, `IngredientBomLineRepository.GetForIngredientAsync`, `RawMaterialReads.GetPackagingLinesAsync`/`GetSetLinesAsync`, `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.GetAddInsForProductAsync`, `ProductCloner`'s add-ins) orders by `SortOrder, Id`.
  - **Why a column, when the lines were already read `ORDER BY l.Id` and inserted in list order:** EF Core inserts several rows into an identity table with one `MERGE … OUTPUT`, and SQL Server doesn't promise to hand out the identities in the `VALUES` order, so Id order was only the insert order by habit. A dragged order is the user's, and has to survive a save for certain.
  - **Migration `LineSortOrder`** adds the five columns at 0 with no data statement: `ORDER BY SortOrder, Id` reads a set that's all 0s in Id order, which is the order its lines were shown in before, and the next save of that set numbers them. Pinned by `LineOrderPersistenceTests` (a reorder round-trips on each table; the stored order beats the Id order; all-0 rows keep their Id order).
- **CostingParameters**: labor, overhead, administrative (office, permits, bookkeeping; added 2026-09-14, at least 0, default 0), margin %, VAT. **Since 2026-09-15 labor, overhead and administrative are percentages** (`LaborPercent`, `OverheadPercent`, `AdministrativePercent`, at the user's request), not pesos per piece. **Since 2026-09-20 the base they're percentages of is the sub-assembly (ingredient) cost per piece alone, packaging left out** (`CostingRules.CalculateCostFromSubAssemblyPercent`, at the user's request: "Labor Cost is Sub-assemblies (ingredients) * Labor Cost %"): 25% labor on ₱20 of sub-assemblies is ₱5, and packaging still goes into the base cost, just not into these three — what they pay for is making the recipe, not wrapping it. They were percentages of the whole material cost (sub-assemblies **plus** packaging, `CalculateCostFromMaterialPercent`) from 2026-09-15 to then, and `LaborCost`, `OverheadCost` and `AdministrativeCost` in pesos before that. **The stored percentages are untouched by the 2026-09-20 change — no migration, no schema change** — but every product with packaging reprices: its labor, overhead and administrative costs, and so its base cost and selling price, all go down. **Always independent per product**, even for derived/linked products.
  - **Costing defaults** (`CostingDefaults`, 2026-09-19, at the user's request: "set the default value of Labor %, Overhead %, Administrative %, Markup %, and VAT % … at the Settings"): the five percentages a **new product's** costing starts with, edited on Settings' **Costing Defaults** tab (`CostingDefaultsView`/`CostingDefaultsViewModel`, checked by `CostingParametersValidator`, and `CostingRules.EnsureValidParameters` in `CostingDefaultsRepository.SetAsync`). A table of one row (`Id` 1, never an identity, `CK_CostingDefaults_SingleRow`; migration `CostingDefaults`), which reads as all zero until first saved. `ProductsViewModel` reads them with every `LoadCommand` (Settings closing reloads the screen on show) and `StartNewProduct` puts them in the Costing fields while what Save compares against stays at none, so the product's first Save writes them as its own `CostingParameters` (all-zero defaults write nothing, as before). A new product still showing the defaults it started with takes up changed ones on the next load; one whose costing was typed into keeps it. **They never reach a saved product**, not even one without a `CostingParameters` row (it still shows zeros), nor a clone or a variant, which copy their source's.

### Derived products (Phase 10; reworked 2026-09-14)

Some products derive from others (e.g. Special Ensaymada Small from Special Ensaymada Big, differing only in weight; or products sharing a dough sub-assembly but differing in toppings). Since 2026-09-14, when sub-assemblies became owned by products, deriving works like this:

- **Since 2026-09-23 every sub-assembly is kept on the Sub-Assemblies screen** (see SubAssembly above): a new section and a copy are saved there as sub-assemblies named after the product, and linked; Make a Copy gives a new section with the linked one's lines and add-ins, piece size and shrinkage, which Save keeps as "Cheese Roll Dough 2" and links instead. Copy an existing one takes the source's role and lines, not its name. The picker names each "Cheese Roll Dough — Sub-Assemblies (Dough)", by name (`GetLinkableAsync`). The bullets below are as they were written, with "another product's" meaning one a product owned.
- **Link a sub-assembly**: a product uses another product's sub-assembly, or a standalone one, as it is, through a `ProductSubAssemblyUsage` of it. The lines are the owner's (or the Sub-Assemblies screen's) and follow its every change (read-only in the linking product until 2026-09-27; since then its lines can be changed there too, and Save writes them to the sub-assembly: see Edit a linked one's lines in the product, under SubAssembly); the **piece size is the linking product's own**, so Small and Big can share a dough at 30 g and 60 g a piece. Links don't chain (only an owner's own or a standalone sub-assembly can be linked), so there's nothing to cycle. A linked section can be turned into the product's own with **Make a Copy**.
- **Copy a sub-assembly**: a new sub-assembly of the product's own, starting from another product's or a standalone one's lines and role (a name, made unique within the product, until 2026-09-17). If the recipe already has that role, the form starts on the first role it doesn't. Nothing connects it to its source afterward.
- The three ways in read **New sub-assembly**, **Link an existing one** and **Copy an existing one** (since 2026-09-15; "…another product's" before). They are the items on Add Sub-Assembly's drop-down since 2026-09-20 — the form's own mode picker before that — and a link's or copy's form is titled with the one picked ("Link an existing sub-assembly"). A new one's form has no title since 2026-09-25: in its place the **Moves to Sub-Assemblies on Save** badge shows while its role is neither product-owned nor packaging (`IsFormNewMovingToSubAssemblies`), and nothing does in a product-owned role, whose new sub-assembly stays the product's own. Its picker, **Existing Sub-Assembly**, lists standalone ones first (`GetLinkableAsync`), as "Butter Glaze — Sub-Assemblies (Finishing)". A linked standalone section's badge reads "From Sub-Assemblies" (`ProductUsageEdit.SourceNameOf`, the one place that names where a linked sub-assembly comes from). A new, unsaved product is offered them too: `RefreshLinkableOptions` matches `OwnerProductId is null` first, since a null owner compared with a new product's null id would otherwise count as its own.
- **Link the packaging** (`PackagingLink`, table `PackagingLinks`, one per product): a product's packaging reads through to another product's instead of owning lines. It resolves every line the linked product has. From 2026-09-18 to 2026-09-20 it resolved the per-piece lines only (`PackId is null`), since a pack's lines pointed at rows in the *linked* product's `ProductPacks` and following them would have shown the linking product lines for packs it didn't sell and couldn't edit; with packs gone there is only one set of lines to resolve. It's saved at once, can be repointed or removed at any time, and **unlinking never loses data**: it first materializes the lines it shows (resolved through the link) as the product's own, then removes the link. Chains are allowed (C's packaging linked to B's linked to A's) and resolved recursively at read time; a cycle is rejected when a link is made (`PackagingLinkRules.EnsureNoCycle`). A product whose packaging another links can't be deleted until that link goes.
  - **Or a packaging set** (2026-09-18): `PackagingLink.LinkedProductId` became nullable beside a new `LinkedSubAssemblyId` (Restrict to `SubAssemblies`), and `CK_PackagingLinks_OneTarget` requires exactly one. `IPackagingLinkRepository.SetSubAssemblyLinkAsync` links a standalone set only; `SetLinkAsync` and it each clear the other target on a relink.
    - **One set per sellable unit**: `PackagingLineRepository.ResolveEffectiveAsync` returns the set's lines as unsaved `PackagingLine`s of the product being resolved (`UnitId` and `QtyPerUnit` the set line's own, `AuthoredField = QtyPerUnit`, the material with its units). So the Products screen, the cost summary, Reports and unlinking's copy take them unchanged, and a chain through products ends at a set (it links nothing, so there's nothing to cycle).
    - **A clone keeps a set its source links directly**, like a standalone recipe (the 2026-09-15 rule); a set reached through another product's link is copied like the rest of that product's packaging.
    - The banner reads "Linked to Gift Box Set (Sub-Assemblies)". The Products `LoadCommand` refetches linked packaging's lines while the form still shows the link as saved, so a set edited on the Sub-Assemblies screen shows on return; own lines and an unsaved link choice aren't touched there, since they may hold unsaved edits.
  - **Made in the Recipe Structure, saved with Save** (2026-09-18, at the user's request: "make the Recipe structure empty for new Products, then we can reuse the Add-Subassembly … If the Role is packaging the packaging datagrid will be use"). The **Links & Clone card is gone**, and the tab is just **Clone**; the user's choices were to save the link with Save (like a linked recipe section, so an unsaved product can link too), to drop the card, and to offer both sets and other products' packaging. **Since 2026-09-25 the Link picker offers only what the Sub-Assemblies screen keeps** (the user's rule: "Link only from the standalone SubAssemblies never from the product"): packaging sets and standalone recipes, never another product's packaging, which Copy still offers (`RefreshLinkableOptions`). A packaging link to a product still comes from Create Variant, and existing ones stay as they are.
    - **Storage didn't change**: the product's packaging is still its per-piece `PackagingLines` or its one `PackagingLink`, never a `SubAssembly` row, so `EnsureRecipeRole` still keeps the Packaging role off a product's own sub-assemblies, and costing, Reports, packs and the cloner are untouched.
    - **`IProductRepository.SaveAsync` takes a `PackagingLinkDraft?`** (`LinkedProductId`/`LinkedSubAssemblyId`, or `PackagingLinkDraft.None`; null leaves the link alone), written in the same transaction just before the lines (`PackagingLinkRepository.SetAsync`, sharing the product and set checks with `SetLinkAsync`/`SetSubAssemblyLinkAsync`). `None` only drops the link; unlike `RemoveLinkAsync` it copies nothing, since the form sends the lines it holds.
    - **Unlinking is Make a Copy on the Packaging tab** (`CopyPackagingCommand`): the lines showing become the product's own, and Save writes both the unlink and the lines (`ChangedLines` writes them whenever the product was linked when loaded, even if they match).
- **Clone** (`IProductCloner.CloneAsync`, one transaction): a one-time copy of a product as saved. **Every sub-assembly it uses, its own and another product's, becomes the clone's own copy** (role, lines and piece size; a source with two in one role, saved before that was refused, can't be cloned until it's put right), plus its packaging as it shows (resolved through a packaging link — a clone never inherits a packaging link, only the data), **its packs with their own packaging lines, as new rows of the clone's own** (2026-09-18), and its `CostingParameters`. Until 2026-09-14 a clone pointed at the same sub-assemblies as its source, so editing one changed both.
  - **A standalone sub-assembly stays linked** (2026-09-15, the user's choice), at the same piece size: it's kept apart to be shared, so the clone follows it like its source does.
  - **Since 2026-09-23 a clone links every sub-assembly its source does** (the user's choice, over copying them as new ones named after the clone), at the same piece sizes and shrinkage, with the source's add-ins as its own: `ProductCloner`'s `CloneRecipe` went, and Clone and Create Variant share `LinkedRecipe`. They differ only in the packaging: Clone copies it, Create Variant links it. Make a Copy splits a section off afterward. **Later the same day** a product's own sub-assemblies (product-owned roles) came back, and a clone or a variant gets its own copy of each of those; see SubAssembly's "Product-owned sub-assemblies".
- **Create Variant** (`IProductCloner.CreateVariantAsync`, one transaction; 2026-09-19, at the user's request: "create a product variant by not only cloning the recipes but also create a linked to them automatically"): a clone whose recipe and packaging are **linked instead of copied**, for Ensaymada Small from Ensaymada Big. It's the Clone tab's second button, beside Clone, sharing its New Product Name.
  - **Every recipe section is linked, at the source's piece sizes**, which are the variant's own to change: the source's own sub-assemblies are linked from the source, and one the source links (another product's or a standalone one) is linked where it's kept, since links don't chain, with the source's add-ins as the variant's own. The variant owns no sub-assembly; Make a Copy unlinks a section as usual. Where a role repeats, one of the source's own without a name goes by the source's name there (`SectionLabel`), so the sections stay apart; a source saved with two unnamed in one role is refused, as for Clone.
  - **Its packaging links the source's** (the user's choice, over copying it), a link to the source product even when the source itself links a set or another product (a chain), and only when the source has packaging (its own lines or a link). Make a Copy on the Packaging tab unlinks it. For a variant that sells differently — a Box of 8 — the link is repointed at a **packaging set** on the Recipe Structure's Packaging tab, which is what makes a variant the way to add a pack.
  - **Its `QuantityRequired` is copied**, and so are the description, category, current price and costing parameters, as for Clone. (Its *packs* were copied until 2026-09-20, when packs became variants of this kind themselves.) `ProductCloner` shares one core between the two (`CreateFromAsync`), which differ only in the recipe (`CloneRecipe`/`VariantRecipe`) and the packaging.
  - The usual guards follow from the links: the source then can't drop a sub-assembly the variant links, or be deleted, until the variant copies or removes it, and the source's sections name the variant among the products linking them.
- **`CostingParameters`** (labor/overhead/administrative/margin/VAT) always stay independent per product: nothing links them.
- **Superseded (2026-09-14): role-tab Live Template links** (`ProductTabLink` with `TabKind = SubAssemblyRole`), which linked all of a product's sub-assemblies in one role to the same role on another product, weights and all. Sub-assembly links replaced them (the user's choice): they cover sharing a recipe, and each product keeps its own weight. What nothing does any more is follow the source's weights, or pick up sub-assemblies added to the source's role later. Migration `ProductOwnedSubAssemblies` turned every role-tab link into links to the sub-assemblies its role showed, at the same weights, and renamed what was left of `ProductTabLinks` (the Packaging-tab links) to `PackagingLinks`.

## Syncfusion UI Framework

The app is styled with **Syncfusion Essential Studio for WPF 34.2.7**, licensed via the `SYNCFUSION_LICENSE_KEY` environment variable, and Syncfusion's **Windows11Light** theme.

- **The theme is Syncfusion's official `Syncfusion.Themes.Windows11Light.WPF` package (Phase 16).** It depends only on `Syncfusion.SfSkinManager.WPF` and `Syncfusion.Licensing`, and it styles every Syncfusion control, so adopting a new control never needs theme work. It replaced a trimmed in-repo theme project (see "Why the theme is no longer trimmed" below) — briefly via a locally packed Theme Studio export, then this package, which is the same theme.
  - **Every Syncfusion package is pinned to the same version, currently `34.2.7`.** The theme package requires `SfSkinManager`/`Licensing` at its own version, NuGet rejects the mismatch as a downgrade (`NU1605`), and Syncfusion doesn't support mixing versions across its assemblies — so upgrading any Syncfusion package means upgrading all of them together: they share the `SyncfusionVersion` property in `Directory.Packages.props`. The `34.2.2` → `34.2.7` upgrade (a patch within the 34.2 release) kept the same license key: no license popup at startup. A move to a new feature release (e.g. 34.3) needs a new key from the Syncfusion account.
  - **One theme gap is patched in the app**: the theme's `SfTextBoxExt` style — unlike its `ComboBoxAdv`, `DoubleTextBox` and `DateTimeEdit` styles — never sets `VerticalContentAlignment`, so the text sits against the top of any box taller than its text (every one, at the 32px `SizeMode="Touch"` height the app used until 2026-09-15). `App`'s static constructor calls `Control.VerticalContentAlignmentProperty.OverrideMetadata(typeof(SfTextBoxExt), new FrameworkPropertyMetadata(VerticalAlignment.Center))`, changing the type's default so every instance (including future ones) is centred without a `Style` on the control; it must run before any `SfTextBoxExt` exists. Verified at runtime: `VerticalContentAlignment=Center`, value source `Default`.
  - **A second `SfTextBoxExt` theme gap: its text sat about 5px further right than the other fields.**
    - The theme's `SfTextBoxExt` template puts a fixed `Margin="10,0,0,0"` on `PART_ContentHost`. `DoubleTextBox`'s template uses 3px of padding instead.
    - Measured in an off-screen replica with the same theme and Touch size, a Name box's text started 13px from its left edge. A `DoubleTextBox` started at 8px and a `ComboBoxAdv` at 7px.
    - The template passes `Padding` (the theme sets -1) to the text presenter inside that margin. `App`'s `AlignTextBoxText` class handler, on `SfTextBoxExt.Loaded`, sets a left padding of -6, which moves the text to 8px.
    - That padding has to be a local value. The theme sets `Padding` through the control's default style (value source `DefaultStyle`), which outranks an `OverrideMetadata` default. A `SetCurrentValue` at `Loaded` was tried first and didn't survive.
    - A `Padding` set in XAML still wins.
  - **A third `SfTextBoxExt` theme gap: its watermark sat at the top of the box, 4px right of typed text.** The template shows `Watermark` in a plain `ContentControl`, whose default template honors neither `VerticalContentAlignment` nor `Padding`, so neither fix above reaches it. `App.xaml`'s `AlignedWatermarkTemplate`, set as the box's `WatermarkTemplate`, centers the hint and moves it 4px left. Measured with the same text typed and as the watermark (a true-size capture with the blinking caret filtered out by keeping each pixel's brightest value across frames), both now start 8px in and cover the same rows. Give it to any `SfTextBoxExt` with a `Watermark`; today that's the Raw Materials search box.
- **Core control set**: the app adopts `SfDataGrid` (lists/grids), `Syncfusion.SfInput.WPF`'s edit controls (`ComboBoxAdv`, `AutoComplete`, `MaskedTextBox`, `DateTimeEdit`, `CurrencyTextBox`, and others), `ButtonAdv`, `TabControlExt`, `DockingManager`/`Ribbon` for the app shell, and `SfChart` for charts (none in use since 2026-09-27). `TinapayanRMS.UI` takes its own `PackageReference`s (pinned to the shared Syncfusion version above) for exactly these.
  - **`DateTimeEdit`'s bindable date property is `DateTime`, not `Value`** (`Syncfusion.Windows.Shared.DateTimeEdit`, in `Syncfusion.Shared.WPF`) — it doesn't follow the `Value`-property convention `DoubleTextBox`/`IntegerTextBox` use. Confirmed by reflecting over the built assembly (`[System.AppDomain]::CurrentDomain.GetAssemblies() | Where Name -eq Syncfusion.Shared.WPF`, then `.GetType(...).GetProperties()`) rather than guessing again after the compiler rejected `Value`.
  - **Every editable `DoubleTextBox` binds `Value` with `UpdateSourceTrigger=PropertyChanged`** (2026-09-13). Without it the typed value reaches the ViewModel only when the box loses focus: the Syncfusion DoubleTextBox skill says to bind this way "for live updates", and Syncfusion's unit-converter showcase does.
    - It showed up as a dead click: Add Line (the BOM and Sub-Assemblies line editors) stays disabled until the quantity is positive, and a disabled button doesn't take focus, so the first click after typing a quantity neither committed it nor added the line.
    - It also kept the Purchase Cost Setup's cost per base unit and Products' Price Summary from updating as a number is typed.
    - Verified in the app on a new made ingredient (quantity starting at 0): with the cursor still in Quantity, Add Line was already enabled, and one click added the line. Typing 1234.5 arrived intact, with no reformatting mid-typing.
    - `Value` is registered on `DoubleTextBox` itself, so unlike the `SfTextBoxExt` default in `App.xaml.cs`, `OverrideMetadata` can't change the default; set it on each new binding.
  - **`ButtonAdv` icons use `IconTemplate` (a `DataTemplate`), not `SmallIcon`/`LargeIcon`** — those two want an `ImageSource` (a bitmap/vector image file), not a font glyph. With no `IconTemplate` set, the Windows11Light theme falls back to a generic placeholder glyph on every button (reported as "always shows the letter A"). Icons are **Segoe Fluent Icons, falling back to Segoe MDL2 Assets** (`App.xaml`'s `IconFontFamily` resource) — Windows' own icon font, so no extra icon package, and it matches the theme natively. Four reusable `DataTemplate` resources in `App.xaml` (`AddIconTemplate` U+E710, `SaveIconTemplate` U+E74E, `DeleteIconTemplate` U+E74D, `RemoveIconTemplate` U+E738) cover every button so far — reuse them (`IconTemplate="{StaticResource AddIconTemplate}"`, etc.) on new screens' buttons rather than repeating the glyph markup, and add a new named template here if a future screen needs a verb these four don't cover.
  - **`ButtonAdv.Label` defaults to the literal string `"Button"` when left unset** (confirmed by reflecting on `ButtonAdv.LabelProperty`'s metadata the same way `DateTimeEdit`'s shape was confirmed) — an icon-only button (`MainWindow`'s hamburger nav toggle, Phase 15, which was one until 2026-09-26) needs `Label=""` set explicitly, or that placeholder text renders squeezed into the button's icon-sized box ("the button has text that doesn't fit"). Even with `Label=""`, the empty `AccessText` keeps its 2px margin on each side, so an icon-only ButtonAdv's glyph sits 2px left of centre. **An icon-only button that must look like a title-bar or tab-strip glyph button is now a plain `Button` in the theme's `WPFGlyphButtonStyle`** (see the Back/Forward passage); a ButtonAdv's opaque, rounded hover can't be flattened to match. Every other `ButtonAdv` in the app already sets a real `Label` ("New", "Save", …) so this never came up before.
  - **`DropDownButtonAdv` is Add Sub-Assembly's button** (Products' Recipe Structure, 2026-09-20): one item per way a sub-assembly comes in, so the way is picked in the click that opens the form. It lives in `Syncfusion.Shared.WPF`, in `Syncfusion.Windows.Tools.Controls` beside `ButtonAdv`, so it needs no new `PackageReference` and takes the same `Label`, `IconTemplate`, `SizeMode` and `ToolTip`; the Windows11Light package styles it. Three things about it, from decompiling it and from Syncfusion's own `editor\Buttons\ButtonView.xaml` demo (no `syncfusion-wpf-*` skill covers it):
    - **Its `Content` *is* the drop-down** — it's a `ContentControl`, and whatever is put inside the element is what the pop-up shows. There is no `Items` or `DropDownContent`.
    - **It doesn't close when something inside the pop-up is clicked.** It closes the drop-down from its own `OnMouseLeftButtonUp`, which only runs for a click on the button itself: the handler it puts on the pop-up (`_dropdown_MouseLeftButtonUp`) forwards to the base `ContentControl` implementation and closes nothing, and a `ButtonAdv` inside has handled the click before it anyway. Each item's `Click` sets `IsDropDownOpen = false` in `RecipeStructureCard.xaml.cs`; the command still runs from the item's own `Command` binding.
    - **The pop-up holds `ButtonAdv`s, not the `ListBox` Syncfusion's demos put there.** A plain `ListBox` keeps the OS default look in this app, since `ApplyThemeAsDefaultStyle` is deliberately off (above), and its blue selection would fight the app's accent; and a `SelectionChanged` command would want `Microsoft.Xaml.Behaviors`, which isn't referenced. Stacked `ButtonAdv`s theme with everything else, reuse the `Add`/`Link`/`Copy` icon templates, and honour `HorizontalContentAlignment="Left"` (checked in the app), so they read as menu items.
      - **Each item is flattened with `Background="Transparent"` and `BorderThickness="0"`** (2026-09-21, at the user's request: the items read as raised buttons, not menu rows). The Windows11Light `ButtonAdv` template `TemplateBinding`s both onto its `ItemBorder`, so setting the two properties is enough — no `Style` and no template override, keeping App.xaml's rule that Syncfusion controls are only ever recolored through `Windows11LightThemeSettings`. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor: the drop-down shows three glyph-and-label rows with no chrome, and the header's own Add/Delete Sub-Assembly buttons are untouched.
      - **The theme's hover survives the transparent background but is nearly invisible on a flat item.** Its `IsMouseOver` trigger sets `ItemBorder.Background` by `TargetName` to an opaque `#FFF9F9F9`, which beats the `TemplateBinding`, so hovering still repaints the row — but against the pop-up's `#FCFCFC` that is a 3-level grey step, measured pixel-by-pixel in a screen capture (252,252,252 → 249,249,249). It also rules out the obvious fix of wrapping each item in a highlighting `Border`: the opaque `#F9F9F9` paints straight over it. A stronger hover would need a template override, which the convention above forbids, so the faint one stands for now.
  - **`SfTreeView` is the Products list** (`Syncfusion.SfTreeView.WPF`, 2026-09-26; see the Products screen's list below), **and since 2026-09-27 the Raw Materials list** (`MaterialListCard` over `RawMaterialTree`, built the same way, so everything below holds for both; see the Raw Materials screen's list). What it took, found in its decompiled source and checked in the app:
    - **A row gets an expander whenever its children are an observable collection, even an empty one.** A product row therefore has no child property at all. The folder type alone gets a `HierarchyPropertyDescriptor` (`ChildPropertyName="Children"`), and the tree's own `ChildPropertyName` is left unset, as Syncfusion's `SelectionDemo` does for its leaf type.
    - **`AutoExpandMode` did nothing here.** It's applied only if the tree's view is built before the control has loaded. Whether a folder is open is kept on the model instead (`ProductFolderNode.IsExpanded`, the descriptor's `IsExpandedPropertyName`), which the tree reads and writes both ways.
    - **The tree reads `IsExpanded` as it changes, but not for a folder added back to its parent** (it expands the parent instead). A folder a search hid would come back closed, so `ProductTree.Refresh` raises `IsExpanded` again for each folder it re-adds (`AnnounceExpanded`).
    - **Nor can it open a folder with nothing in it** (`TreeCollectionView.ExpandNode` sets the node's `IsExpanded` to `ChildNodes.Any()`), and it doesn't look again when rows arrive under a folder it holds closed. Found when the open folders were first remembered between runs (2026-09-26): they came back closed, because `LoadCommand` merges the categories before the products, so every folder is made empty and filled a moment later. `Refresh` therefore also re-announces every open folder that has just got its first rows; this also covers a product moved into an open, empty folder. **The same holds for the top-level folders on the screen showing at startup** (found 2026-09-27 on Raw Materials, the first tab): its tree binds to the type folders before the first load has filled them, so they came up closed however they were set. `Refresh` therefore also re-announces an open top-level folder that was empty when it began (`RawMaterialTree` and `ProductTree` both).
    - **Removing the selected item from its collection drops it from the selection**, and `SelectedItem` goes null. A product moved to another folder leaves one collection and joins another, so the Products screen puts the row back on the product afterwards (`SyncTreeSelection`). `ProductTree.Refresh` also makes every removal before any insertion, so a moved product is never in two folders at once.
    - **Drag and drop:** `ItemDragOver`'s `DropPosition` only drives the indicator and the cursor. `ItemDropping` works its position out again, and `Handled = true` there stops the tree moving any node itself. `DropAsChild` is offered only over a row whose type has children, so over a product the list shows a line under it instead. A drag starts only on a move past the system drag distance while the pointer is still over the row pressed: a script driving it has to nudge within the row before moving away.
    - **Folders stay selectable.** Cancelling `SelectionChanging` for folders would trap the arrow keys, which move by selecting the next row, so a folder row would stop them dead. A folder picked just leaves the product showing.
    - **Several rows can be picked (`SelectionMode="Extended"`), so the screen binds `SelectedItems`, not `SelectedItem`** (2026-09-26). The tree takes the ViewModel's `ObservableCollection<object>` (`SelectedTreeNodes`) as its own and changes it as rows are clicked; the ViewModel changes it too, and the tree follows, setting its `SelectedItem` to the collection's first item itself (`SelectionController.UpdateSelectionProperties`). Binding both would loop: in Extended mode a `SelectedItem` set from outside is *inserted* at the front of `SelectedItems` without clearing it (`ProcessSelectionPropertiesChanged`), and from inside a `CollectionChanged` handler that throws, since the tree is a second handler. `SelectedTreeNode` is therefore the collection's first item, raised by the ViewModel; setting it picks that row alone, as a click does.
      - **A plain click adds the new row before removing the old ones**, so the first item only changes on the removal; a Ctrl+click appends, leaving the first (and the product showing) as it was. When a product row becomes first, picking its product leads to `SyncTreeSelection`, which finds the row first already and leaves the collection alone, as it must mid-change.
      - **A moved row is dropped from the selection too**, and with several picked the next one would become first and its product picked. So `ProductTree.Refresh` runs flagged (`_isSyncingTreeSelection`), and after a move the rows picked are put back (`RestoreTreeSelection`).
      - **A drag carries every row picked** when the row pressed is one of them (`GetDraggingNodes`), and only that row otherwise.
    - **A row's right-click menu is `ItemContextMenu`, swapped per row in `ItemContextMenuOpening`** (2026-09-26; the approach of Syncfusion's `treeview/Views/EditingDemo.xaml`). The tree opens a row's menu only while `ItemContextMenu` is non-null, so a stand-in empty `ContextMenu` is set in XAML and `e.ContextMenu` replaced with the one built for the row (`ProductsViewModel.TreeMenuFor`, turned into `MenuItem`s by `ProductListCard.BuildMenu`). The tree sets the menu's `DataContext` to a `TreeViewItemContextMenuInfo` and opens it itself, then marks `ContextMenuOpening` handled, so the tree's own `ContextMenu` (also a stand-in, swapped in the tree's `ContextMenuOpening`) only opens on the empty space below the rows. From the menu key or Shift+F10 (`ContextMenuEventArgs.CursorLeft` and `CursorTop` both −1) the event is handled and the picked row's menu opened by hand under its row: a menu the context-menu service opens from the keyboard is placed by the service, over the row, whatever `PlacementTarget` and `Placement` it was given. **A right-click doesn't select the row** (`TreeViewItem.OnTreeViewItemMouseRightButtonDown` only focuses the tree), so `ItemContextMenuOpening` picks it first unless it's already among the rows picked. **The menu is themed explicitly** (`SfSkinManager.SetTheme` with the window's theme), like a dialog: it opens in a popup of its own, outside the window's tree.
    - **F2, Del and Enter reach the tree's `KeyDown`**: its own keyboard handling takes F2 only with `AllowEditing`, Delete only with `AllowDeleting`, and Enter only to end an edit, none of which is on (`SelectionController.HandleKeyBoardInteraction`).
    - **The theme draws the tree transparent and never reads its `Background`** (its template's grid, border, scroll viewer and row panel are all fixed to Transparent), so it showed the Latte Base card through it while the grids beside it are white. At the user's request (2026-09-26: "why isn't it white?") `ProductListCard` puts a Border behind it filled with `ListBackgroundBrush`, rounded as the tree's own border (4px). The theme's own brush, `ContentBackground`, can't be used from XAML: `{skinManager:ThemeResource ThemeKey={skinManager:ThemeKey Key=ContentBackground}}`, which Syncfusion's demos use, resolves only with `SfSkinManager.ApplyThemeAsDefaultStyle` on (`Theme.Helper` is created only then), and it stays off here. Checked by rendering `ProductListCard` off-screen with the app's resources and the theme: `#FFFFFF` inside the tree, Base around it.
- **`PrintPreview` is the Products screen's print pane** (`Syncfusion.Windows.Shared.Printing`, in `Syncfusion.Shared.WPF`, already referenced; 2026-09-27, see Print under the Products screen). It's the print backstage SfDiagram uses. On its left are Print (straight to the printer picked there), Copies, the printer and its properties, the page range, collation, orientation, the printer's own paper sizes, and margins. On its right are the pages, with page navigation and zoom. No `syncfusion-wpf-*` skill covers it, and no Sample Browser demo drives it with content of its own (SfDiagram's `PrintingService` hosts it), so what follows comes from decompiling 34.2.9 and SfDiagram's `DiagramPrintManager`:
  - **It's driven by a `PrintManager` subclass** (`Printing/ProductPrintManager`).
    - `ComputePageCount` (protected virtual) lays the printout out and sets `PageCount`.
    - `CreatePage(int, PrintPageControl)` sets the page control's `Content`. It's `protected internal`, so it's overridden as `protected override` from outside the assembly.
    - The page control's template lays the page out with its margins (a Viewbox, then the page's Border with `Margin=PageMargin`, then header, content and footer rows), so the content only fills the printable area.
    - `InitializePrint` swaps `PageWidth` and `PageHeight` for the orientation before it calls `ComputePageCount`. The pane calls it again (`PrintPreviewPanel.InValidate`) on every change of paper, margins or orientation. `OnPrintPropertyChanged()` (protected internal) does the same for a change of the subclass's own.
  - **The pane shows one page at a time.** It reuses one page control as it scrolls (`CreatePage(i, sameControl)`), and printing asks again with new controls, so every call builds new visuals: an element can only have one parent. `InValidate` returns early when `PageCount` is 0 and leaves the old pages showing, so a layout always has a page.
  - **`ProductPrintManager` overrides `PrintDocument`** (`protected virtual`), so the job goes out on the printer's own ticket.
    - The base class prints on the ticket the pane builds (`GetCustomizedPrintTicket`), after swapping its named paper for a bare size. Microsoft Print to PDF can't take that ticket: its filter pipeline stops (event 824 in the PrintService log, "The print filter pipeline process was terminated", 0x80040004) and the PDF comes out empty, 0 bytes. The same pages printed on the printer's own default ticket, both through `PrintQueue.AddJob` and through a `PrintDialog`, which is how the ticket was found to be the cause.
    - `JobTicket` takes the printer's own ticket (`UserPrintTicket ?? DefaultPrintTicket`), puts the pane's orientation, copies and collation on it with the printer's own paper nearest the page (to 2 px either way), and has the printer validate the result (`MergeAndValidatePrintTicket`).
    - The base class also swallows every exception, so a failed print showed nothing. The override logs the failure and raises `PrintFailed`, which the Print tab (`ProductPrintView`) shows in a message box. A cancel where the printer asks something, such as Microsoft Print to PDF's file name, stays quiet.
    - Only Microsoft Print to PDF has printed it so far: the user's terms for the in-app check ruled out a real printer.
  - **It's themed like everything else, but the theme has to come first.** The Windows11Light package has its style (`printpreview/printpreview.baml`), applied with `SfSkinManager.SetTheme` on its window, as for any dialog. `Syncfusion.Shared.WPF`'s own generic template (`Print/themes/generic.baml`) covers the pages printed outside the window.
    - **The window is themed before the pane goes in.** A `PrintPreview` already in the window when `SetTheme` reaches it throws a `NullReferenceException` from `OnApplyTemplate` on its first layout, and the first Print in the app crashed that way. SfDiagram's own print window themes itself first too. The pane was in a window of its own then (`ProductPrintWindow`, whose constructor called `SetTheme` before creating it); it's on the main window's Print tab now, which the app themes at startup, before the pane is ever made.
    - `PrintPreviewThemeOrderTests` opens a pane both ways round and expects the wrong order to throw, so it fails once Syncfusion fixes the pane. SfSkinManager keeps the dictionaries it loads for a theme with the thread that loaded them, and a window themed on a second thread fails with "The calling thread cannot access this object because a different thread owns it" (a `XamlParseException`). So **every test that themes a window runs on one shared STA thread, `StaThread.RunThemed`**, which also registers the app's theme settings (`App.ThemeSettings`, the accent and the 14 px text), so a test measures what the app shows.
  - **It's fitted to the app's 14 px text** (`Printing/PrintPaneFit`, 2026-09-27, at the user's request: "we can increase the width of the left pane. we can also increase some of the controls to fit the font size"). The theme sizes the pane for its own 12 px, so at 14 px "Copies:" lost its colon, the Print caption lost its foot, the two-line dropdowns ran their second line into the edge ("Left: 2.5 cm Right: 2.5 c…") and the page box under the pages clipped its number.
    - The theme's resources can't be reached from the app's XAML (see "the Products tree's white" above), so a copy of its templates would lose their styles. Instead, once the pane is loaded (its templates applied, before its first frame), `PrintPaneFit` changes the parts it finds by name: the settings column 250 → 340 px (`OptionsWidth`), each dropdown 220 → 300 px and as tall as its item, the Copies, From and To boxes 60×24 → 76×30, the Copies label and the Print caption sized to their text, and the page box 24×16 → 40×26 with "/ n" beside it as wide as it needs. A part it can't find is logged and left alone, for a Syncfusion template that has changed. (The dialog it was in then grew by the same 90 px; it's a main tab now.)
    - The dropdowns get item templates of their own (`Printing/PrintPaneTemplates.xaml`, loaded as a class so tests can load it too): the theme's are 43 px rows for 12 px text. Each item is an icon and a name, with a second line where the theme has one (what the page range means, the collation order, the paper's size, the margins), at least 44 px tall and growing with its text. The default printer's badge is a white tick on `AccentForegroundBrush`.
    - **A ComboBox keeps the item template its choice was first shown with**: it draws its closed box again only on a new selection, a new template, or a change to `IsEditable`. The printer, page range and collation are chosen while the template loads, so they kept the theme's item. Choosing again would make Syncfusion reload the printer's papers, so `PrintPaneFit` switches `IsEditable` on and clears it again.
    - Covered by `PrintPaneFitTests`, on `RunThemed`: the column and dropdown widths, every closed box on the new item, and no visible text in the settings cut off, measured against its layout clip (a block held to a smaller `Height` keeps its text's `ActualHeight`; only `LayoutInformation.GetLayoutClip` shows the cut). Without the fit, both tests fail.
    - **Neither template gives the page its paper.** The theme's leaves the page transparent over the pane's grey, and the generic one draws a grey (#808080) line round every printed page. `CreatePage` sets the page control's `Background` to `PrintPaperBrush` (white) and its `BorderThickness` to 0.
  - **The page starts as `PrintSettingsBase`'s A4 with 2.5 cm margins.** Once the pane loads, it takes the printer's paper list and default paper (`SelectDefaultPageMediaSize`).
  - With `GetScaleOptions` left null, the Scaling picker hides.
  - **A re-layout used to draw the page at 100% while the zoom still read, say, 59%** (found on the Print tab, 2026-09-27; the earlier note here that the pane "keeps its scroll position" after a re-layout was most likely this). `PrintPreviewPanel.InValidate` takes its one page control out of the panel and puts it back (`Children.Clear()`, then `Add`) within one layout pass. Out of the tree it loses the theme's style, so back in, it gets its template again, with a new `PART_Viewbox` of no size, and the pane sizes that viewbox only in `OnZoomFactorChanged`. The page is never unloaded, so `Loaded` doesn't fire; its `Template` changing does. `PrintPaneFit.KeepZoom` watches that (a `DependencyPropertyDescriptor`) and, when the viewbox has no size, moves `ZoomFactor` by one and back so the pane sizes it itself. Covered by `PrintPaneFitTests` (a part ticked out and a new printout keep the page's width), which fails without it.
  - `GetPrintDocument()` returns the pages as a `FixedDocument`. That's the fallback if the pane ever misbehaves: a WPF `DocumentViewer` and `PrintDialog` over the same layout.
- **Why the theme is no longer trimmed (Phase 16).** Through Phase 15 the theme was an in-repo project, `TinapayanRMS.Theme`, trimmed from Theme Studio's ~50-control output to the controls the app used, with the rest listed in `_ExcludedThemeFolders`. Trimming silently broke `SfDataGrid` from Phase 4 onward: the theme's `SfDataGrid/SfDataGrid.xaml` merges `ChromelessWindow/ChromelessWindow.xaml` (for the grid's own popup windows), which the trim had excluded. That dictionary therefore failed to load (`IOException: Cannot locate resource 'chromelesswindow/chromelesswindow.xaml'`), **and `SfSkinManager` swallows dictionary-load failures** — so every grid rendered with Syncfusion's unthemed generic style (14pt headers larger than the 12pt data, gridlines on every cell, no Touch-mode row heights) while every other control themed normally. It surfaced as "the grid header font is too big"; three fixes edited the theme's header style and did nothing, because that style was never applied.
  - **What found it**: a throwaway runtime diagnostic reading `DependencyPropertyHelper.GetValueSource` on a header cell's `FontSize` — it reported `DefaultStyle` (Syncfusion's generic style) rather than a theme `Style` — followed by loading `SfDataGrid.xaml` directly into a `ResourceDictionary` inside a try/catch, which surfaced the swallowed exception. **When a theme edit appears to do nothing, check at runtime whether the theme style is applied at all before editing it further.** Reading theme XAML, eyeballing screenshots, and UI Automation bounding boxes (which track row height, not font size) all misled here.
  - A scan of the trimmed theme for merged dictionaries pointing at missing folders found `SfDataGrid` was the only affected control the app used; `Ribbon` and `DockingManager` were also broken but unused. The complete theme has none of these gaps, which is why the app now uses Syncfusion's full official theme package instead.
  - Unrelated fact from the trimming era, still true: the export's "SfTabControl" folder is **not** a general tab control — it's `SfChromelessWindow`'s own tab strip (`Syncfusion.SfChromelessWindow.WPF`). The general-purpose tab control is `TabControlExt` (`Syncfusion.Tools.WPF`).
- **Syncfusion's own agent skills are installed in this repo** at `.claude/skills/syncfusion-wpf-*`, taken from [github.com/syncfusion/wpf-ui-components-skills](https://github.com/syncfusion/wpf-ui-components-skills). That repo carries 96 control skills; the ones matching this app's control set were copied in (navigation-drawer, theming, tabcontrol, datagrid, button, combobox, charts, the `SfInput` editors, plus getting-started and licensing) rather than all of them, keeping it to what's actually used. Syncfusion ships them for the cross-agent `skills` CLI (`npx skills add syncfusion/wpf-ui-components-skills`), which installs to `.agents/skills` — **a path Claude Code does not scan** — so they were copied to `.claude/skills/` instead. Copy in any of the remaining skills the same way if a new control is adopted. These are the first place to look for "how is this control meant to be used", ahead of the Sample Browser source noted in Open Items.
  - **`syncfusion-wpf-ui-builder`** (from [github.com/syncfusion/wpf-ui-builder](https://github.com/syncfusion/wpf-ui-builder), which packages it under `.apm/skills/`) is installed alongside them but is a different kind of thing: an 8-stage generator for building a screen from scratch, not per-control reference. It was installed **deliberately modified** — its `description` narrowed so it only triggers on an explicit "scaffold a new screen" request rather than on any WPF work, and a "TinapayanRMS override" note added at the top of its `SKILL.md`. The override matters because the skill prescribes `INotifyPropertyChanged` + `RelayCommand` MVVM, which contradicts this project's ReactiveUI convention, and its own screen/data-access conventions likewise lose to the ones in this document. **If that skill is ever refreshed from upstream, re-apply both edits** — an unmodified copy will fight the conventions here.
  - **`ui-ux-pro-max`** (third-party, MIT, from [github.com/nextlevelbuilder/ui-ux-pro-max-skill](https://github.com/nextlevelbuilder/ui-ux-pro-max-skill)) is general design intelligence — palettes, typography, 119 UX guidelines, and per-stack guidance including a `wpf` stack — backed by a local Python search script over bundled CSV data. Upstream ships it as a Claude Code **plugin**; it was copied in as a project skill instead to match the Syncfusion ones, which required **three adaptations**, all recorded in a "TinapayanRMS override" note at the top of its `SKILL.md`:
    1. **Script paths rewritten.** Upstream calls `search.py` via `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`, which only exists in plugin mode (upstream's own path test says as much) — as a project skill it's empty, so every path resolved to the filesystem root and every search failed. They now use `$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)`, verified working from a subdirectory.
    2. **Stack pinned to `wpf`.** Its stack detection only reads `package.json`/`pubspec.yaml`/`*.xcodeproj`/`composer.json`, so in a .NET repo it detects nothing and would ask every time.
    3. **MVVM guidance overridden.** 9 of its 56 WPF guidelines assume CommunityToolkit.Mvvm and none mention ReactiveUI; its validation guideline even labels this project's deliberate `ViewModelBase.ErrorMessage` + FluentValidation pattern as the anti-pattern, and it recommends unit-testing ViewModels. The override tells it to use only the 47 toolkit-agnostic guidelines, and not to generate a design system that competes with the Syncfusion Windows11Light theme.

    Its upstream `scripts/tests/` CI suite was dropped (the skill never uses it at runtime, and it would now fail against the rewritten paths). Its sibling `ui-styling` skill was **not** installed: it's shadcn/ui + Radix + Tailwind, pure web. **Re-apply all three adaptations if `ui-ux-pro-max` is ever refreshed from upstream.**
- **Size mode: `Default`** (`skinManager:SfSkinManager.SizeMode="Default"` on `MainWindow` and `SettingsWindow`; since 2026-09-15, at the user's request). It was `Touch` before, where the theme gives inputs and buttons a 32px `MinHeight` (`TouchMode.MinHeight` in the theme's `Common.xaml`) and `SfDataGrid` 32px rows under a 36px header. Measured in the running app after the switch, at 100% scaling: `ComboBoxAdv` 25px, `ButtonAdv` 24px, `DoubleTextBox` 22px, grid rows 24px, and `SfTextBoxExt` only 18–20px at its natural height, since no theme minimum applies to it in `Default`. An `SfTextBoxExt` was taller only where its row stretched it (Raw Materials' search box 21px and Name 24px, the product header's name 37px), so an empty one on its own row (Settings' Name and Abbreviation) looked like a thin bar. The alignment fixes above still hold: `AlignTextBoxText`'s padding is a local value, which outranks the theme's size-mode triggers. Checked in the app: Raw Materials, a product's Recipe and Costing tabs, the sub-assembly form (its ingredient pop-up still opens clear of the cell) and Settings.
