  - **Every text box and button is 24px tall** (the same day, at the user's request). `App`'s static constructor overrides `FrameworkElement.HeightProperty`'s default to 24 for `SfTextBoxExt`, `DoubleTextBox`, `IntegerTextBox`, `CurrencyTextBox` and `ButtonAdv` (the middle two since 2026-09-24, when Products' Quantity Required and Current Price, their labels moved beside them, stood shorter than Product Name; `ControlHeight`). The theme sets no height on them in `Default`, and a type default is the lowest precedence, so a `Height` set in XAML still wins; a box in a taller row now sits centred at 24px instead of stretching. The error toast's close button (a plain `Button` with its own template) went from 28px to 24px square. `ComboBoxAdv` pickers were left at their own 25px. A `DoubleTextBox` editing a grid number cell is sized by the grid to the 24px row either way (a handler clearing the height there changed nothing, and was taken out). Verified in the app on the secondary monitor (by then 1920×1080 at 125%, so UI Automation read 30 physical px = 24px): Raw Materials, a product's header, section buttons and sub-assembly form, a grams cell being edited, Settings and the error toast.
  - **Every number box's text is right-aligned** (2026-09-28, at the user's request: "make textbox containing numeric values like percentage and double be right aligned"). `App` registers a `Loaded` class handler on `EditorBase`, the `TextBox` Syncfusion's `DoubleTextBox`, `IntegerTextBox`, `CurrencyTextBox` and `PercentTextBox` are built on (`AlignNumberRight`), which sets `TextAlignment` `Right` as a local value unless the box already has one (from its XAML, or a grid's editor), as `AlignTextBoxText` does for `SfTextBoxExt`'s padding, so the theme's style can't outrank it and no `Style` replaces the theme's. The three batch-parameter boxes in the sub-assembly form, which set it themselves, no longer need to.
- **Applying the theme**: `App.xaml.cs`'s static constructor registers the license (`SyncfusionLicenseProvider.RegisterLicense`) before anything else touches a Syncfusion type; `OnStartup` then calls `SfSkinManager.SetTheme(mainWindow, new Theme("Windows11Light"))` before showing the window. Verified by actually launching the built app and screenshotting the themed `MainWindow` (custom title bar chrome, styled `ButtonAdv`) — not just a successful build.
- **Per-monitor DPI awareness (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/app.manifest`, wired via `<ApplicationManifest>`)**. On its own WPF is only *system*-DPI-aware: it renders at the scale the primary monitor had at sign-in, and on a monitor with a different scale Windows bitmap-stretches the whole window. That was reported as "the app is a little blurry on the secondary monitor": the dev machine's primary is 1920×1080 at 125% and its secondary 1366×768 at 100%, so the window rendered at 120 DPI and was shrunk by 0.8. The manifest declares `dpiAwareness` `PerMonitorV2, PerMonitor`, plus the legacy `dpiAware` `true` that only pre-1607 Windows reads. WPF then re-renders natively at each monitor's DPI and handles `WM_DPICHANGED` itself. That needs no code — none of the native-helper/`DisableDpiAwareness` scale-transform approach from Microsoft's Windows 8.1-era article. Layout is unchanged, since everything is in DIPs (the window is 1400×800 physical px on the 100% monitor either way); only sharpness changes. Things that would need extra per-DPI work if ever added: bitmap image assets, `HwndHost`/`WindowsFormsHost`, `RenderTargetBitmap`; the app has none today.
  - **Verified** by querying the running window before and after the manifest: `GetWindowDpiAwarenessContext` went from System aware to Per-monitor V2, and `GetDpiForWindow` on the secondary from 120 to 96. A zoomed before/after crop of the same grid text showed the blur gone. **`PrintWindow` can't show this kind of blur**: it captures the window's own surface at its render DPI, before Windows stretches it. A DPI-scaling check has to capture the screen itself (`CopyFromScreen`, with the window kept topmost via `SWP_NOACTIVATE` so it doesn't take focus).
  - **`MainWindow` has to hand `WM_DPICHANGED` back to WPF** (2026-09-26, found by the user after the switch to `ChromelessWindow`, as "some elements appear much larger than usual" after moving the app between monitors). With `UseNativeChrome`, `ChromelessWindow.OnSourceInitialized` adds a hook (a private `WndProc`) that sets `handled = true` on message 736, `WM_DPICHANGED`, and does nothing else. `HwndSource` calls public hooks before its own `HwndTarget` handling, so WPF never rescaled. On this machine (primary 125%, secondary 1366×768 at 100%), a window opened on the primary kept its 1750×1000 px size on the secondary and drew everything at 125%: the Raw Materials tab measured 226×27 px on both monitors. `Controls/ChromelessWindowDpi.LetWpfHandleDpiChanges`, called from `AppWindow.OnSourceInitialized` after base (in `MainWindow` until 2026-09-29), removes that hook with `HwndSource.RemoveHook`. It finds the method by reflection, and a delegate made from it equals the one Syncfusion added. Verified in the app: moving to the secondary now resizes the window and scales the tab to 182×21 px (×0.8), and back to the primary restores 226×27. `ChromelessWindowDpiTests` sends a real `WM_DPICHANGED` to an off-screen `ChromelessWindow` and checks `DpiChanged`. One test fails if a Syncfusion update renames the hook, and the other fails once Syncfusion stops swallowing the message, when the workaround can go.
- **Accent customization (Phase 16, UI/UX refactor)**: `App.xaml.cs` calls `SfSkinManager.RegisterThemeSettings("Windows11Light", ThemeSettings(primaryBackground, primaryForeground))` before the host is built (`App.ThemeSettings` builds the `Windows11LightThemeSettings`, text sizes included, and is public since 2026-09-27 so the themed tests register the same), re-coloring the theme's accent to Catppuccin Latte's Sky (`#04A5E5`, with `PrimaryForeground` Sky's Ink, `#013245`) app-wide — `PrimaryBackground`'s setter alone derives the Dark1-3/Light1-3/Opacity1-3 shades, so one property recolors `SfDataGrid` selection, the `TabControlExt`/`SfNavigationDrawer` selection indicators, `SfTextBoxExt` focus underlines, and `ButtonAdv` hover/press. **`SfSkinManager.ApplyThemeAsDefaultStyle` is deliberately never set to `true`** — turning it on makes `SfSkinManager` re-style every open `Window` as a `ChromelessWindow` (`SfSkinManager.ApplyWindowStyle`), replacing the native title bar with Syncfusion's custom chrome. (With the old trimmed theme it also crashed the app on startup, since `ChromelessWindow.xaml` was excluded; the full theme has it, so it would no longer crash — but the chrome change was never wanted for every window.) **`MainWindow` is a `ChromelessWindow` by choice** (2026-09-25, at the user's request: no Windows caption bar, and minimize, maximize and close in the app's own title strip), declared as one rather than restyled by the skin manager, so a window is chromeless only when it's declared so. **So is the Settings dialog** (2026-09-29, at the user's request). Both derive from `Controls/AppWindow`, a `ChromelessWindow` that sets `UseNativeChrome`, `CornerRadius` 0 and `ResizeBorderThickness` 0 in its constructor and carries every fix below: the resize band, the corner hook (`DropWindowRegion`), the DWM corner preference, `ChromelessWindowMask`, `ChromelessWindowDpi` and `RestoreRendering`. They lived in `MainWindow` until then. A new chromeless window derives from `AppWindow` rather than `ChromelessWindow`, or it brings back the bugs those fix. `ChromelessWindow` overrides `DefaultStyleKey` only for its own type, so a subclass still gets the theme's style. Settings has the main window's title bar (card colour, height 30, no icon), with its heading, `AppTitleStyle`, as a left header item: `ShowTitle`'s text sits flush against the window's left edge. It has no Minimize button (`ShowMinimizeButton` off), since it's modal and has no taskbar button. `TextPromptWindow` keeps its own borderless card (see the Products notes). It follows the Sample Browser's own main window: `ShowTitle` and `ShowIcon` off (the window `Title` still names it in the taskbar), `TitleBarHeight` 30 (40, the Sample Browser's, until 2026-09-30, when the user asked for a quarter less; the theme's 40×32 caption buttons and the 40×32 menu toggle are clipped to the 30px row, so their hover wash fills the bar exactly), `TitleBarBackground` the card colour, and the header strip's menu toggle and heading moved into `LeftHeaderItemsSource` (an `ArrayList` in XAML; the items keep the window's names, `DataContext` and `Click` handlers). **Back and Forward sit at the main tab strip's right end** (the same day, at the user's request; they were in the title bar for the first hours), as `MainTabControl`'s `TabPanelItem`: the Windows11Light template's one slot for content of the strip's own, a `ContentPresenter` (`PART_CustomItems`) docked right after the tab-list (˅) and close (✕) buttons, its `MaxHeight` the tab row's. **They're built like the strip's own ✕ and ˅** (2026-09-26, at the user's request: the ButtonAdvs were too big beside the ✕, and their hover didn't match it). Each is a plain WPF `Button`, 16×16 with an 8px right margin and `Focusable="False"`, as `PART_CloseButton` is. Its style is the theme's `WPFGlyphButtonStyle`, which the template's `TabPanelCaptionButtonsStyle` is based on and whose rounded `ContentBackgroundHovered` wash, pressed state and transparent disabled state are the same. The style is fetched as `{DynamicResource {skinManager:ThemeKey Key=WPFGlyphButtonStyle, Theme={x:Type sfTheme:Windows11LightSkinHelper}}}`. The Sample Browser's `{sfskin:ThemeResource ThemeKey=…}` form left the default Aero button here: its converter calls `TryFindResource` once, before `OnStartup`'s `SetTheme` has merged the theme's MSControls dictionaries into the window, and nothing makes it look again. A `DynamicResource` does look again. A ButtonAdv can't match the ✕: the theme gives it a 24px minimum height, `App`'s static constructor sets 24px too, and its hover and disabled fills are opaque, which `Background="Transparent"` doesn't reach. The arrows are 10px Segoe Fluent glyphs (about 8px drawn, the size of the ✕ and ˅ paths) and take the Button's `Foreground`, which the style's triggers change. Checked on the secondary monitor with real hovers: the same wash on the ✕ and on Back. **The title bar's hamburger (`ToggleNavigationButton`) is built the same way to hover like the minimise and maximise buttons** (the same day, at the user's request). It's the same style with `Template="{DynamicResource WPFGlyphWindowButtonTemplate}"`, the template `ChromelessWindow` gives those buttons. That template has the same hover with square corners. It isn't published under a ThemeKey, so it's found by its plain key in the theme dictionary merged into the window. The button is 40×32, their size. It needs `WindowChrome.IsHitTestVisibleInChrome="True"`, as the theme's title buttons set: without it the caption area swallows the mouse and the button never hovers. Measured on screen, the wash (231,232,236) and the box (50×40 physical at 125%) are the minimise button's, and the menu still opens and closes on a real click. It stays focusable, unlike the title buttons, because `CloseDrawer` returns the keyboard to it. The strip's own "navigation buttons" (`TabScrollButtonVisibility`/`TabScrollStyle`) were looked at first and can't do this: decompiled, `TabLayoutPanel.ScrollToNextTab` and the rest only move the strip's `HorizontalOffset`, never the selection, let alone the screen history. Placing them at the strip's left end would have needed a Style on `TabPanelAdv` or an overlay, so the right end it is. While the menu is open they're under the drawer's scrim (a first click puts the menu away); Alt+Left/Alt+Right work regardless. Their disabled look is the theme's opaque `#F9F9F9` box, which shows more on the Crust strip than it did on the Base title bar. No `TitleBarTemplate`, so the title bar and its Minimize, Maximize/Restore and Close buttons are the theme's. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor with real clicks: each caption button, dragging the bar (the window moved by the drag), double-clicking it (maximizes to the work area exactly), the menu toggle, and Close ending the process. **It runs with `UseNativeChrome="True"`, `CornerRadius="0"` and `ResizeBorderThickness="0"`** (the same day, after the user reported a black outline and bottom corners that wouldn't resize). The plain chromeless mode had both bugs, measured with a probe that sent `WM_NCHITTEST` and dragged real mouse input from points near each edge and corner: every point hit-tested `HTCLIENT`, and `ChromelessWindow`'s own mouse-driven sizing (`GetSizingDirection`) only caught the outermost pixel of the left and right edges, so the bottom edge and all four corners were dead. It also cut the theme's 8px rounded corners (`CornerRadius` 8 in the Windows11Light style) out of an opaque window, so the corners and a 1px ring drew black. `UseNativeChrome` switches to the theme's `SyncfusionChromelessWindowWindows11Template` and a WPF `System.Windows.Shell.WindowChrome` (`GlassFrameThickness` 1 while normal), so Windows does the hit-testing (`HTBOTTOMLEFT`, `HTBOTTOMRIGHT` and the rest) and Windows 11 draws the rounded corners, shadow and 1px border itself. The chrome's resize band is bound to `ResizeBorderThickness`, which is also the width of the border the template paints (`OuterBorder`), so the window sets that to 0 and `OnSourceInitialized` gives the `WindowChrome` a local `ResizeBorderThickness` of `SystemParameters.WindowResizeBorderThickness` instead. `CornerRadius` 0 stops WPF painting its own curve inside Windows' (which left a black wedge). Known leftover: maximizing with the Maximize button makes the window 3px taller than the work area, behind the taskbar. `ChromelessWindow`'s `WM_GETMINMAXINFO` handler adds that on purpose (+3, or +2 with no taskbar) to cover the border its own template draws. Only 3 of the page's 4px bottom margin is lost, so it was left alone. Double-clicking the bar or Win+Up gives the exact work area. **Keeping the rounded corners** (2026-09-26, the user saw them go square after dragging the window onto the secondary monitor): `ChromelessWindow` keeps giving the window a square window region (`UpdateWindowRegion` → `SetWindowRgn`, since with native chrome its glass mode is off) from its own hook on `WM_SIZE`/`WM_SIZING`/`WM_EXITSIZEMOVE` and from its `StateChanged` handler, and Windows 11 doesn't round a window that has a region. My earlier checks had moved the window with `SetWindowPos`, which sends no `WM_EXITSIZEMOVE`, so they missed it; a real drag reproduced it (`GetWindowRgn` = SIMPLEREGION, square corners). `MainWindow` now adds its own hook before `base.OnSourceInitialized` (WPF calls the newest hook first, so it runs after Syncfusion's), which takes the region off (`SetWindowRgn(hwnd, IntPtr.Zero, true)`) on those three messages and again from a queued `Dispatcher.BeginInvoke`, because WPF raises `StateChanged` from `WM_SIZE` after every hook. With the region gone Windows still didn't round the window by itself, so `OnSourceInitialized` also asks for it with `DwmSetWindowAttribute(DWMWA_WINDOW_CORNER_PREFERENCE = 33, DWMWCP_ROUND = 2)`; Windows 10 lacks the attribute and keeps square corners. Verified on the secondary monitor with real drags: after launch, a title-bar move, a corner resize, and Maximize then Restore through the buttons, the window has no region and screen captures show all four corners rounded. While maximized the region is back (the work area, 1366×736, trimming the 3px overhang), which is harmless since Windows doesn't round a maximized window. The trade-off: Syncfusion controls theme normally via `SetTheme`, while plain framework controls (`CheckBox`, `Expander`, `ProgressBar`) keep the OS default look.
  - **No black frame after a restore from the taskbar** (2026-09-29, the user's recording: minimized and restored while maximized on Reports, the window showed, then went black for a frame, then came back). Measured with a probe that restores the window with `WM_SYSCOMMAND`/`SC_RESTORE` (what the taskbar sends) and samples a screen pixel every ~8 ms: black on almost every restore, 20–60 ms long, some 60–140 ms after the restore, always after the window had already shown its content. Three things, each fixed separately:
    - **`DropWindowRegion` cleared the region with a redraw.** The queued second clear landed just after a restore's first frame and blanked it. It clears without one now (`SetWindowRgn(hwnd, IntPtr.Zero, false)`), which still takes the region off, so the corners stay round (checked after restores, a resize, Maximize then Restore, and a real title-bar drag). At normal size that alone took it from most restores to none.
    - **ChromelessWindow's content mask** (`Controls/ChromelessWindowMask`, called from `AppWindow.OnApplyTemplate`). The Windows11 native-chrome template masks its whole root grid (`RootGrid`) with an OpacityMask, a VisualBrush of an inner border, and ChromelessWindow applies it again on every region update (`SetBorderMask`). It only rounds the content's bottom corners to `CornerRadius`, which is 0 here. A bare ChromelessWindow flashed with it (6 restores in 20) and never without it, so the mask is cleared and the private `rootGrid` field ChromelessWindow keeps for it is nulled, found by name as `ChromelessWindowDpi` finds its hook; `ChromelessWindowMaskTests` fails if a Syncfusion update renames it or drops the mask.
    - **Maximized, WPF's hardware surface** (`Controls/RestoreRendering`, from `AppWindow.OnSourceInitialized`). Still black on most maximized restores after both fixes above. In software rendering (`HwndSource.CompositionTarget.RenderMode = SoftwareOnly`) never, so the window draws in software from being minimized until 500 ms after it's back, then with the GPU again; switching back showed no black either, and the page's colour moves by at most one level. The cause itself wasn't found. It isn't the corner hook, ChromelessWindow's hook, its StateChanged handler, its template, the ClearType hint it sets, the DPI hook, the DWM restore transition, WPF WindowChrome's `WS_VISIBLE` toggle in `_UpdateSystemMenu`, or the clear colour (each switched off in turn). A plain WPF window with the same content, DPI awareness and WindowChrome never did it; a ChromelessWindow with heavy content and PerMonitorV2 did, on some restores.
    - A finding on the way: the theme's style replaces ChromelessWindow's own WindowChrome (glass 1 normal, 0 maximized, no aero caption buttons) with WPF's defaults (`<WindowChrome />` in the `UseNativeChrome` trigger), so at runtime MainWindow has glass 3.2, 26.4, 3.2, 3.2, `UseAeroCaptionButtons` true, a caption height of 23.2 and a transparent clear colour, and ChromelessWindow's `UpdateWindowChrome` changes nothing. Giving it the intended chrome didn't help the flash, so it was left.
    - Verified on the secondary monitor: 0 black frames in 20 maximized restores and 20 normal ones, against 13–19 in 20 before.
    - UI automation note: `Process.MainWindowHandle` can pick one of the app's hidden windows (a `MessageWindowClass` one, or WPF's `SystemResourceNotifyWindow`); a harness that restores that window shows it as a black box. Find MainWindow by its class (`HwndWrapper[…]`) and title.
- **App-level design tokens (Phase 16)**, in `App.xaml`, alongside the icon templates: `PageBackgroundBrush`, `CardBackgroundBrush`, `CardBorderBrush`, `HeadingForegroundBrush`, `MutedForegroundBrush`, `ReadOnlyForegroundBrush`, `AccentBrush`/`AccentForegroundBrush`/`AccentSoftBrush`, background+foreground pairs for info, link, success, warning and error, and two for printed pages (`PrintRuleBrush` and `PrintPaperBrush`, see the Products screen's Print). **The theme is Catppuccin Latte** (2026-09-25, at the user's request; it replaced a Tailwind Amber scale with Stone neutrals). `Themes/Palette.xaml`, merged first into `Application.Resources`, holds all 26 Latte colours by their Catppuccin names (`LatteMauveColor`, `LatteBaseBrush` and so on), and **every token names one of them; no token carries a hex code**. The roles follow Catppuccin's style guide: the page is Mantle, cards Base with a Crust border, headings Text, muted text Subtext 1 (Subtext 0 is only 4.4:1 on Base), read-only values Overlay 0, the accent Sky (the user's pick, 2026-09-25; Mauve for the first hours), links Blue, success Green, warning Yellow, error Red, and the neutral info notice (Raw Materials' linked-cost preview) Mauve, so it reads apart from the warning, the link and the accent (it was Sapphire while Mauve was the accent, but Sapphire and Sky read as one colour). **Latte's hues are pastels, so the palette file adds two derived steps per hue that needs them**: a *Tint* (the hue at 10% over Base) for a banner or badge's background, and a *Deep* shade (the hue darkened toward black in 5% steps until it makes 4.5:1 on its Tint and on Base) for text in that hue. As text on a light background only Mauve, Blue and Red reach 4.5:1 unaided; Green, Yellow, Peach, Sky, Sapphire and Teal fall to 2.3–3.3:1. Darkening toward black keeps the hue, where darkening toward Text turned Green and Yellow grey. So each coloured pair is `<Hue>Tint`/`<Hue>Deep` (link: Blue, since Blue itself on its Tint is only 3.7:1 at the badges' 11px), the toasts' borders are the plain hue, and `AccentSoftBrush` (the Costing Summary's Subtotal and Total Selling Price bands) is the Sky Tint. `AccentBrush` is Sky, and `App.xaml.cs` reads `LatteSkyColor` for the theme's `PrimaryBackground`, so the two can't drift. **Sky is the palest accent Latte has**: white on it is only 2.8:1 and none of Latte's own colours reaches 3:1 on it, so the palette adds a third derived step for it, an *Ink* (Sky darkened toward black until it makes 4.5:1 on Sky itself, `#013245`), used as the theme's `PrimaryForeground`. `AccentBrush` (plain Sky) is for fills, lines and borders (the grids' `CurrentCellBorderBrush`, the unit diagram's boxes); accent *text and glyphs* (Current Price, the Total Selling Price figures, the unit diagram's arrows, the fallback product glyph) use `AccentForegroundBrush`, Sky's Deep shade (`#036B95`), since Sky is 2.5:1 on Base. The two chart brushes (Mauve base cost, Sky selling price) went with Reports' charts on 2026-09-27. Only the product category icons (cake, pastry, bread) keep their own colours, as illustrations, and the hover washes stay translucent black. **The theme's own neutrals aren't Latte**: `Windows11LightThemeSettings` exposes only the primary colour and fonts, so Syncfusion inputs and grids keep Windows11Light's white fields and grey borders. Recolouring those would take a custom theme built in Syncfusion Theme Studio, since Syncfusion controls are never restyled through a `Style`. **Every `TabControlExt` is recoloured through its own properties** (`Background`, `TabPanelBackground`, `TabItemSelectedBackground`, `TabItemHoverBackground`; the Sample Browser's Unit Converter demo sets the same ones inline), since the theme's content area is white and its strip and tabs neutral greys (`#F3F3F3`, selected `#F9F9F9`): the main window's tabs showed a white frame around every screen, and the in-card tabs a white panel on the Base card. A strip is a step darker than what it sits over and the selected tab is its content's colour, so tab and page read as one piece: the window's tabs (`MainWindow`, `SettingsWindow`) have a Crust strip over the Mantle page (`PageTabStripBrush`, hover Surface 0 via `PageTabHoverBrush`), and the tabs inside a card (Raw Materials' and Products' detail tabs, a product's recipe tabs) a Mantle strip over the Base card (`CardTabStripBrush`, hover Crust via `CardTabHoverBrush`). **The header figures sit on coloured tiles** (2026-09-25, at the user's request; a plain row with a thin Crust line between each figure before): each figure in the Raw Materials, Sub-Assemblies and Products headers has a rounded `FigureTileStyle` block behind its label and value, 8 apart (`FigureTileGapStyle`, in the columns the lines had), in a Latte *Wash*, a fourth derived step (the hue at 20% over Base, `Latte<Hue>WashColor`, brushed as `<Hue>TileBrush` in App.xaml). Full-strength Latte hues can't take text (Text on them is 1.5–3:1); on a Wash it's 5.3–6:1. A kind of figure keeps its colour on every screen: Base Unit Rosewater, a cost as it stands (Current Cost, Material Cost) Peach, a cost per gram (Buffered Cost, Cost per g) Yellow, a total weight Teal, a total cost (Total Cost, Batch Cost, Set Cost) Flamingo, Base Cost Pink, Current Price the accent's Sky, Markup % Green, Last Updated Mauve. Labels on a tile are Text (`FigureTileLabelStyle`), not muted, since Subtext 1 on the Mauve Wash is 4.1:1; Current Price's value is Text too, since Sky's Deep shade makes only 4.35:1 on the Sky Wash (Markup %'s red, at 4.65:1 on Green, stays). The keyed styles are built from the tokens (`CardStyle`, `SectionHeaderStyle`, `FieldLabelStyle`, `CaptionStyle`, `EmptyStateStyle`, `ErrorBannerStyle`, `AppTitleStyle`; the `WarningBannerStyle` that stood beside it went with the last banner using it, 2026-09-27). **`TextBlock` has no `CharacterSpacing` property in WPF** — that's a WinUI/UWP-only property; a `Style TargetType="TextBlock"` `Setter` for it fails the build with `MC4005`, found while first writing `AppTitleStyle`. **These styles are keyed and target only native elements (`Border`, `TextBlock`) — never a `Style` with no `x:Key`, and never one whose `TargetType` is a Syncfusion control.** An implicit (unkeyed) style on a native type would leak into every Syncfusion control template that hosts one internally; a `Style` set directly on a Syncfusion control would replace its themed template outright. Syncfusion controls only ever get recolored through `Windows11LightThemeSettings`, per the bullet above. `EmptyStateVisibilityConverter` (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/Converters/EmptyStateVisibilityConverter.cs`) is registered alongside them — `Visible` when the bound value is `null` or `false`, `Collapsed` otherwise, so the same converter drives an empty-state hint off either a nullable selection (`SelectedIngredient == null`) or a boolean flag (Products' `HasMaterialCost == false`). `ListBackgroundBrush` (2026-09-26) and `PrintPaperBrush` (2026-09-27) are the only tokens that aren't Latte colours. `ListBackgroundBrush` is white, the Windows11Light theme's `ContentBackground` that fills every `SfDataGrid`'s rows, for a list the theme leaves transparent (the Products tree), so it matches the grids. `PrintPaperBrush` is the printout's paper, white whatever the theme.
- **`ErrorToast`, the error toast** (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/Controls/ErrorToast.xaml`; 2026-09-14, at the user's request, replacing the Phase 16 `StatusBanner`). A screen's `ErrorMessage` shows as a toast in its page's bottom-right corner, drawn over the cards: a red error icon, "Error", the message and a close button. Like every control here, it never sets its own `DataContext`; it binds to the `ErrorMessage` every `ViewModelBase` has, so a screen only drops it into its root Grid, spanning every row and column (the toast aligns itself to the corner, 16 px in).
  - It stays until its close button is clicked or the error clears, which the next action does (`RunSafelyAsync` clears `ErrorMessage` first); a new error replaces it, and the same error twice still shows twice. Closing it only hides it: the error stays on the ViewModel.
  - The banner it replaced took a row at the top of every page, leaving an empty 12 px strip, and carried a busy bar. Both went: saves take well under a second and buttons disable while they run, so `ViewModelBase.IsBusy` and `HasError` were removed too.
  - **Why not Syncfusion's `SfToastNotification`, which the user asked for first:** tried and dropped (34.2.7). Its toasts are always-on-top popups, so an error left open floated over other programs (it covered Excel). And `Window` mode always anchors to the window's outer corner, 12 px in, reading `Window.Left/Top`, which a maximized WPF window reports as its restore position, so a maximized window's toast landed too low. It has no offset setting (the `Margin` in the 34.2.2 docs is gone). Its defaults also add a "Here's an update for you" header and Continue/Cancel buttons. The user chose a toast drawn inside the page instead.
  - `ReportsView` has no toast: nothing there can fail (its Phase 12 rationale, still true of the summary grids).
- **`SuccessToast`, the success toast** (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/Controls/SuccessToast.xaml`; 2026-09-15, at the user's request: a toast after saving a raw material). A screen's `SuccessMessage` (on `ViewModelBase`, cleared with `ErrorMessage` at the start of `RunSafelyAsync`) shows in the error toast's corner, laid out like it in green: a check icon, "Success", the message and a close button. A screen drops it into its root Grid beside the `ErrorToast`; since every action clears both messages first, only one shows at a time.
  - It fades out 4 seconds after it opens, unless the mouse is over it (checked again every 4 seconds), and its close button hides it at once. An error replaces it. Verified in the app: shown after Save, gone 5 seconds later, still up after 6 seconds under the mouse.
  - Only the Raw Materials screen sets it so far: its Save says "Cake Flour was saved.", naming the material as saved.
  - The close button's style (`ToastCloseButtonStyle`) moved from `ErrorToast` into `App.xaml` for both toasts, next to the new `SuccessBorderBrush`.
- **`SfChart` shape** (kept for when a chart comes back: no screen has one since 2026-09-27) (`Syncfusion.UI.Xaml.Charts` namespace, first used Phase 12 — confirmed by reflecting over the built `Syncfusion.SfChart.WPF.dll` the same way `DateTimeEdit` was, rather than guessing): the root `SfChart` element takes exactly one `PrimaryAxis` and one `SecondaryAxis` (property elements, not collections) plus a `Series` collection (`SfChart.Series`) of `ChartSeriesBase`-derived series (`LineSeries`, `ColumnSeries`, …) and an optional `Legend` (`SfChart.Legend`, a `ChartLegend`). Each series binds independently — `ItemsSource`, `XBindingPath`, `YBindingPath`, `Label` (the legend text) all live on `ChartSeriesBase`/`XyDataSeries`, so two series (e.g. `ColumnSeries` for "Base Cost" and another for "Selling Price") can share one `ItemsSource` and just point `YBindingPath` at different properties of the same row type. `DateTimeAxis` binds cleanly to a plain `DateTime` `XBindingPath` — project a `DateOnly` column to `DateTime` (`.ToDateTime(TimeOnly.MinValue)`) in the chart-row DTO rather than binding `DateOnly` directly, since that conversion's support wasn't worth gambling on for a type newer than the library's own value-binding internals. **A fixed, distinct color per series is just `Interior="{StaticResource ...Brush}"` on that series** (`ChartSeriesBase`) — `Palette="Custom"` plus a `ColorModel`/`ChartColorModel.CustomBrushes` list (Phase 16's Reports rework first reached for this, going by the pattern in `.claude/skills/syncfusion-wpf-charts`) is for coloring the individual *segments within one series* differently (e.g. each category's bar its own color), not for giving two separate named series — "Base Cost" and "Selling Price" here — two different fixed colors; using it for that would need the two series merged into one with a value-based color selector, which is unnecessary when `Interior` alone does the job per series. Direct value labels on a `ColumnSeries` (so a comparison doesn't rely on color alone) are `<syncfusion:ColumnSeries.AdornmentsInfo><syncfusion:ChartAdornmentInfo ShowLabel="True" /></syncfusion:ColumnSeries.AdornmentsInfo>` — the property is `AdornmentsInfo`, the element type is `ChartAdornmentInfo`. **One series whose segments each need their own colour is where `Palette="Custom"` + `ChartColorModel` does apply** (Products' Recipe Cost Overview, 2026-09-14 until it was removed on 2026-09-19, following the Sample Browser's `Circular Charts\Doughnut\DoughnutChart.xaml` and `StackedDoughnut\CustomizedStackedDoughnut.xaml`): a `DoughnutSeries` over `CostShares`, with a `ChartColorModel` in the card's own resources (`CustomBrushes`: the amber accent, then teal, amber-light, slate, rose, sky, lime, violet, so neighbouring slices differ). The legend's `ItemTemplate` binds `{Binding Interior}` for the swatch and `Item.*` for the row's own fields (`Item.Name`, `Item.Amount`, `Item.Percent`). `CenterView` needs a `ContentControl`, as in the sample (a panel is rejected at runtime), with `Content="{Binding Costs}"` and a template.
- **Reports: two summary grids on sub-tabs** (2026-09-27, at the user's request: the charts were "not showing much"; "remove the graph for now let's replace it with subtabs for report such as Product Cost Summary, Raw Materials Price Summary"). `ReportsView` is one card holding a `TabControlExt` styled like the in-card detail tabs (Mantle strip along the bottom, fixed tabs, no drag, no menus), each tab a read-only `SfDataGrid` that sorts on a header click, with a heading and a one-line caption.
  - **Product Cost Summary** (`ProductCostRow`): Product, Stage, Category, Pieces (`QuantityRequired`), Base Cost, Selling Price, Current Price and Markup % (`ProductCostSummary.CurrentMarkupPercent`, what the current price earns, as the Products header shows it). Every product as saved, priced through `SavedProductReader`, so it agrees with Products and Print. Blank Current Price and Markup % without a current price (`AllowNullValue`). **Double-clicking a row opens its product on Products** (2026-09-27, at the user's request): the grid's `CellDoubleTapped` (record cells only, so a header double-click still just sorts) runs `ReportsViewModel.OpenProductCommand`, whose `ProductOpenRequests` `MainWindow` merges with Raw Materials' and Sub-Assemblies' Used By requests: `ProductsViewModel.OpenProduct`, then `ShowTab(ProductsTab)`. **The open waits until the double-click's press is over** (`Dispatcher.BeginInvoke` at `Input` priority in `ReportsView`; found the same day, when the user reported that the double-click didn't go anywhere). The grid raises `CellDoubleTapped` from `GridCell.OnMouseDoubleClick`, halfway through the second press's `MouseLeftButtonDown`. Switching the main tab there takes Reports' content off the window, so the rest of that press bubbles through the content's *logical* parent, the Reports `TabItemExt`, and `TabItem.OnMouseLeftButtonDown` focuses that tab, which selects it again. Products flashed and went. Reproduced and confirmed fixed with a scratch harness: the real `MainWindow`, built from `CompositionRoot` and shown off-screen, a double-click raised on a grid cell (`ClickCount` 2, set by reflection), and a `SelectionChanged` stack trace. The Used By tiles never hit this because a button clicks on the release. **Any handler that changes the main tab from inside a mouse press has to defer the change the same way.**
  - **Raw Materials Price Summary** (`RawMaterialPriceSummaryRow`): every *purchased* material (made and linked ones have no price history), by name: Raw Material, Type, Category, Unit, Price, Change % and Since. Price, Unit and Since are the latest price-history entry; Change % is its cost per base unit against the entry before it, per base unit because the two can be in different units (a sack, then a kilo). Blank figures for a material never priced, and a blank Change % with one price. Read with one query for every material's history, `IRawMaterialPriceHistoryRepository.GetAllAsync` (grouped by material, newest first), rather than one per material.
  - **Several products are read at once** (2026-09-27, the user reported a delay loading it). A product's reads were six round trips one after another (`SavedProductReader.ReadDetailAsync`; since 2026-09-28 all but the sections' lines go at once, so up to 40 connections with 8 products), about 110 for 18 products. They were done one at a time, and the tab took 0.4–1.8 s to fill. `ReadProductCostsAsync` now reads up to 8 products at once (`Parallel.ForEachAsync`, `ProductsReadAtOnce`; each read has its own context, and 8 is well inside SqlClient's pool of 100). The rows go into an array by index, so they stay in name order. Both summaries are read inside `Task.Run`, as Products and Raw Materials read their details. Measured in an off-screen copy of the real window against the dev database: visits after the first fell to 0.18–0.28 s. The first visit after launch is still about 1.6 s, mostly first-use cost for queries the startup screen didn't run (the same reads cold in a console: 0.58 s sequential, 0.33 s parallel). `Task.Run` alone made no measurable difference; the round trips were the cost. Print's sets (`PrintTabViewModel`) still read one product at a time, for their "Preparing 12 of 30…" progress.
  - The Product, Stage, Pieces and Raw Material columns have fixed widths and the rest are `Star`: `ColumnSizer="Auto"` measured the empty grid before the rows arrived and sized to the headers.
  - **Tests**: `ReportsViewModelTests` (the add-in pricing kept from the chart, and the price summary's latest price, unit and change across units, and a never-priced material) and a `BrandPricePersistenceTests` case for `GetAllAsync`. Before, the screen had no tests of its own on the grounds that it only re-orchestrated tested pieces (Phase 12); the add-in bug above showed that doesn't hold.
  - **The old open item "Reports' price trend can double on a quick material change" went with the chart**: there's no material picker any more.

## Application Architecture

- **Repository interfaces live in `ApplicationCore`, implementations in `Infrastructure`** (`IRawMaterialRepository`/`RawMaterialRepository`, and so on), consumed by UI ViewModels through DI. `ILookupRepository<T>` in `ApplicationCore`, with one generic `LookupRepository<T>` implementation in `Infrastructure`, covers every simple **read-only** lookup table (`UnitOfMeasure`, `IngredientCategory`, `PackagingCategory`) instead of writing a near-identical class per table. `SubAssemblyRole` turned out **not** to fit this: unlike those three, it's user-editable (Phase 7 gave it its own small embedded management panel), so it needed a dedicated `ISubAssemblyRoleRepository`/`SubAssemblyRoleRepository` with full CRUD instead. `ILookupRepository<T>` is for genuinely read-only lookups only — don't reach for it just because a table is small.
- **`TinapayanRMSDbContext` is registered with `AddDbContextFactory`, not `AddDbContext`.** ViewModels are DI singletons (`MainWindowViewModel`, `RawMaterialsViewModel`, …) so they outlive any single screen, and WPF has no ASP.NET-style per-request scope to hand a plain `AddDbContext`-registered instance to — resolving one into two singleton ViewModels at startup handed them the *same* `DbContext` instance, and two screens' tabs loading concurrently on app startup then threw `InvalidOperationException: A second operation was started on this context instance before a previous operation completed` the first time the app was actually run (not caught by the build or by any test — only by launching it). Every repository instead takes `IDbContextFactory<TinapayanRMSDbContext>` and creates-and-disposes a short-lived context per call (`await using var dbContext = await dbContextFactory.CreateDbContextAsync(cancellationToken);`), which is also naturally safe for concurrent calls from different screens. Follow this pattern for every future repository — never inject `TinapayanRMSDbContext` directly into a repository or ViewModel.
- **CRUD screen pattern** (established by `IngredientsView`/`IngredientsViewModel` and `PackagingMaterialsView`/`PackagingMaterialsViewModel`, Phase 4, merged into `RawMaterialsView`/`RawMaterialsViewModel` on 2026-09-13; card layout and right-rail arrangement reworked Phase 16): a read-only `SfDataGrid` list on the left, bound to a `DynamicData SourceCache<T,int>` via `.Connect().SortAndBind(out collection, comparer).Subscribe()` (not the obsolete `.Sort().Bind()` pair); a master-detail edit form on the right, populated from the grid's `SelectedItem` via a `WhenAnyValue` subscription; `New`/`Save`/`Delete` as `ButtonAdv`s bound to `ReactiveCommand`s. Follow this pattern for future CRUD screens rather than in-grid cell editing, which needs SfDataGrid event wiring that's much harder to get right without a live designer.
  - **Since Phase 16, the shape is a list card plus a right rail, not fixed Grid columns of bare panels.** The root `Grid` carries `PageBackgroundBrush`, `Margin="16"`, and an `ErrorToast` laid over the whole Grid (it takes no row of its own). The Grid is two columns: a `*`-width list card (`CardStyle` `Border` + `SectionHeaderStyle` header + the `SfDataGrid`, `ColumnSizer="AutoWithLastColumnFill"` so every column — including money columns — fits without a horizontal scrollbar; `RawMaterialsView` gives every column a fixed width instead and sizes its list card to fit them (see the Raw Materials note below). A column's `Width` accepts pixels only, as in `Width="120"`. A value of `"*"` or `"2*"` builds cleanly but crashes the app at startup with a `XamlParseException` ("* is not a valid value for Double"), so star-size through `ColumnSizer="Star"` on the grid or on a column), and a fixed ~360px right rail in a `ScrollViewer` holding, top to bottom: a Details card (the edit form), the screen's existing conditional detail card(s) (Price History, BOM, Lines) shown under their existing `Show*` flags with an `EmptyStateStyle` hint (bound through `EmptyStateVisibilityConverter`) filling the same slot when nothing is selected, and a collapsed `Expander` for any embedded lookup panel (see the note below). `UnitsOfMeasureView` and `CategoriesView`, the Settings dialog's tabs, are the simplest case — list card plus a single form card, no expander, no conditional detail card. Every number column in a grid, money included, uses `controls:NumericColumn` (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/Controls/NumericColumn.cs`). Never use `syncfusion:GridNumericColumn` or `syncfusion:GridCurrencyColumn` directly. Money shows as a bare number (`930.00`, not `₱930.00`), so `GridCurrencyColumn` would add nothing useful: its currency editor never opens in these read-only grids, its parentheses for negatives never apply because costs are never negative, and it doesn't group thousands by default. Money columns use 2 decimals (`NumberDecimalDigits`) for totals and prices, and 4 for per-unit costs (`CostPerBaseUnit`, `CostPerPiece`). A packaging line's own total (`PackagingLineRow.CostPerUnit`, headed Cost per Set, Cost per Unit or Cost per Piece) counts as a total: 2 decimals since 2026-09-28, at the user's request; its Unit Cost stays at 4. `NumericColumn` is a `GridNumericColumn` whose constructor sets the app's defaults: `NumberGroupSeparator=","`, `NumberGroupSizes="3"` and `HorizontalHeaderContentAlignment="Right"`. Out of the box, `GridNumericColumn` doesn't group thousands (`NumberGroupSizes` is `[0]`) and centres its header. A grid column is a `Freezable`, not a `FrameworkElement`, so an implicit `Style` can't carry these defaults. This was confirmed by reflecting over `Syncfusion.SfGrid.WPF` 34.2.7. Set only what differs per column (`NumberDecimalDigits`); a value set in XAML still overrides a default.
  - **`ProductsView` (Phase 8/9) no longer uses a horizontal `ScrollViewer` of fixed-width `StackPanel`s — Phase 16 replaced that with sub-tabs plus a pinned summary**, once the six-panel version pushed the Selling Price summary off-screen. Now three columns: a 300px-wide left column (Products list card, then a Product form card below it), a centre `TabControlExt` (`CloseButtonType="Hide"`, `ShowTabItemContextMenu="False"`, `ShowTabListContextMenu="False"`, every `TabItemExt` `CanClose="False"` — these are fixed sub-tabs, not user-closable top-level tabs) holding **Usages**/**Packaging**/**Costing**/**Links & Clone**, each carrying its existing panel's content verbatim, and a pinned 260px right column with the Price Summary card (Material Cost, Base Cost, Pre-VAT Price, then Selling Price large and `AccentBrush`-colored) that stays visible across every sub-tab. Both the tab strip and the summary card are visible only when `ShowProductDetail` is true; an `EmptyStateStyle` hint fills the centre otherwise. The linked-tab notices (`IsSelectedRoleLinked`/`IsPackagingLinked`) moved from inline amber `TextBlock`s into `WarningBannerStyle` banners.
    - **Superseded (2026-09-14): the sub-tabs, pinned summary and per-part Saves are gone.** The screen was rebuilt from the user's Cream Loaf / Chocolate Cake mockups, in the Raw Materials shape: see "One Products screen" below.
- **Grids show lines both ways, and a grid with height to spare is padded with empty rows down to its bottom edge** (2026-09-15, at the user's request, following the technique in their `SfDataGrid_Demo_fixed` sample).
  - **`GridLinesVisibility="Both"` is set on every `SfDataGrid` in `Views/`.** The property's own default is Both, but the Windows11Light style sets Horizontal, so it's set locally on each grid (the app doesn't style Syncfusion controls itself: a `Style` would replace the theme's). The two recipe section tables draw their Total row under the grid, so each total cell but the last has a right divider (`SectionTotalDividerStyle`, `CardBorderBrush`) carrying the grid's vertical lines through it. The ingredient pickers' pop-up grids keep their look.
  - **The tables lines are typed into show a row header (`ShowRowHeader="True"`)** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: "add row selector to datagrids", the typed-into grids when asked which): Packaging, the Products sub-assembly form's lines and add-ins, Sub-Assemblies' lines, the Bill of Materials and Alternative Units. It's the grid's own column: an arrow on the current row, a pencil while a row is edited and + on the add row; filler rows' header cells stay blank. The read-only lists (Raw Materials, Products, Sub-Assemblies, Price History, Settings) don't have it. The app's grid behaviours need no change for it: they go by row index, and `GridFillerRows.IsRecordCell` uses `GetFirstColumnIndex`, which counts the header. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor on all five screens' grids.
  - **Rows are dragged into order** (2026-09-23, at the user's request: "Sub-Assembly Lines, Product Recipe Lines, Packaging Lines, and BOM should allow reordering of rows through dragging"). `controls:GridRowReordering.IsEnabled="True"` (`Controls/GridRowReordering.cs`) is on the seven grids lines are typed into and saved from: Sub-Assemblies' lines and a packaging set's lines, the Products sub-assembly form's lines, add-ins and packaging, the Packaging tab, and the Bill of Materials. Not on Alternative Units or Purchase Cost Setup, whose order means nothing. The order is the ViewModel collection's, which Save writes (see Domain Model: Line order), and it needs nothing else to count as a change: every form snapshot compares its lines with `SequenceEqual`, so a move is an undo step, marks the form dirty, and an undo puts the order back.
    - **It turns on the grid's own row drag** (`AllowDraggingRows`, `AllowDrop`, `RowDropIndicatorMode="Line"`), which starts from the row header or any cell not being typed into, once the mouse has moved the system drag distance: `GridRowHeaderCell` is a `GridCell`, and `GridCell.OnPreviewMouseDown` arms a drag only when the cell isn't editing, so selecting text in an open cell never drags the row. A plain click still opens the cell (`EditTrigger="OnTap"`).
    - **Each press decides whether the grid can drag** (`ArmOnLinePress`, on the grid's `PreviewMouseDown`, which tunnels before the cell's): only a press on a line of a grid whose lines can be changed sets `AllowDraggingRows`. Once armed, `GridCell` calls `ProcessOnDragStarting` on every mouse move until the button comes up, and that logs Syncfusion telemetry each time (`TelemetryService.Log` → `Telemetry.TrackFeature`, a new client and an HTTP post per call, no de-duplication; decompiled from 34.2.7). A read-only grid's drag is refused and the add row has no line to drag, so either would have retried, and posted, on every move of the gesture. Presses on filler rows never reach the cell (`GridFillerRows` handles them).
    - **Every drop is handled by the behaviour, because the grid's own doesn't reorder anything that's saved.** Read from the decompiled `GridRowDragDropController` (Syncfusion 34.2.7): within one grid, `DroppingItemsInDataGrid` removes and re-inserts the `RecordEntry` in `View.Records` only, so the bound `ObservableCollection` never moves. And across grids it moves a row into any other grid whose items are the same type, which the recipe lines and add-ins are (`IngredientLineRow`). So `Drop` is always `Handled`, and the line is moved in the collection with `ObservableCollection<T>.Move`, bracketed by the grid's `GridUndoSteps` recorder. It's a single Move notification, not a remove and an insert, so the row stays selected and a formula-base line never briefly leaves the batch, which would set off the baker's-percentage recomputation twice. `Move` is found by reflection, since the behaviour only knows the collection as an `IList`.
    - **The drop's `TargetRecord` is a record index in the view, not the item** (`ResolveToRecordIndex` of the row dropped on), and the line grids allow sorting, so it's resolved through `View.Records[i].Data` and then found in the collection. A sorted view is unsorted on the drop (`SortColumnDescriptions.Clear()`), so the grid shows the order that was just changed rather than a sort that hides it.
    - **Refused:** a drag from a read-only grid (`AllowEditing` false: a linked section's lines, a linked packaging), a drag in from another grid (`IsFromOutSideSource`, `DragOver` handled so no drop line shows), and a drop on the add row or a filler row, both of which `ResolveToRecordIndex` gives -1, so the grid's `GetDropPosition` returns `None` for them itself. Selection is `Single`, so one line moves at a time.
    - **Verified in the app** on the secondary monitor against the dev database, with real mouse drags and nothing saved: Sub-Assemblies' lines (up from the row header, down from a cell, and in a view sorted by Grams, which the drop unsorted with the line placed by the lines' own order), the Products Packaging tab, and a made ingredient's Bill of Materials each moved the line, kept it selected, and offered Save and Undo; Undo put the order back and Save went off again. A linked section's lines didn't move, and an add-in dragged onto the recipe lines above it showed no drop line and stayed where it was. Not driven by hand: a packaging set's lines and the Products form's own lines and packaging (the same behaviour on the same kind of grid). That a dragged order survives a save is `LineOrderPersistenceTests`' to show.
  - **Every grid's header row fits its text** (2026-09-27, at the user's request: "Increase the height of all the datagrid header to fit the font height"). The Windows11Light grid style fixes `HeaderRowHeight` at 24, sized for its 12px body text; since the fonts went up 2 (App's `BodyFontSize = 14`), a header's text needs 19px plus the header cell's 3px padding above and below and 1px bottom border, so descenders were cut ("Ingredient" read "Inqredient"). `Controls/GridHeaderHeight`, registered once from `App`'s static constructor (class handlers on `SfDataGrid` for `Loaded` and `SizeChanged`), sets each grid's `HeaderRowHeight` (a local value, unless its XAML sets one) from a header cell's own font, padding and border: 26 today. **The header cell's font isn't the grid's**: the theme gives `GridHeaderCellControl` its `BodyTextStyle` (14) while the grid's own `FontSize` stays 12, so it's measured from a realized header cell. A grid hidden when it loads has none, so it's fitted the first time it's sized, once per grid (an attached `IsFitted` flag), which covers the multi-column drop-downs' lists too. Checked by rendering a themed grid off-screen at the app's font sizes (before: the g's descender clipped; after: whole), and covered by `GridHeaderHeightTests`. Rendering a themed control in the test host needs `PackUriHelper.UriSchemePack` touched and an `Application` created first, or the theme's `pack://` resources throw "The URI prefix is not recognized".
  - **Keyboard focus in a grid sits on a cell, never on the whole grid** (2026-09-27, at the user's request: "Keyboard focus must be on the grid instead of the outline. It's absurd and nonstandard"). `Controls/GridKeyboardFocus`, registered once from `App`'s static constructor (class handlers on `SfDataGrid`, so every grid, including future ones, gets it without XAML): as a grid loads its `FocusVisualStyle` is set to null (a local value, unless its XAML sets one), which removes the dotted rectangle WPF drew round the whole grid whenever the grid itself took focus by keyboard (tabbed into, or handed focus back as a cell's edit ended); and when the grid itself takes keyboard focus by keyboard (`InputManager.MostRecentInputDevice`, so a click, which places the current cell where it lands, is left alone), `PlaceCurrentCell` gives a grid without a current cell one in the first column: on the selected row, else the first line, else an empty table's add row (without starting a line). A row-selecting list (`NavigationMode="Row"`: the Sub-Assemblies list, the Settings lists, Price History) only gets one on its selected row, since selecting a row there opens it in the form, which tabbing past mustn't do. Covered by `GridKeyboardFocusTests`; the Tab path itself needs a focused window, so it wasn't verified in the app (only one monitor was connected that day).
  - **`CurrentCellBorderBrush="{StaticResource AccentBrush}"` is set on every `SfDataGrid` in `Views/`, the same way and for the same reason** (2026-09-17, at the user's request: the cell selector outline was black). `SfGridBase` registers the property with a hardcoded `SolidColorBrush(Colors.Black)` default (confirmed by decompiling `Syncfusion.SfGrid.WPF` 34.2.7 with `ilspycmd`) that the Windows11Light accent re-color (`PrimaryBackground` in `App.xaml.cs`'s `OnStartup`) doesn't reach, so it stays black regardless of theme unless set explicitly. `CurrentCellBorderThickness` (default `2`) is set to `1` too, at the user's request the same day. `1.5` looked identical to the default when checked pixel-by-pixel in a screenshot (both rendered a 2px border at the secondary monitor's 100% scale — `UseLayoutRounding` snaps a fractional thickness up to the next whole device pixel), so only a whole-number step down actually reads as thinner.
  - **`controls:GridFillerRows.IsEnabled="True"`** (`Controls/GridFillerRows.cs`) is on the grids with a height to fill: the Raw Materials, Products and Sub-Assemblies lists, Price History, Settings' Units, Categories and Roles, and the tables lines are typed into (Sub-Assemblies' lines, the Products sub-assembly form and Packaging tab, the Bill of Materials). Not on the recipe section tables, which are sized to their rows, or Alternative Units, whose `MaxHeight` would make it always 240 px tall.
  - Like the sample, the rows are `GridUnBoundRow`s, recalculated when the grid loads, resizes, its view's records change, or a group expands or collapses. **What differs was read from the decompiled Syncfusion 34.2.7 (ilspycmd):**
    - **`ShowBelowSummary = true`.** `GridIndexResolver.ResolveUnboundRowToRowIndex` and `GridAddNewRowController.GetAddNewRowIndex` put bottom unbound rows *without* it before the add-new row, which would have sunk the add row below the filler rows. With it they're the grid's last rows.
    - **Those rows are frozen footers** (`UpdateFreezePaneRows` counts them in `FooterRows`). With a short body they follow it (`ScrollAxisBase.GetVisibleLines` starts footers at `Max(HeaderExtent, ViewSize − FooterExtent)`, and `PixelScrollAxis.ViewSize` is `Min(RenderSize, TotalDistance)`), but rows that no longer fit would be pinned over the grid's last rows. So rows that don't fit are removed at once, synchronously, when records are added, a group expands or the grid shrinks, **and after any layout pass that finds them overflowing** (see the crash notes below); adding rows waits for layout (one `Background`-priority recalculation however many triggers ask) and, when the grid grows, for 150 ms of calm.
    - **The space is measured on the row scroll axis:** `GetVisualContainer().ScrollRows` (`PixelScrollAxis`) `RenderSize` against `TotalExtent`, which counts the header, group captions and add-new row as drawn, in O(1). `GridFillerRows.FillerRowCount` (unit-tested) floors to whole rows, so filler never adds a scrollbar.
    - **Only the difference is added or removed, never `Clear()`**: `UnBoundRows`' Reset handling (and `ReCalculateUnboundRowsCount`) counts rows with `Select(cond).Count()`, which counts every row.
    - **The rows are inert.** Unbound rows are selectable, and in a single-selection list a click on one deselects the record, which clears the form beside it; in a table with `EditTrigger="OnTap"` a click anywhere begins editing the current cell (`GridCurrentCellManager.ProcessOnTapped`). A press on a filler row is handled in `PreviewMouseDown`, and `SelectionChanging`/`CurrentCellActivating` are cancelled for one (keyboard). Filler rows are recognised by position (the last rows of `VisualContainer.RowCount`), not with `SfDataGrid.GetUnBoundRow`: an unbound row's `RowIndex` isn't kept up to date as records arrive, and a first version using it swallowed clicks on the Ingredients group caption.
      - **Only a press in the grid's own window counts** (2026-09-17, reported by the user: a packaging material picked with the mouse never showed in its cell). A drop-down cell's list is a `Popup`, a window of its own but inside the grid's tree, so its presses tunnel through the grid's `PreviewMouseDown` too, and the filler-row check was by position alone. Where the list opens below its cell it lies over the filler rows, so a click on an item was taken for a click on a filler row and stopped. The list's own `GridCell` then never recorded the press (`GridSelectionController.ProcessPointerPressed`), the release selected nothing, and `SfMultiColumnDropDownControl.OnInternalGridTapped` committed no item. Opened above (the sub-assembly form, lower on the screen) it worked, which is what gave it away. Now the press must come from the grid's own `PresentationSource`. It hit every filler-row grid with a drop-down; found with a temporary trace of the press's `Handled` flag down the route. Verified in the app: a material picked with the mouse from a list opened below the add row, and a click on a filler row still selecting nothing.
    - **Changing unbound rows reaches the selection** (`GridSelectionController.ProcessUnBoundRowChanged`): it ends an edit of an existing row without committing it, and adding one while the current cell is on the row above the add-new row moves the current cell onto that row. Nothing changes while `View.IsEditingItem` (tried again 150 ms later), and a current cell moved by an addition is put back (`MoveCurrentCell`) — **only from the add-new row, and only onto a record's cell that still exists** (see the crash notes below).
    - **A current cell left on a filler row is put back on the add-new row** (2026-09-25, reported by the user: "sometimes two cell selection is being shown simultaneously" on Raw Materials' Purchase Cost Setup). When the last lines go while the current cell is on the add-new row, SfDataGrid moves the add-new row up but leaves `CurrentRowColumnIndex` at the old row index. Without filler rows nothing is there; with them a filler row is, and the grid outlines a cell on it as well as keeping the add-new row cell's outline, and every later click moves only the filler row's outline. Ctrl+Z back to no brands did it (`UndoShortcuts` commits the grid's edit, then `RestoreSnapshot` clears `BrandPriceRows`), and so would any clear of a typed-into table with the add-new row current. `FillerState.MoveCurrentCellOffFillerRow` moves it onto the add-new row one dispatcher pass after `Apply`: straight after rows are added the grid can't find the add-new row's cell yet, notes only the index, and the next move then leaves that cell outlined. Test: `Removing_every_line_while_the_add_row_is_current_leaves_one_current_cell_on_the_add_row` (fails on the old code). Verified in the app on the secondary monitor with real clicks and keystrokes, nothing saved: a new material's first brand typed in, Ctrl+Z from the add-new row's Purchase Price, then clicks on Buffer % and Brand each left one outline.
  - **Two crashes it caused, fixed 2026-09-16** (reported as "sometimes the program crashes"; neither could be reproduced by clicking around). The evidence was the Windows Application event log (`.NET Runtime`, event 1026, which keeps the full stack of an unhandled exception) and the app's own Serilog file (`logs/tinapayanrms-<date>.log` beside the exe), whose warnings showed what the user did just before. Both were then reproduced by **fuzzing a real `SfDataGrid`** off-screen: random sequences of what the screens do to a grid, with the app's own behaviours attached, until one threw. The fuzz harnesses were throwaway; the sequences they found are the tests.
    - **"InValid RowColumnIndex" from `FillerState.Apply`** (twice on the Products screen, each shortly after an add-in was refused). The grid keeps the current cell of a row that's gone: select a line (the grid's `SelectedItem`, which `SelectedFormLine` binds), then replace the lines (another sub-assembly's form), and the current cell still points at the old row index. Filler rows added to the emptier table make the grid drop it (`ProcessUnBoundRowChanged`), and `Apply` moved it back — to a row past `GetLastNavigatingRowIndex`, which `CanMoveCurrentCell` rejects by throwing, from a `Background` dispatcher callback, so nothing caught it. **The move back now only undoes the one move it exists for**: the current cell pushed from a record onto the add-new row, and only onto a record's cell that still exists (`ResolveToRecordIndex`, the visible column range). `GridFillerRowsCurrentCellTests` fails on the old code with the same exception.
    - **`ArgumentOutOfRangeException` from `GroupDisplayElements.GetItemAt` during layout** (the grouped Raw Materials list). Fuzzing the list as `MaterialListCard` sets it up — grouped, name-sorted, `AllowDataShaping`, fed by a DynamicData `SortAndBind` with the ViewModel's merge-then-reselect refresh — crashed within 15 steps with filler rows on, and never in the identical sequence with them off. The row counts always added up (header + group display elements + filler rows); what preceded every crash was **filler rows left overflowing**: `ExpandAllGroup` (and `CollapseAllGroup`) raise no `GroupExpanded`/`GroupCollapsed` and change no records, so nothing told `GridFillerRows` the rows had grown, and frozen footer rows were left over the body until a later refresh broke row generation. Deferring the handlers (so they ran after the notification) changed nothing. **A `LayoutUpdated` check now takes away rows that no longer fit after any layout** — two numbers compared, so it's cheap on every grid. With it, 30 seeds × 70 steps of the list and 12 × 60 of an editable lines table ran with no crash and no filler row left overflowing after layout. `GridFillerRowsGroupedTests` pins the overflow (it fails on the old code); the crash itself only ever followed it.
    - **Grid tests run in a real window**: `Fixtures/OffscreenGrid` shows an `SfDataGrid` off-screen on an STA thread of its own and pumps the dispatcher, so an exception the grid throws from a dispatcher callback surfaces in the test as it would have closed the app. It needs `SYNCFUSION_LICENSE_KEY` (an unlicensed control shows a dialog) and skips without it. **These classes share one xUnit collection, `OffscreenGridCollection`, so they run one at a time** (2026-09-28). While each class was its own collection, their windows were up at once on separate STA threads, and a different current-cell or focus test failed on almost every full run, though each passed on its own.
  - **Found on the way, not caused by it:** Raw Materials' groups start collapsed although the grid has `AutoExpandGroups`. Syncfusion.Data expands a new top-level group only while grouping is being applied (`CollectionView.IsInGroup`), and the list loads empty, its records arriving later through `LoadCommand`.
  - **Verified in the app** on the secondary monitor against the dev database, nothing saved:
    - lists filled to the bottom with vertical lines and no scrollbar; a search down to one match filled more rows; expanding Ingredients removed them all (the list scrolled) and collapsing brought them back, with the form (New, or Cake Flour) untouched;
    - clicking a filler row changed nothing;
    - in Sub-Assemblies' lines table, a committed line kept the add row right under it, Down from the add row stayed put, a click below started no edit, and deleting the line brought a filler row back;
    - the Products list and form, Price History, and the Settings dialog's lists (opened twice, padded once) were padded; the recipe Total row's dividers lined up;
    - shrinking the window showed no filler rows over the data even 60 ms later, and maximizing refilled.
- **A tab keeps its view state while the app runs, and still picks up changes made on other tabs.** Nothing is saved to disk; the state lasts for the session only.
  - **Tab content stays loaded.** `MainTabControl` has `IsDisableUnloadTabItemExtContent="True"`. By default `TabControlExt` unloads a tab's content on every switch; measured off-screen, content was unloaded twice and re-loaded three times over four switches, and never unloaded with the property set. So scroll position, sorting, sub-tabs, expanders, focus and typed text survive a switch without any code.
  - **One load trigger: tab activation.** Views don't load data on `Loaded`. `MainWindow.xaml.cs` maps each `TabItemExt` to its screen's `LoadCommand` and runs it for the tab shown at startup and on each `MainTabControl.SelectionChanged`. The Print tab's is a command of the window's own, which lays out the product Products has open (see the Products screen's Print). It ignores bubbled events whose `OriginalSource` isn't `MainTabControl`, and skips a tab whose refresh is still running.
  - **Every `LoadCommand` is a fetch-then-merge, never a clear-and-reload.** Clearing a list is what used to lose the selected row, picker choices and Products' unsaved rows on every return.
    1. All `await`s finish first.
    2. The ids of the selected row, every picker and the lookup-panel selection are recorded. A ComboBoxAdv can clear its own `SelectedItem` when its item is replaced mid-merge, so the ids must be read before merging.
    3. Inside `ApplyRefresh` (`ViewModelBase`, which sets `IsRefreshing`), the lists are merged with `CollectionMerge.MergeFrom`, and each selection and picker is re-pointed by id.
  - **`CollectionMerge`** (`src/TinapayanRMS.UI/ViewModels/CollectionMerge.cs`) keeps the existing object for every row whose values didn't change, which is what a grid's selection and scroll and a picker's `SelectedItem` are tied to. A `SourceCache` merge uses DynamicData's `EditDiff`; an `ObservableCollection` is synced in place. `HasSameValues` compares scalar properties plus those of referenced entities one level deep (so a renamed category counts as a change to an ingredient showing it). A collection the row owns (a raw material's `Units`) counts as part of the row: its elements are compared in order, the same way as the row, so an added unit — or a unit of measure renamed in Settings — replaces the row. A screen whose selected row is replaced that way rebuilds the unit rows and pickers derived from it, keeping picks by id.
  - **Selection-driven subscriptions skip while `IsRefreshing`**: form loads, "reset the detail fields" blocks, detail reloads, lookup-panel form loads, and Products' role and packaging-material pickers. Re-pointing a selection at its refreshed copy must not reload the form or throw away unsaved edits. `ReportsViewModel` isn't a `ViewModelBase` and has no selection to re-point: its Load replaces both summaries outright.
  - **Products** also rebuilds its unsaved usage and packaging rows with the refreshed sub-assemblies, materials and units (`RefreshUnsavedRows`), keeping each row's numbers, so costs changed on other tabs reach the recipe sections and the cost summary. Since 2026-09-14 it also refetches the lines of every sub-assembly the recipe uses (`GetForSubAssembliesAsync`, one query), and rebuilds only a row whose sub-assembly or lines changed (`CollectionMerge.HasSameValues`). It also refreshes the selected product's tab links.
    - **Since sub-assemblies became owned (2026-09-14)** it's `RefreshRecipeRows`: a section of the product's own keeps its unsaved lines and is only repriced with the refreshed ingredients; a linked section is refetched (`ISubAssemblyRepository.GetByIdsAsync` and its lines, one query each), since its owner may have changed its lines, name or role there; each keeps its own piece size, and the sections are re-sorted if a role or name moved one. Who links the product's own sub-assemblies ("Also used by …") and its packaging link are refreshed too.
  - **Save, Delete and the lookup panels call the same `LoadCommand`**, so an existing record stays selected after saving, and renaming a category or role updates the grid.
  - Covered by `tests/TinapayanRMS.UI.Tests/ViewModels/`: `CollectionMergeTests`, plus Ingredients and Products refresh tests that build the ViewModel on real repositories.
  - **Back and Forward between screens** (2026-09-23, at the user's request).
    - **What they do.** ← → buttons in the header, after the menu toggle, and Alt+Left / Alt+Right.
    - **What a step is.** A change of screen, however it happens: the menu, the tab strip, a tab closed, or a link from one screen to another (a Used By tile, a linked badge). Picking another record in a list isn't a step. The user chose this over "every record opened" and "screens only".
    - **Where it lives.** `NavigationHistory` (`ViewModels/NavigationHistory.cs`, on `MainWindowViewModel.Navigation`) holds the back and forward stacks of `ScreenVisit(AppScreen, RecordId)`, capped at 50. It knows nothing of tabs.
    - **Recording.** `MainWindow.MainTabControl_SelectionChanged` records each step, with the record the screen had open *as it was left*. Each tab maps to a `HistoryScreen`: the selected product, sub-assembly or raw material id; the screen's `Open*` method (`OpenProduct`, `OpenSubAssembly`, `OpenRawMaterial`); and its form's `History.IsDirty`. Reports has no record.
    - **Restoring.** Back reopens that record through the screen's `Open*` method before showing the tab, as the links do, **unless the screen's form holds unsaved changes**. Then the screen shows as it is, so Back never throws away typed work. `_isRestoringHistory` keeps the move from being recorded as a new step.
    - **The keys.** They're on the window's `PreviewKeyDown`, so no grid or box takes them first. With Alt held, WPF reports the key as `Key.System`, so the handler checks `SystemKey`.
    - **The lists scroll to a record opened this way.** `SubAssembliesView` watches its grid's `SelectedItem`, and `ProductListCard` and `MaterialListCard` their tree's, which also opens the folders above the record (`BringIntoView` with `canExpand`).
  - **Fixed along the way: Products listed a role's usages twice.** `LoadProductDetailAsync` set `EditingUsageRole`, whose subscription loads the usages, then also awaited its own load. Whenever the role actually changed (the first product picked each session, for example), both loads ran side by side and each added every row, which double-counted those costs in the summary. It now loads directly only when the role didn't change, and `LoadUsagesForRoleAsync` recomputes the summary itself. (The per-role loading itself went in the 2026-09-14 rebuild: every role's usages now load together, with the product.)
- **Embedded management panel for an editable child lookup** (`SubAssembliesView`, Phase 7): `SubAssemblyRole` is editable but has no screen of its own — it gets a small self-contained grid + form + New/Save/Delete panel living directly inside `SubAssembliesView`, with its own independent `SourceCache`, selection, and commands in `SubAssembliesViewModel` (not a separate tab, and not folded into the sub-assembly form itself). Use this shape for a future lookup that's editable but too small/tightly-coupled to its owning screen to deserve a top-level tab. **Since Phase 16 it sits inside a collapsed native `Expander`** (`IsExpanded="False"`) at the bottom of the screen's right rail, rather than an always-visible side panel — it's used rarely enough (editing categories/roles is occasional, not part of the main workflow) that keeping it collapsed by default gives the main list more room without hiding the feature. The native `Expander` is used rather than a Syncfusion accordion (`SfAccordion` would need its own package just for this), and since `ApplyThemeAsDefaultStyle` is off (see the accent-customization note above), the native `Expander` renders with the plain OS chrome rather than the theme's — functional, just not accent-styled, the same trade-off as the other native fallbacks (`CheckBox`, `ProgressBar`) noted elsewhere in this document.
  - **Superseded (2026-09-14): the Sub-Assemblies screen is gone**, since sub-assemblies are made inside the product that owns them (see the Domain Model). Roles moved to a **Roles** tab in the Settings dialog (`SubAssemblyRolesView`/`SubAssemblyRolesViewModel`: Name and Order, the same shape as the Categories tab — the form resets after Save and Delete, and a fresh one offers the next place in the order). **Since 2026-09-29 (at the user's request) the form has no Order box**: `SubAssemblyRoleRepository.AddAsync` numbers a new role one past the last whatever it's given, `UpdateAsync` leaves the order alone, and **Move Up** / **Move Down** in the list card's header (`MoveUpCommand`/`MoveDownCommand`, off for the first and last row and with nothing picked) call `MoveAsync`, which swaps the role with its neighbour. `MoveAsync` and `DeleteAsync` both number every role 1…n again (ties by name, the order the tab lists them in), so the Order column never has a gap. A move leaves the form's unsaved edits alone and keeps the role picked.
    - **The Roles tab's Product-owned checkbox** (2026-09-23): `SubAssemblyRolesViewModel.EditingIsProductOwned`, disabled for the Packaging role (`CanEditProductOwned`); a read-only `GridCheckBoxColumn` shows it in the list. It's WPF's own `CheckBox`, the app's first outside a grid: Syncfusion has none of its own outside its grids and ribbon, and its samples use WPF's, which the theme styles. Changing it on a saved role asks first (`RunWithConfirmationAsync`, `OwnershipChangeMessage`), saying how many sub-assemblies move; a rename alone doesn't ask.
  - **Since 2026-09-23 it lists every sub-assembly but products' own** (product-owned roles), which are edited in their products; the ones products saved are named after them. The Name column is 260 wide and Role 110 (210 and 160 before), the same 370 in all, with a tooltip for a name cut short.
  - **Restored (2026-09-15) for standalone sub-assemblies only** (`SubAssembliesView`/`SubAssembliesViewModel`, a tab and drawer item between Raw Materials and Products; the user chose standalone only over also listing products' own read-only). Roles stay in Settings. It follows today's conventions, not the old screen's:
    - A list card (fixed-width columns, so the Auto column fits them: Name and Role; until 2026-09-17 also Batch (g), Batch Cost and Used By, removed at the user's request, since the form shows all three for the selected one) beside one form: the title and New, Copy, Save and Delete; Name and Role beside the batch weight, batch cost and cost per g, worked out from the lines as they're typed; the lines table from the BOM card (`IngredientLineRow`, autocomplete, `GridEditCommit`, `AutoCompleteDropDown`); and Used By: one tile per linking product (2026-09-23, at the user's request; a comma-separated sentence before), in a `WrapPanel` with a fixed `ItemWidth` so the tiles line up as rows and columns (a long name wraps inside its tile, and the row's tiles share its height), over a caption saying they follow every change and block Delete. A tile looks like a file in File Explorer's icon view (an icon over the name, flat until hovered; the icon is a full-color cake, pastry or bread drawing when the product's category name says so, else the Products glyph — `ProductCategoryIconConverter`, 2026-09-23) and a click opens the product on the Products screen: `GetStandaloneLinkedByAsync` returns `LinkingProduct`s (id, name and category name), the tile runs `OpenProductCommand`, `MainWindow` subscribes to `ProductOpenRequests` and calls `ProductsViewModel.OpenProduct`, which clears a search hiding the product, or, when the list hasn't loaded it yet, leaves it for the load that showing the tab starts. `ProductListCard` scrolls the list to a product selected this way. The request is a `Subject`, not the command's own results, because those arrive on the main-thread scheduler, which never runs in the tests.
    - **One Save** (`SaveStandaloneAsync`): `SubAssemblyValidator`, then `IngredientLineRow.CompleteLines` and at least one line; then `LoadCommand`, the saved row re-selected inside `ApplyRefresh` and its form reloaded directly, and a success toast. Delete asks first and shows the refusal naming the linking products.
    - **Copy** (2026-09-27, at the user's request: "a copy command button to SubAssemblies, where i can copy the subassembly into a new subassembly"), between New and Save, enabled while one is picked. It turns the form into a new, unsaved sub-assembly holding what it shows, unsaved edits included: the same role and lines (or a set's materials), named "<name> Copy" (`SubAssemblyRules.UniqueStandaloneName`, so "Copy 2" and on when taken), titled "Copy of <name>", nothing selected in the list and Used By empty. Like New it's a fresh history (nothing to undo), measured against a blank form, so Save is offered at once and adds it beside the original, which is left as last saved. `SubAssembliesViewModel.CopyForm` reuses `CaptureSnapshot`/`RestoreSnapshot`. Covered by three `SubAssembliesViewModelRefreshTests`.
    - Rows are `StandaloneSubAssemblyRow`s: "Used by" is a text property (`UsedByText`), with the names behind a method, because `CollectionMerge.HasSameValues` compares a list of strings by each string's scalar properties, which is only `Length`.
    - `LoadCommand` merges roles, ingredients and rows, keeps unsaved lines (`IngredientLineRow.Resolve` reprices them), and re-points the selection by id. A form load counts itself (`_formLoadVersion`), so one overtaken by another pick or New drops its lines rather than adding them twice.
    - Covered by `StandaloneSubAssemblyPersistenceTests`, `SubAssembliesViewModelRefreshTests` and a `ProductsViewModelRefreshTests` test that links one.
