But `TabControlExt` coerces `AllowDragDrop` to true whenever `ItemsSource` is bound, unless it's set False (`OnCoerceAllowDragDrop`), and a tab dropped on another is removed from the bound list and inserted at the drop (`TabLayoutPanel.MoveItem`; both read from the decompiled 34.2.7). That took `RecipeTabs` out of role order and out of step with `UsageEdits`, which `MirrorUsageTabs` mirrors index for index, so later edits could land on the wrong tab. `AllowDragDrop="False"` stops a drag where both of its starts check it (`OnPreviewMouseLeftButtonDown`, `OnMouseMove`); the scroll arrows don't depend on it, only the drag's own auto-scroll buttons (`EnableAutoScroll`, off). Verified in the app: dragging Filling onto Topping left the tabs where they were.
        - **The dev database's Almond Cheese Bread was in order for the roles it has**: its "Topping" sub-assembly has the Dough role (the add form's Role starts on the first role), so it ties with the linked Variety Bread Dough and sorts first by name.
        - **Add Sub-Assembly** inserts `AddSubAssemblyTab`, now headed "New Sub-Assembly", before Packaging and shows it. The form showing is applied first, as leaving its tab would; one a rule refuses keeps its tab, and nothing opens. Since 2026-09-20 it is a drop-down of the three ways in and stays offered while New Sub-Assembly shows, since that is the only place the way can be changed: another item over an add form nothing was entered in (`IsUsageFormUntouched`) only changes its way, keeping the tab, while over one already typed into it applies what's there and starts another, as a second Add Sub-Assembly always did. That tab goes once another shows: after Save, or a click on another tab (what was typed is applied; nothing typed, the form just closes). A new product starts on Packaging, its only tab.
          - **Taking the tab away has to wait for the tab strip.** This screen's own `WhenAnyValue` hears of a `SelectedRecipeTab` change before the strip's binding does, so taking the tab out of `RecipeTabs` there, on a pick made by the screen, took away the tab the strip still showed, and the strip picked a neighbour of its own (Packaging, not the Filling picked; reproduced off-screen with a real `TabControlExt`). `SelectRecipeTab` sets `_selectingRecipeTab` while its pick is on its way, and its second `RecipeTabShown` takes the tab away; a click, which the strip has already made, takes it away at once (`RemoveAddSubAssemblyTab`, with the strip's pick ignored under `_arrangingRecipeTabs`).
          - **Another product's recipe opens on its first tab.** The permanent Add tab used to take the strip's pick as `ClearDetails` removed the old sub-assemblies, and "else the first sub-assembly's" followed; without it the strip lands on Packaging, and "Packaging if that was showing" would have opened every product there. `ClearDetails` now sets `_isRecipeClearedForAnother`, and `ReplaceUsages` reselects with no previous tab. A new product's first Save isn't another product's recipe: `ReloadSavedProductAsync` sets `_detailProductId` before selecting it, so it keeps its tab as any reload does.
        - **Delete Sub-Assembly** (`RemoveUsageCommand`, parameterless now) takes out the sub-assembly whose tab shows, and is only offered on one; Remove left the form's button row. Still nothing to confirm: the sub-assembly goes when the product is saved (Save deletes the product's own; a linked one stays with its owner), and one others link is refused on the spot.
        - **Show Add-Ins** sits under a linked recipe, beside the caption saying whose its lines are (`IsFormOfferingAddIns`), and opens the add-ins table (`IsFormAddInsExpanded`, `ToggleFormAddInsCommand`): "Hide Add-Ins" while it's open, "Show Add-Ins (2)" while two are out of sight, with a chevron that points as `ExpandChevronStyle`'s does. A link's tab opens with the table open only when it has add-ins, and a refused add-in opens it (`CompleteAddIns`), so the line the message is about is in sight. Add-ins out of sight still count in the batch. The Linked Recipe heading binds `IsFormOfferingAddIns`, so it doesn't come and go with the table.
          - **Superseded 2026-09-28: the add-ins table always shows, and a badge says whether there are any** (at the user's request: "I had an accessibility issue for SubAssemblies having the Hide/Show Add-Ins … I need a badge that declare if there is an add-in or none … clicking that badge will scroll me down to the add ins datagrid", then "Remove the show/hide add-in control … always show the add-in datagrid"). A shut table hid that the batch had extras in it. Show/Hide Add-Ins, `IsFormAddInsExpanded` (and its `ProductFormSnapshot` field), `ToggleFormAddInsCommand`, `FormAddInsToggleText` and its chevron template are gone, and so is `CompleteAddIns`' opening of the table on a refusal. `IsFormShowingAddIns` (formerly `IsFormOfferingAddIns`, which it replaces) is the linked recipe's alone, and the table has an "Add-Ins" heading of its own in the toggle's place. A badge sits level with the linked badge, rather than on a row of its own over the lines, to save the height, and aligned on the top right corner of the lines table (two follow-ups the same day, at the user's request): a background-less layer over that row with the tables' columns, the piece-size column's width shared through `SharedSizeGroup="PieceSizeColumn"` (scope on the form's root StackPanel), so the badge's right edge is the table's while Unlink and Copy keep the far right (`FormAddInsBadgeText`: "No add-ins", "1 add-in", "3 add-ins"; `FormAddInCount` counts lines with an ingredient picked, so the add row doesn't count). With add-ins it's a `LinkedBadgeLinkStyle` button whose click (`UsageFormPanel.AddInsBadge_Click`) focuses the add-ins grid and brings the add-ins section into view in RecipeStructureCard's ScrollViewer; `FocusScrolling.SkipOnClick` doesn't stop it, since a Button's Click fires on release. With none it's a plain grey tag. Out of the add-ins-only row, each piece carries the linked-recipe condition itself (`HasFormAddIns`, `IsFormShowingNoAddIns`, both false unless `IsFormShowingAddIns`). The add-ins grid also gained the lines grid's Cost/g column, its figure columns all 96 px as the lines grid's are, so the two tables line up (same follow-up, at the user's request). The way back, also that day at the user's request: a **Go to top** `LinkedBadgeLinkStyle` badge right-aligned in the Add-Ins heading row, over the add-ins table's top right corner; its click (`GoToTopBadge_Click`) focuses the lines grid and brings the form's top left corner into view (`FormRoot.BringIntoView(new Rect(0, 0, 1, 1))`), which scrolls the tab to its top so the badges over the lines show too.
        - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor against the dev database, nothing saved: a new product showed only Packaging, with Delete Sub-Assembly greyed out; Almond Cheese Bread showed Topping, Variety Bread Dough, Filling and Packaging; Add Sub-Assembly put New Sub-Assembly before Packaging, both buttons greyed out, and a click on Variety Bread Dough took it away; Show Add-Ins opened the table and read Hide Add-Ins; the drag above moved nothing; Delete Sub-Assembly on Filling showed Variety Bread Dough and took the material cost from ₱67.1820 to ₱44.9985; Cheese Roll then opened on its Dough, and Almond Cheese Bread reopened on Topping with Filling back. ViewModel tests: New Sub-Assembly's tab through a click away, Apply and Cancel, Show Add-Ins including a refused add-in, and a product picked from a sub-assembly's tab (the strip's pick simulated), with the tab tests updated.
      - **Tabs are called by their roles** (2026-09-17, at the user's request; the domain side is under SubAssembly above), **and by their names too where a role repeats** (2026-09-18). Every sub-assembly tab's header, linked ones too (the user's choice), is `ProductUsageEdit.SectionName`: its `RoleName`, or "Filling: Ube" while `IsRoleRepeated` (its `Label` beside the role, when it has one). So are the form's heading and title, the Recipe Cost Overview's slices and the Unit Conversion Overview's batch ("Filling: Ube batch"). `ProductUsageEdit` is a `ReactiveObject` for that flag alone: `MarkRepeatedRoles` sets it on every section whenever `UsageEdits` changes (after `MirrorUsageTabs`), and refreshes the tabs' headers (`SubAssemblyRecipeTab.RefreshHeader`). `Name` is a product's sub-assembly's own name (this product's, or the owner's for a linked one); a linked standalone one keeps its name in `StandaloneName`, which its badge reads ("Variety Bread Dough, from Sub-Assemblies"), and `DisplayName` gives it where the recipe isn't plain (the add-ins caption and messages).
        - **The form has Role and an optional Name** (Name since 2026-09-18, gone from 2026-09-17), side by side under the add form's pickers (`EditingUsageRole`, `EditingUsageName`); packaging has no name. Role offers every recipe role (`FormRoleOptions`, `RefreshFormRoleOptions`, refreshed with `RefreshLinkableOptions`, which puts the pick back since the picker can drop it as its list changes), and the add form starts on the first role the recipe doesn't have yet, else the first, so sections can be added in role order without picking; a copy takes its source's role and name. Apply refuses sections it can't tell apart (`EnsureSectionsDistinct` in the view model, before `SetRecipeAsync`'s own check) — but only for a section that changed, and never for a linked one being edited, whose role and name are its owner's. A name typed alone counts as an entry, so leaving the add form with one applies it.
        - **Order and reselection**: `RecipeOrder` breaks a tie in role by saved id, a new one last; `IsSameSubAssembly` finds an unsaved own section after a reload by its role and name. Make a Copy keeps what the section went by (its `Label` while its role repeats), and the link picker leaves out only the sub-assemblies the recipe already links.
        - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor against the migrated dev database, nothing saved: Almond Cheese Bread showed Dough (linked, "Variety Bread Dough, from Sub-Assemblies"), Dough, Filling and Packaging — its old Topping is saved in the Dough role — with Dough, Dough and Filling slices and two "Dough batch" boxes; the own Dough's form had only Role, whose list was Dough, Topping, Inclusion and Finishing; Add Sub-Assembly's form had no Name and started on Topping. ViewModel tests: the tab headers, the role offered to an add and an edit, a role the recipe has refused on Apply and on leaving a tab, and a link in a taken role not offered. Persistence tests: one per role across own and linked, an old name cleared on save, a role change refused where a linking product has it (owned and standalone), and a clone of a source with two in one role refused.
    - **Recipe lines are typed into the table** (2026-09-14, at the user's request), in the sub-assembly form and in Raw Materials' Bill of Materials card alike. Until then each had an Ingredient (or Component) picker, a Grams box and Add Line under a read-only grid.
      - The grid is an editable `SfDataGrid`: `AddNewRowPosition="Bottom"`, `EditTrigger="OnTap"`, `AllowDeleting` (the Delete key removes the selected line; Remove Line stays under it), a `GridMultiColumnDropDownList` on `IngredientId` and a `NumericColumn` on `Grams`.
      - **The add row is a plain empty row, with no "Click here to add an ingredient"** (2026-09-16, at the user's request, on every grid). The Windows11Light `AddNewRowControl` template covers the row, until it's edited, with a light-gray band (`PART_AddNewRowTextBorder`) holding `AddNewRowText`, and an empty `AddNewRowText` only brings back the localized "Click here to add new row" (`SfDataGrid.OnAddNewRowTextChanged`). So `App`'s static constructor registers a class handler on `AddNewRowControl.Loaded` (`HideAddNewRowText`) that collapses the band; the grids set no `AddNewRowText`. The band's `Edit` visual state collapses it too, so the local value holds either way. The row then looks like the filler rows under it, with column lines, and clicking it still starts a line (the filler rows stay inert). The user chose this over keeping an empty gray band. Since the band no longer covers the row, the grid's current-cell outline shows on it when the current cell is there (after Enter commits a line). Verified in the app on the secondary monitor: Sub-Assemblies' lines table and Butter Icing, Special Ensaymada's Bill of Materials showed the add row as an empty row with column lines; clicking it began a line ("salt", Tab, 5, Enter committed Salt, Fine at 0.08, not saved), and the next add row was plain too.
      - **The Base column is the app's first `GridCheckBoxColumn`** (2026-09-16, baker's percentage), the last column (moved there from between Ingredient and Grams on 2026-09-17, at the user's request), with a read-only `NumericColumn` "Baker's %" (1 decimal) after Grams. It's on Sub-Assemblies' lines table and the Products form's lines table; the add-ins table has the percentage but no tick. On Products its `AllowEditing` binds `IsFormEditingLines`, the same as the grid's, so a linked recipe shows its owner's tick greyed out. **One click toggles it** (with `EditTrigger="OnTap"`; verified in the app on the secondary monitor: ticking Water beside the flour moved the base to 1,450 g and every percentage with it, flour and water reading 69.0 and 31.0), and `IngredientLineRow.ApplyFormulaBase` pushes the new base to every row as the tick, the grams or the lines change. **The add row shows no tick** (2026-09-16, at the user's request: "hide the blank tick box"), so it stays the plain empty row the other grids show: `controls:GridAddNewRow.HideCheckBoxes` swaps the grid's `"CheckBox"` renderer for `AddRowHiddenCheckBoxRenderer`, which hides the checkbox where `DataGrid.IsAddNewIndex` and shows it everywhere else — the way the renderer's own doc comment shows it being replaced. `OnUpdateEditBinding` just calls `OnInitializeEditElement`, so the one override covers rows as they recycle. A new line gets its tick once it's committed. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor, with the typing both ways above: the add row showed no box; typing 65 into Water's Baker's % made it 650 g and bold; doubling the flour to 2,000 g took Water to 1,300 g at 65% while every line typed in grams kept them (Washed Sugar stayed 250 g, 25.0 to 12.5); typing into the flour's own percentage did nothing. None of it was saved.
      - **The ingredient cell autocompletes** (at the user's request, replacing a `GridComboBoxColumn` the same day; Syncfusion's "Auto-complete support" for this column, as in its `GridDropDownAndReadOnlyColumnsDemo.xaml`). `DisplayMember="Name"`, `ValueMember="Id"`, `ItemsSource="{Binding Ingredients}"` (a column takes it from the grid's DataContext). `AllowAutoComplete` picks an ingredient as it's typed, and `AllowIncrementalFiltering` narrows the dropdown to the matches. Since 2026-09-15 the dropdown opens as you type and lists the names that contain the text, not only those that start with it (see "Names that contain the typed text" below). Text that matches nothing clears the cell when you leave it.
      - Its dropdown is a small grid of Name, Category and Cost per g (the buffered cost, 4 decimals), styled by the Windows11Light theme (`SfMultiColumnDropDownControl`). `PopUpMinWidth` and `PopUpWidth` alone were ignored, and the popup took the cell's width, clipping the cost. It needs `IsAutoPopupSize="False"` with `PopUpWidth="380"`/`PopUpHeight="260"`, plus `GridColumnSizer="Star"` so Name takes what the fixed-width columns leave.
      - Keyboard entry: click the add row once, type part of a name (the dropdown opens), Tab to Grams, Enter commits the line and moves to a new add row. With the dropdown open, Enter picks the highlighted row and closes it, and the next Enter commits the line.
      - **Three bugs in Syncfusion's drop-down cell, fixed by `controls:AutoCompleteDropDown.IsEnabled="True"` on the grid plus `AllowNullInput="True"` on the column** (`Controls/AutoCompleteDropDown.cs`, reported by the user on first use, the causes read from the decompiled `SfMultiColumnDropDownControl` and `GridCellMultiColumnDropDownRenderer` 34.2.7). The attached property swaps the grid's `"MultiColumnDropDown"` cell renderer for `AutoCompleteDropDownRenderer`.
        - **A new row started as the previous row's ingredient.** One editor serves every row of the column. With `AllowNullInput` off (the default), an empty text box, and a commit whose text doesn't match, both fall back to the control's `PreviousSelectedItem`: the last row's pick. Its text box also kept what was last typed, because the text is only cleared when the stale `SearchText` is empty too. `AllowNullInput="True"` (`IngredientId` is nullable) stops the fallback, and the renderer's `OnInitializeEditElement` sets the editor's text to the row's own ingredient, or nothing, as each edit begins.
        - **A name typed and tabbed out of at once wasn't picked.** Autocomplete runs `FilterDelay` (500 ms by default) after a keystroke, and a quick Tab committed before it ran, leaving the line with no ingredient. The renderer's editor has `FilterDelay = 0`.
        - **The pop-up covered the cell being typed into.** The control places it in `ProcessOnPopupPositionCalculation`, measuring the room below against `SystemParameters.VirtualScreenHeight` (the whole desktop's height, not the cell's monitor). On the 768 px secondary monitor beside a 1080 px primary it opened below regardless, and Windows pushed it back up over the cell. `ScreenAwareDropDownControl` overrides that method: it takes the monitor's work area (`MonitorFromPoint`/`GetMonitorInfoW`) and opens below when the pop-up fits, above otherwise, using the pop-up's drawn height (its border and resize grip make it about 40 px taller than `PopUpHeight`, which the control's own placement also missed). The renderer creates it in `OnCreateEditUIElement`, repeating the base renderers' setup (no error template, `WantsMouseInput`, a 1 px border).
        - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor with real keystrokes: "fir" + Tab straight away picked First Class Flour; clicking the next row opened it empty, and "u" + Tab picked Unsalted Butter; clicking an existing row showed its own ingredient; F4 on a row near the bottom opened the list entirely above the cell; the BOM card did the same, and saved Cake Flour (typed "cak" + Tab) and Unsalted Butter.
      - **Names that contain the typed text, in a dropdown that opens as you type** (2026-09-15, at the user's request: like `SfTextBoxExt` with `SuggestionMode="Contains"`; the first key typed didn't open the list, and only names starting with the text were found). All three columns set `SearchCondition="Contains"`, and the renderer's editor sets `AllowImmediatePopup`, which opens the dropdown on the key press (the column has no property for it, so the renderer sets it when it creates the editor, like `FilterDelay`).
        - **`SearchCondition` only ever filtered the dropdown's rows.** The pick as you type is a separate path (the control's `ProcessTextChanged`, read from the decompiled `SfMultiColumnDropDownControl` 34.2.7): it takes the first item for which `ProcessAppendText` is true, a hard-coded `StartsWith`, then writes that item's name into the box through `ProcessAppendStringList` and selects the part not yet typed. `ScreenAwareDropDownControl` overrides both, only when the condition is `Contains`.
        - **Which name is picked** (the user's choice, over always the list's top row): the first name starting with the text, or, when none does, the first containing it. "ss" lists First Class Flour, SSL and Third Class Flour and picks SSL; "flour" picks All Purpose Flour. `ProcessAppendText` is asked of each name in list order, so it asks once per text change whether any name starts with the text (reading `DisplayMember` itself, since the control's own lookup is private).
        - **The box keeps what was typed.** A name the text sits inside can't be completed in place, so `ProcessAppendStringList` hands back the typed text (`SearchText`) instead of the name.
        - **Leaving the cell keeps the pick.** `AllowAutoComplete` stays on, so `SelectedItem` follows the typing and Tab commits it. But leaving the cell runs `CommitValue(false)`, which keeps a pick only when the box holds its whole name, and would have cleared a pick made from "ss". The override commits it as Tab does.
        - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor with real keystrokes, on the Sub-Assemblies form against the dev database (nothing saved): "s" opened the dropdown at once, listing the names containing it with Salt, Fine highlighted; "ss" narrowed it to First Class Flour, SSL and Third Class Flour, with SSL highlighted and "ss" still in the box; focus leaving the app then committed the line as SSL. On a new form, "flour" listed the four flours with All Purpose Flour highlighted, Tab took it to Grams on the line, and 10 + Enter committed it at 0.52. Not yet checked in the app: Down + Enter, clicking another cell, "zzz", F4, and the Bill of Materials card and product form (same renderer and column settings).
      - **The key that starts an edit is typed into the box** (2026-09-19, reported by the user: on the Ingredient cell, reached by keyboard, a letter had to be typed twice before it showed). A key typed on a cell that isn't being edited starts the edit, and the grid keeps its character in `PreviewInputText` (`GridCellRendererBase.OnPreviewTextInput`), since the editor doesn't exist yet to take it. `GridCellTextBoxRenderer.OnEditElementLoaded` writes it into its box; `GridCellMultiColumnDropDownRenderer`'s (decompiled 34.2.7) only focuses the box and sets it to null, so the first key was lost. `AutoCompleteDropDownRenderer.OnEditElementLoaded` now takes it before the base drops it and, once the editor has finished loading (`Dispatcher.BeginInvoke` at `Input`; typed during `Loaded`, it showed but neither filtered nor picked), selects the box's text (so it replaces the row's name, as a text cell's does), opens the list (`IsDropDownOpen`, as the control's own key-down does for `AllowImmediatePopup`) and types it in as a `TextComposition`, so it filters and picks like any key. The box is reached through the template's `PART_TextBox` (`ScreenAwareDropDownControl.TypingBox`), since the control's `Editor` is internal. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor on a new sub-assembly (nothing saved): Grams on the add row, Esc, Left onto Ingredient, one "a": the box read "a", the list opened on the names containing it and All Purpose Flour was picked (₱0.0519 a gram). Before the fix the same "a" only opened the editor.
      - Both screens' rows are `IngredientLineRow` (`ViewModels/IngredientLineRow.cs`), a `ReactiveObject` with a parameterless constructor for the add row. It replaced `SubAssemblyLineEdit` in `FormLines` (applying copies the rows into `SubAssemblyLineEdit`s, so editing the form never touches a section's lines) and `IngredientBomLineEdit` in `BomLineEdits`. The picker sets `IngredientId`, and a DynamicData `AutoRefresh` subscription on the collection points `Ingredient` at the screen's copy (`IngredientLineRow.Resolve`), which prices the row. It maps by id rather than by object because BOM lines load their component from a separate query, and a refresh swaps in new ingredient copies. On the Products form, the same subscription recomputes the batch weight, the other piece-size figure and the preview.
      - **A line being typed into the add row prices itself** (2026-09-15, at the user's request: its Cost read 0.00 until the next row). The add row's item isn't in the screen's collection until it's committed, so the `AutoRefresh` subscription never reached it. `controls:GridAddNewRow.ItemFactory` (`Controls/GridAddNewRow.cs`) hands the grid's `AddNewRowInitiating` the screen's own item (`e.NewObject`, settable in 34.2.7, confirmed by reflection): `SubAssembliesViewModel.NewLine` and `ProductsViewModel.NewFormLine` make an `IngredientLineRow` with a lookup, which finds its ingredient when `IngredientId` is set. The Cost cell updates as soon as the grams are entered. Verified in the app on both tables ("Corn" + Tab, 50 + Tab: 2.41 while still in the add row). The batch totals still count committed lines only. The BOM card has no Cost column, so it's unchanged.
      - `IngredientLineRow.CompleteLines` checks the rows on Apply (and on Save for the BOM). It leaves out a blank row, and refuses grams with no ingredient, an ingredient with no grams, or an ingredient on two rows, each with a message naming it ("Enter how many grams of Cake Flour go into Half Filled, or delete its line.").
      - **`controls:GridEditCommit.OnFocusLeave`** (`Controls/GridEditCommit.cs`): a cell or add row being typed into only reaches its row when the edit ends, and the grid doesn't end it when the user clicks away. So a line typed just before Apply or Save would have been missed. When keyboard focus leaves the grid it ends the edit, then commits the add row through the grid's `GetAddNewRowController()`, or cancels it when every column still has a new row's value (otherwise an add row the user only clicked became an empty line). Three things came out of checking it in the app. Focus in a cell's dropdown counts as inside the grid (the dropdown is a `Popup`, followed back through its `PlacementTarget`; without that, opening the dropdown ended the edit and closed it). Ending the add row takes focus with it, so focus is handed back to the element that was clicked (the first click on Weight per Piece was lost otherwise). `ButtonAdv` takes focus, so Apply and Save both commit first.
      - **Leaving the add row for another line drops it when nothing was typed in it** (2026-09-16, reported by the user: clicking the empty row under the lines and back onto a line added an empty line). `GridEditCommit` only covers focus leaving the grid. Inside it, the grid commits the add row whenever the current cell moves off it, whatever it holds: a click on another line (`GridCurrentCellManager.HandlePointerOperation`), an arrow key or Enter (`GridSelectionController`'s key handling), all through the selection controller's `CommitAddNew` (read from the decompiled 34.2.7). **`controls:GridAddNewRow.DropWhenUntouched="True"`** (`Controls/GridAddNewRow.cs`) replaces the grid's `SelectionController` with `UntouchedAddRowSelectionController`, a `GridSelectionController` whose `CommitAddNew` cancels an untouched row (`GetAddNewRowController().CancelAddNew()`, as the grid's own Esc does) and commits any other. "Untouched" is the same test as `GridEditCommit`'s, now shared as `GridAddNewRow.IsUntouched`. The grid makes its controller only in its constructor (or when `SelectionUnit` changes, which these grids never set), so the replacement stays. A row with anything in it is still added when left, as on Apply and Save: the user asked for the addition to be cancelled when the row is left, reported for an empty row. On all three tables. Verified in the app on the secondary monitor on a new, unsaved Sub-Assemblies form: after a Salt, Fine line, clicking the empty row and back onto the line left one line; Water picked in the add row and left with a click on Salt was added; Enter on an untouched add row added nothing.
      - **An add row still open when the screen changes the grid's lines itself is dropped, typed into or not** (2026-09-28, from the log of a crash on Products' Save). `GridSelectionController.ProcessSourceCollectionChanged` commits an open add row whenever the bound collection changes, which adds to the collection from inside its own `CollectionChanged`. `ObservableCollection` throws "Cannot change ObservableCollection during a CollectionChanged event" for that once it has a second listener, as the form's lines do (`ProductsViewModel` mirrors `UsageEdits` into them). Save's reload refilled them (`RefreshRecipeRows` → `MirrorUsageTabs` → `ShowFormLines`) under an open add row, and the app closed. There's no committing the row from inside that event, so `UntouchedAddRowSelectionController` overrides `ProcessSourceCollectionChanged` to end the cell's edit without committing it and cancel the row first, as Esc does. The grid's own commit reaches that method only after the row has closed, so it's unaffected. `GridAddNewRowSourceChangeTests` reproduce the crash in an `OffscreenGrid`. How the add row was still open at Save wasn't found: clicking Save takes focus, which `GridEditCommit` answers.
      - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor, against a migrated copy of `TinapayanRMS_Demo`, with real clicks and keystrokes. A new product's sub-assembly was entered by keyboard (First Class Flour 1,000 g at ₱40.90, Unsalted Butter 150 g left uncommitted), a weight per piece of 50 typed with one click, then Apply and Save: 1,150 g, ₱155.65, ₱6.7674 a piece. Editing it loaded both lines by name. Delete removed the butter, 1,200 g typed over the flour was picked up by a direct click on Apply, and Save stored one 1,200 g line. The half-filled line above was refused. A new made ingredient's BOM line (Cake Flour, 500 g, typed and saved straight from the cell) saved and reloaded at ₱0.0460 a gram. After the switch to autocomplete, again: "fir" completed to First Class Flour and "u" with the dropdown open filtered it to Unsalted Butter (both lines committed, ₱40.90 and ₱114.75), "zzz" cleared on Tab, and a BOM line typed as "Unsa" saved as Unsalted Butter, 250 g, at ₱0.7500 a gram.
      - **Syncfusion's classic `GridControl` (`Syncfusion.Grid.WPF`) was tried first, at the user's request, and dropped.** It's a cell-indexed, spreadsheet-style grid: rows come from `Model[row, col]` or `QueryCellInfo`/`CommitCellInfo` handlers, not an `ItemsSource`, so every line read and write would be code-behind. More decisively, the Windows11Light theme package has no style for it (it covers `SfDataGrid`, `SfTreeGrid` and the rest; the Sample Browser only ever shows GridControl's demos in its default theme). A scratch render beside an `SfDataGrid` showed the Excel look: gray headers and row-header column, dense rows, hard borders. The user chose `SfDataGrid` after seeing it.
  - **The compact layout** (2026-09-15, at the user's request: "limit the use of card border, and use StackPanel and Grid in positioning"). Only the two regions are bordered; the sections inside them (Recipe Structure, the side column's three, Costing's three, Packaging Link, Clone) lost their `CardStyle` boxes and are set apart by `SectionDividerStyle` lines (1 px, `CardBorderBrush`, 12 px either side) and, side by side, a 1 px column divider in the gap. Their titles use `PanelHeaderStyle` (15 px, 8 px under), in `App.xaml` beside `CardStyle`, which Raw Materials still uses unchanged. The Products views position with `Grid` and `StackPanel` only: the `DockPanel`s (list, header, section headers, form button rows, banners, breakdown rows) and the Costing `UniformGrid` were converted. Inside a tab, a section's header lost its glyph tile (the tab shows the glyph), and the sub-assembly form its grey box (the tab shows only the form); the packaging form keeps its box, above the packaging table. Spacing went from 16 to 8–12 px throughout. The recipe's tab strip starts about 150 px higher than before (with the header from the user's mockup, below), and the sub-assembly form needs little or no scrolling in a maximized 1080 p window.
    - Verified in the app on the secondary monitor against a migrated copy of `TinapayanRMS_Demo`: Cream Loaf's Recipe, Costing and Links & Clone tabs maximized, and at 1,100 px wide (the header's figures and the side column both stacked, nothing cut off); the Edit form in its tab; the Packaging tab, own and linked (badge, Add Packaging disabled, Edit and Remove hidden); the packaging link banner; a usage example worked out.
  - **Costing:** the five inputs are tiles (a coloured edge beside each, Latte Peach, Yellow, Green, Teal and Sky down the column since 2026-09-25, ending on the accent (Sapphire and Mauve for the first hours; Amber 400 to 800 from 2026-09-24; teal, amber, violet, rose and sky before), no box, one under another in `CostingCard`'s left column since 2026-09-19; a five-column row before), all percentages (Labor %, Overhead % and Administrative % since 2026-09-15, **of the sub-assemblies (ingredients) cost since 2026-09-20**; Markup % and VAT %), each showing what it comes to in ₱ per piece and its share of the selling price ("₱23.7729 · 11.9% of the price"). The breakdown lists sub-assemblies (then each sub-assembly in recipe order since 2026-09-25, indented, smaller and muted under their total, named as its recipe tab is: `ProductCostSummary.SubAssemblyLines`), packaging, labor, overhead and administrative (each labelled with its percentage, as in "Labor (25% of sub-assemblies)"), the subtotal (the base cost, highlighted), markup and VAT, then the total selling price, each with ₱ per piece and % of the price. It all comes from one `ProductCostSummary`, worked out from the form as it's edited, so unsaved changes show everywhere at once.
   - **The four metrics: a percentage box and a value box each** (2026-09-28, at the user's request: "Labor %, Overhead %, Administrative %, and Markup % will be renamed to Labor Metric, Overhead Metric, Admin Metric and Markup Metric. Then there are two textbox one for percentage and one for value... changing the value of one textbox will update the other textbox and vice versa"). Each metric's tile holds a Syncfusion `PercentTextBox` (`PercentDecimalDigits="1"`, `PercentPositivePattern="1"` for "25.0%"; the default `PercentEditMode` `DoubleMode` takes `PercentValue` in percent, 35 for 35%, and keeps the % sign while it's typed in, where `PercentMode` reads it as a fraction and showed 3,500.0%) and a `DoubleTextBox` beside it (`NumberDecimalDigits="2"`) bound to `EditingLaborAmount`, `EditingOverheadAmount`, `EditingAdministrativeAmount` or `EditingMarkupAmount`; the caption under both is the share of the price alone, since the amount now has a box. **Those settings go on each box, never in a `Style`**: the first cut put them in `MetricPercentStyle` and `MetricAmountStyle`, and the boxes came out unthemed (the user: "why does the textbox you use is not fluent themed?"), because `SfSkinManager.SetTheme` themes a control through its `Style`, which an explicit one replaces (`ApplyThemeAsDefaultStyle` is off). **The percentage stays the only thing kept**: an amount's getter is `Costs.Labor` (and so on), raised again on every `Costs` change, and its setter sets the percentage that comes to it, of the sub-assemblies cost (Markup: of the base cost), rounded to the costing columns' 4 decimals (`TypedAmount.PercentFor`). With no cost to take a percentage of, the percentage stays and the box goes back to 0.00. Worked out again from that percentage the amount can come back a hair off what was typed (23.77 as 23.7699…), and the binding would push that back into the box under the cursor, so `TypedAmount.Shown` hands back the typed amount while the percentage and its cost are still the ones it was typed against. VAT % keeps one box, a `PercentTextBox` in the same column so the five line up, with its amount and share under it. The right-hand breakdown's labels ("Labor (25% of sub-assemblies)"), the header's Markup % and Settings' Costing Defaults keep their names. Tests: `TypedAmountTests`, `ProductsViewModelRefreshTests.Each_metric_s_value_follows_its_percentage_and_a_value_typed_sets_the_percentage_Save_keeps`.
  - **Print** (2026-09-27, at the user's request: "add the print functionality to the Products that can preview and print the Product's Recipe and Costing"). A **Print** button on the header, between Revert and Delete (`PrintCommand`; always enabled, and a click while a picked product's details are still on their way does nothing, as Save's does: enabled with `AreDetailsReady`, it flickered off and on with every pick, fixed 2026-09-28), shows the main window's **Print tab** (`Views/ProductPrintView`): Syncfusion's print pane (`PrintPreview`, see the Syncfusion section), with an Include bar under it. The user chose each of the following over the alternatives offered:
    - **A main tab, not a dialog** (2026-09-27, at the user's request: "instead of a dialog, a tab alongside the other tabs appears for printing"; it was a modal `ProductPrintWindow` until then). The user chose both of the following over the alternatives offered:
      - **One Print tab, replaced.** `PrintTab` sits after Reports, hidden until Print is pressed (`ProductsViewModel.PrintRequests`, which `MainWindow` answers with `ShowTab(PrintTab)`), and closes with the strip's ✕ like the other tabs (`CloseMode="Hide"`). It isn't in the menu, since it prints what Products has open. One tab per product was offered and not taken.
      - **It follows Products each time it's shown.** Its "load" (`_loadCommands[PrintTab]`, run on every switch to it like the other tabs' LoadCommands) asks `ProductsViewModel.PreparePrintout()` for the product Products has open now, the edits made since and an open form included (applied first, as Print applies it). A product opened on Products since shows here too. Keeping the snapshot taken at Print was offered and not taken. While the details are still arriving the tab keeps its pages, and when a rule refuses the form it keeps them and says why in its Include bar, in the warning colour (Products shows the message too).
      - The pane is made once, with the first printout, and each later one is handed to it (`ProductPrintManager.PrintSet`, which lays it out again), so the printer, paper, orientation, margins and ticked parts stay as picked. It comes into the main window, themed at startup, so it gets the theme-first order it needs. `Back` and `Forward` treat it as a screen (`AppScreen.Print`, no record).
      - Checked in the app on the user's screen with their leave (one monitor): Print on Mini Ube-Cheese Ensaymada x 8 showed the tab with the fitted pane; Costing unticked, Cheese Roll opened on Products and the tab shown again showed Cheese Roll's recipe with no costs, at the pane's zoom; the ✕ hid the tab and went back to Products. Nothing saved or printed.
    - **Both parts in one printout, with tick boxes.** The Include bar's **Recipe** and **Costing** tick boxes print either part alone. The one still ticked can't be cleared (each box's `IsEnabled` is the other's value), so something always prints. The recipe part printed with the costing keeps its costs. **Printed without the costing it has none** (the user's request, 2026-09-27, after first choosing to keep them): the recipe tables drop Cost/g and Cost, the packaging table drops Unit Cost, Cost and its Total row, and each section's piece-size line drops its cost per piece, so the pages carry no cost at all (`ProductPrintLayout.RecipeCells`/`PackagingCells`).
    - **What the screen shows, unsaved edits included.**
      - A sub-assembly form left open is applied first, as Save applies it (`ApplyOpenForm`: `SelectRecipeTab(ApplyForm())`, then the tab shows its form again).
      - The apply is one undo step, measured as leaving a tab measures one (`History.End((before, now) => before.WithFormOf(now))`). A rule refusing the form shows its message, and the Print tab doesn't open.
      - The footer says "Includes unsaved changes" (`History.IsDirty`), or "Not saved yet" for a product never saved.
    - **The header button only.** Ctrl+P and an item in the list's right-click menu were offered and not taken.
    - **What's printed** (`ViewModels/ProductPrintout`, a snapshot taken each time the Print tab is shown, with no WPF in it):
      - The title: the name, then "Category · Stage · Per piece" (or "Per unit of 8 pieces"), then Last updated.
      - The **Recipe**, in recipe order. Each section (`PrintedSection.From`) has its heading, and a linked one says where it's from ("Cream Loaf Dough, from Sub-Assemblies"). Its table is Ingredient, Grams, Baker's % or Absolute %, Cost/g, Cost and Base (✓). Every cost-per-gram column header, in the app and in print, has read Cost/g since 2026-09-28, at the user's request; it was Cost per Gram or Cost per g. The Sub-Assemblies screen's Cost per g total tile keeps its name, with an "Add-ins" row and the add-ins under it, then a Total row (grams, the whole formula's %, the batch cost).
      - Under each section's table, a line with its shrinkage and final weight (when it shrinks), weight per piece, yield and cost per piece, or a note that it has no piece size yet.
      - Percentages are read as the recipe form reads them (`RecomputeUsageForm`): against the base's weight while a line is ticked, add-ins included, and against the whole batch otherwise. **Cost/g** (the ingredient's buffered cost per gram, 4 decimals, as the recipe form's column shows it) was left out at first, as it only explains Cost; the user asked for it on 2026-09-27, in every sub-assembly's table but not the packaging's. Add-ins show theirs too, since they share the table, though the form's add-ins grid has no such column. The Total row leaves it blank.
      - **The Base column's tick is drawn** (a `Path`), not typed. Segoe UI has no ✓, so WPF fell back to Segoe UI Emoji for it, and the job sent to the printer carried that whole font, 8 MB of it (a 4.2 MB spool file, 207 KB without it), where Segoe UI goes in as a subset. So the printout keeps to characters Segoe UI has, and `ProductPrintManagerTests` checks that every font in the printed pages is a subset (under 1 MB).
      - Then **Packaging**: its "Linked to …" note, then Material, Qty, Unit (the unit it's typed in), Unit Cost, Cost and a Total.
      - The **Costing**: Material Cost, Base Cost, Current Price and "Markup at Current Price" (the header's Markup %, named for what it is beside the breakdown's own Markup row). Then the Costing tab's breakdown (Component, per unit, % of Price), with each sub-assembly indented under Sub-assemblies, and the Subtotal and Total Selling Price on the accent's soft band.
      - A footer on every page: "Ube Roll — Recipe and Costing · Printed Sep 27, 2026, 3:40 PM", the save note, and "Page 1 of 2". The name and time wrap on to a second line rather than being cut short, as they were beside the save note for Mini Ube-Cheese Ensaymada x 8 ("Printed Sep 27, 2026, 1…"). The footer is measured once, with a two-digit page count, and every page's body is kept above it.
      - Colours are the Costing card's tokens, with two of their own: `PrintRuleBrush` (Surface 1) for the rules, since the card's Crust lines all but vanish on paper, and `PrintPaperBrush` (white) for the paper, which neither of the pane's page templates gives (see the Syncfusion section).
    - **Pagination** (`Printing/PrintPagination`, pure and tested without WPF). `ProductPrintLayout` builds every block in code, measures it at the printable width and paginates:
      - A heading and a table's header row are kept with what follows them. A section's last line is kept with its Total, and the Total with its piece-size line.
      - A table running on to a new page starts it with its heading, "(continued)", and its header row again.
      - The costing is kept on one page when it fits on one.
      - A block that starts a page (`StartsPage`, a set's product's title) starts one unless the page is still empty.
      - A block taller than a page gets a page to itself.
    - **A folder, or all products** (2026-09-27, at the user's request: "how can we extend this to print all products recipe and costing?"). The user took each of these recommendations:
      - **Which:** all products, or one folder of the Products list. The list's menus have **Print Folder** (a category's folder, or Uncategorized), **Print Stage** (a stage's) and **Print All Products** (its empty space); each asks for the Print tab with that choice (`PrintFolderCommand`, `PrintAllCommand`, `ProductsViewModel.PrintRequests` carrying a `PrintScope`). The tab's **Print** dropdown (a `ComboBoxAdv`) offers This product, the folder last asked for, and All products; another pick prepares the pages again. All products is both stages, Production first; a folder holds what the list files under it (`PrintScope.Holds`), read from the saved products, not the list, so a search on the list doesn't narrow it.
      - **As saved.** A set is read from the database (`ViewModels/SavedProductReader`), not from the screen, so the Products screen's unsaved edits aren't in it. When the product open there has some and is in the set, the tab's bar says "Cheese Roll has unsaved changes on Products; it's printed as saved." This product still prints the screen's edits, as before.
      - **A contents page, and a page per product.** The contents page has the set's title ("All Products", "Breads · Production", "Production"), "30 products · Recipe and Costing · Printed …", a line that the pages are as saved, and a table of Product, Folder and Page in the list's order (`ProductTree.InListOrder`). Each product then starts a page of its own (`StartsPage`). The Page figures come from the pagination: the rows are measured with "888" in their place, and filled in when a page is built (`ProductPrintLayout.StartPageOf`). Each footer names its page's product ("Ube Roll — Recipe · Printed …", or "All Products — Contents") and counts the pages over the whole document, and the footer's room is the tallest of them. An empty folder prints one page: "No products in Cakes · Production." Table numbers run on across products, so a continued heading is always its own product's.
      - `ViewModels/PrintTabViewModel` holds the choice (`Scopes`, `SelectedScope`, `Pick`) and prepares the pages (`LoadCommand`, the tab's load in `MainWindow`, which also runs when another choice is picked), showing "Preparing 12 of 30…" while a set is read. A newer preparation cancels one still running (a `CancellationTokenSource`; SqlClient reports a read cancelled half-way as a `SqlException`, which is ignored only then). `ProductPrintView` binds its bar to it and hands each `ProductPrintSet` to the manager. Another product or set opens at its first page (the pane's own `FirstCommand`, run once the pages are laid out); the same pages brought up to date keep their place. `PrintScope.ToString()` is its label, which a screen reader reads for the dropdown's choice (it read as the record's fields before).
      - Checked in the app on the user's screen with their leave (one monitor), nothing saved or printed: Print Folder on Production › Breads gave the tab "Breads · Production", a contents page of 13 breads over 26 pages, and page 18 was Mini Ube-Cheese Ensaymada x 8's first, as the contents said, with "Page 18 of 26". All products then gave 18 products over 33 pages, Production first, from page 1; unticking Costing made it 19 pages, the contents renumbered. The check found the new set opening at the old page (now its first), and the dropdown's names for UI Automation.
    - **One reader for a saved product** (`ViewModels/SavedProductReader`, 2026-09-27). A saved product was read and priced in two places, the Products screen's load and a copy in Reports, and a set would have made a third. Now `ReadDetailAsync` does the six reads Products did (`ProductDetail` moved out with them), and `ReadAsync`/`Build` turn them into what the screen shows: sections (`ProductUsageEdit.FromSaved`, moved from Products' `ToUsageEdit`), packaging rows (`PackagingLineRow.FromSaved`, was `ToPackagingRow`), the link text (`PackagingLinkTarget.From`, was `ToLinkTarget`) and the `ProductCostSummary`; the header texts are `ProductPrintout.CostUnitCaptionFor` and `LastUpdatedTextFor`. Products makes its own reader from the repositories it already has, so its constructor, and every test that builds one, is unchanged; Reports and the Print tab get the DI singleton. `PrintTabViewModelTests` checks that a saved product reads exactly as Products shows it once opened (`BeEquivalentTo`, figures and all).
    - Covered by `PrintPaginationTests`, `ProductPrintoutTests`, `ProductPrintLayoutTests` (a long name's footer, and a set's contents, pages and footers, included), `PrintTabViewModelTests`, `ProductPrintManagerTests` (the pages through Syncfusion's `GetPrintDocument()`: white and unframed, with font subsets only, and laid out again when a part is ticked out), `PrintPreviewThemeOrderTests`, `PrintPaneFitTests` and `ProductsViewModelPrintTests`. The WPF and Syncfusion tests run on an STA thread (`Fixtures/StaThread`; those that theme a window share one, `RunThemed`), and those needing a license skip without `SYNCFUSION_LICENSE_KEY`. No migration.
    - **Verified in the app** (2026-09-27, on the user's screen with their leave: real clicks, nothing saved, printing only to Microsoft Print to PDF, the PDFs deleted):
      - Mini Ube-Cheese Ensaymada x 8 printed as its tabs show it: Material Cost 22.73, Base Cost 35.93, Current Price 60.00, Markup 49.09% and Total Selling Price 64.39, with the Dough's batch cost of 131.60 at 203.5% and 1.00 a piece.
      - Unticking Costing left one page, with Recipe greyed out, and the other way round.
      - Landscape took three pages, the Topping running on under "Topping (continued)" and its header row. The PDF matched the preview.
      - A Current Price of 65, typed and not saved, printed as 65.00 and 61.52% under "Includes unsaved changes", and Undo put it back. A new product printed "Not saved yet".
      - At 1366 px wide the header's buttons all fit, and the name trims.
      - The check found the crash on the first Print (the theme order), the grey page and its frame, the empty PDFs (the ticket) and the footer's cut-off time, all fixed as described above.
  - **Unbuffered** (2026-09-29, at the user's request: "add a command in the Products View wherein the unbuffered cost will be use instead. we will use that command to toggle between buffered and unbuffered cost"). An **Unbuffered** button on the header, between Print and Delete (`ToggleCostBasisCommand`), switches every cost on the screen between the materials' `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit` and their true `CostPerBaseUnit`. While it's on, an **Unbuffered costs** badge (`LinkedBadgeStyle`) sits beside the title. The user chose each of these over the alternatives offered:
    - **For the session, not saved.** `ProductsViewModel.CostBasis` (`Domain/Enums/CostBasis`: `Buffered`, `Unbuffered`) isn't part of the product, its snapshots or `History`, so the switch adds no undo step and doesn't make Save available. It stays as it is from one product to the next and after Revert. Every launch starts on buffered, and no setting is stored.
    - **Print follows it**, This product and a set alike (`PrintTabViewModel.PrepareSetAsync` reads at `_products.CostBasis`), and the page says so: " · Unbuffered costs" after the title's cost caption and in the Costing heading (`ProductPrintout.CostBasis`, `ProductPrintLayout.BasisSuffix`). **Reports stay buffered** (`SavedProductReader.ReadAsync` without a basis).
    - **How it reaches every figure.** `RawMaterialRules.CostPerBaseUnitAt(material, basis)` is the one place that picks the cost.
      - The immutable section types hold both: `SubAssemblyLineEdit.CostAt`/`CostPerGramAt`, and `ProductUsageEdit.TotalCostAt`/`CostPerPieceAt`, both worked out in its constructor, which replaced `TotalCost` and `CostPerPiece`. So a switch rebuilds nothing, undo snapshots and saved-versus-edited comparisons are untouched, and `RecomputeCosts` reads the basis.
      - The live rows (`IngredientLineRow`, `PackagingLineRow`) have a settable `CostBasis`, `Buffered` by default, so Raw Materials and Sub-Assemblies are unchanged. A `PackagingLineRow` prices its unit choices again and keeps its unit. The screen sets the basis on every row put in `FormLines`, `FormAddIns`, `FormPackagingLines` and `LineEdits` (`OnItemAdded`: a read, an undo, a form opened), in the add rows' factories, and on every row when it's switched.
      - A composite's products: `SavedProductReader.ReadRecipeCostsAsync` returns `RecipeCosts(Buffered, Unbuffered)`, pricing each product twice from the one read (`Build(product, detail, basis)` is pure), and `SavedComponent` carries them. The screen keeps both per product (`_componentCosts`) and resolves its rows at the basis (`ComponentCostsAtBasis`), so a switch reads nothing again.
      - The pickers' pop-ups (the form's ingredient, add-in and packaging pickers, and the Packaging tab's) each have a Buffered and an Unbuffered cost column, one hidden. `controls:CostBasisColumns.Basis`, bound on the SfDataGrid, hides the one that doesn't match (`CostBasisColumns.Shows` marks them), since a `GridMultiColumnDropDownList`'s columns aren't in the visual tree and can't bind.
    - **The button is named for the costs it switches to** (the user's request, the same day: "Unbuffered button should change its Text Content to Buffered when the costs becomes unbuffered"): **Unbuffered** on the buffered costs, **Buffered** on the true ones (`CostBasisButtonLabel`, and `CostBasisButtonTooltip` to match). It first shipped as a checkable `ButtonAdv` kept pressed while unbuffered; that went with the label, since a pressed button reading Buffered contradicts itself, and the badge says which costs show. (Kept pressed, it needed code-behind: the Windows11Light template draws the pressed look only with `IsCheckable` and `IsChecked` both true, and a checkable `ButtonAdv` flips `IsChecked` itself with `SetValue` on a mouse press, `OnMouseLeftButtonDown`, decompiled 34.2.9, which drops a one-way binding, and not on a key.)
  - **Composite products on the screen** (2026-09-29; the domain side is under Composite products in the Domain Model):
    - **Making one.** **New** is a `SplitButtonAdv`: its button starts a product, and its arrow offers New product and New composite product. The items are flattened `ButtonAdv`s that close the drop-down on click, as Add Sub-Assembly's are.
      - The list's stage, category and Uncategorized folders offer New Composite Product Here, and its empty space New Composite Product (`NewCompositeCommand`, `NewCompositeProductInCommand`).
      - `IsEditingComposite` is set with a product's details (`SetComposite`), and a new one's by `StartNewProduct(isComposite)`. The header reads "New composite product", and the costs "Per unit".
    - **The Products tab** (`ComponentsRecipeTab`, AllApps U+E71D) comes first, before the composite's own sections' tabs. `MirrorUsageTabs` mirrors `UsageEdits` index for index after it (`SectionTabOffset`), and the add tab goes after the sections (`InsertAddSubAssemblyTab`). A composite opens on its Products tab, and deleting its last section falls back to it.
      - `ComponentsSectionView` is typed straight into, like the Packaging tab. Its columns are Product (a `GridMultiColumnDropDownList` over `ComponentProductOptions`), Qty, Recipe Cost and Cost, with Remove Line, and rows drag into order. Qty is an `int?` in a 0-decimal `NumericColumn`: WPF converts the DoubleTextBox's double through System.Convert.
      - The rows are `ProductComponentRow`s. A product's recipe cost is read with the composite's detail, off the UI thread the first time it's picked (`FindRecipeCost` starts the read, so the add row prices itself before it's committed), and again on every `LoadCommand`, so a product repriced elsewhere reaches its lines.
      - A reprice isn't an edit: the undo snapshot holds only ids and quantities (`ComponentLineState`, `RecipeTabKind.Components`).
      - The caption's figure is what the products bring alone (`ComponentsCost`, the Cost column's sum), without the composite's own sections.
      - **Include each product's packaging** (2026-09-30) is a plain `CheckBox` on Remove Line's row, as the app's other checkboxes are. Its `IsChecked` binds two-way to `IsComponentPackagingTicked`, whose setter makes each change one undo step (`AsUndoStep`) around `EditingIncludesComponentPackaging`, which loading and undo set directly. A step around the box's focus (`FieldUndoStep`) would have merged every click made while it kept the cursor, and missed a click after an undo. A `Command` with a one-way `IsChecked` was tried first and dropped: UI Automation's Toggle calls `ToggleButton.OnToggle`, which doesn't run the command, so the box ticked and the costs didn't follow. `EditingIncludesComponentPackaging` is in the form snapshot, so Save offers itself and Revert takes it back. The caption above the table (`ComponentsCaption`) and the header's Material Cost tooltip say where the products' packaging goes. While it's ticked the table shows a **Packaging Cost** column, and each line's Cost is Qty × (Recipe Cost + Packaging Cost) (the user's follow-up the same day: "the products table should show the packaging cost column when the product's packaging is included and the Cost column should show the sum"). The costing still splits them: the Costing tab's product lines take `ProductComponentRow.RecipeCostTotal`, and the composite's packaging takes each row's `PackagingCostTotal`, while `Cost` and the caption's `ComponentsCost` (the Cost column's sum) include the packaging. The rows carry `PackagingCost` and an `IncludesPackaging` flag, which `EditingIncludesComponentPackaging`'s setter sets on every row and the add row before it raises the change, since the change recomputes the costs from the rows; `NewComponentRowFor` gives each new row the flag. The screen's subscription to the rows watches `ComponentProductId`, `Quantity`, `RecipeCost` and `PackagingCost` through **one** `AutoRefreshOnObservable(WhenAnyPropertyChanged(…))`, not a chained `AutoRefresh` each: adding a fourth link for `PackagingCost` deadlocked a full test run the same day (found with `dotnet-stack`). Each DynamicData `AutoRefresh` takes its own lock, and the subscriber sets the rows' costs (`Resolve`), so a cost read landing on a pool thread held the `RecipeCost` link's lock waiting for the `PackagingCost` one while a `LoadCommand` on another held them the other way round. The app runs both on the UI thread, so it only showed in tests, but it's the code's to avoid. The column is hidden and shown by `Controls/OptionalColumns` (a grid's columns aren't in the visual tree and can't bind), which also reapplies on the grid's `Loaded` in case the binding lands before the columns exist. Print follows: `PrintedComponent.PackagingCost`, set only when included, gives the printed table the same column (`ProductPrintLayout.ComponentColumnsWithPackaging`). A product picked is read for its packaging along with its recipe, and `FetchComponentCostsAsync` recomputes once it arrives, since only a recipe cost moving reprices through the rows.
    - **Add Sub-Assembly** and **Delete Sub-Assembly** are the same as a product's. The form offers a composite only the product-owned roles and Packaging (`RefreshFormRoleOptions`), and to link or copy only sub-assemblies in those roles and packaging (`RefreshLinkableOptions`). The composite-only Add Packaging and Delete Packaging buttons of the first cut went once composites could have sections (2026-09-29).
    - **Quantity Required** hides for a composite. Its column takes no width (`BooleanToStarWidthConverter` gained a ConverterParameter, "0.5"), and the gap after it became its box's margin, so the gap goes too.
    - **The Costing tab** reads Products (ingredients), with each product under it as "Ensaymada × 6", and "% of products". The metric tooltips and the header's Material Cost tooltip follow the kind (`MetricBaseTooltip`, `MaterialCostTooltip`).
    - **Elsewhere:**
      - A composite's row in the list has a muted AllApps glyph after its name (`ProductTreeNode.IsComposite`).
      - Print puts its Products table where sections go (`ProductPrintout.Components`, `PrintedComponent`, `ProductPrintLayout.AddComponents`).
      - Reports' Pieces is blank for a composite.
      - Delete's confirmation says the products it holds stay.
  - **One Save writes the whole product.**
    - Any open form is applied first, then `ProductValidator` and `CostingParametersValidator` run.
    - The form is compared with what was loaded (the baseline: the product's recipe, its packaging lines and its costing). `IProductRepository.SaveAsync(product, recipe?, packagingLines?, costing?)` gets only what changed: the whole `RecipeDraft` (its own sub-assemblies with their lines, and its links, each with its piece size) or null; the packaging lines or null; the costing or null. (Until 2026-09-14 it took each unlinked role whose usage set differed.)
    - One context and one transaction, as for Raw Materials: the product (added or updated from a copy of its fields, stamping `UpdatedAt`), its recipe, the packaging lines, the costing, then the commit. Each step goes through `internal static` write methods (`ProductRepository.AddAsync`/`UpdateAsync`, `ProductSubAssemblyUsageRepository.SetRecipeAsync` — `SetUsagesForRoleAsync` until 2026-09-14 — `PackagingLineRepository.SetLinesAsync`, `CostingParametersRepository.SetAsync`), so every check (own and linked sub-assemblies, names, lines, piece sizes, duplicates, linked packaging, packaging type) runs, and a rejection anywhere rolls everything back — sub-assemblies included. The caller's entities are never attached, so a rolled-back save leaves no id on them. `ProductCloner` adds its product through the same static `AddAsync`.
    - Afterwards `LoadCommand` runs and a fresh copy of the product is selected (`ReloadSavedProductAsync`), so the form and its baseline are what was saved. `LoadCommand`'s ten reads go at once on the thread pool (2026-09-28): each is mostly a round trip's wait, and one after another, each resuming on the UI thread, they took 130–250 ms against the dev database, against about 10 ms at once (measured outside the app).
    - **Since 2026-09-23, Save waits for its reload**: it keeps each new section on the Sub-Assemblies screen, and until the reload shows them linked the form still has them as new, so a second Save in that moment would keep each twice. `ReloadSavedProductAsync` cleared `AreDetailsReady`, which Save already waits on and the tabs' hit-testing follows, and the reload set it again. **Since 2026-09-28 the reload finishes inside Save** (the user reported a flicker on every Save): selecting the same product closes its form (`ResetDetails`), and the detail was read only after that, so the screen drew the form without its section until the read came back and opened it again. Now `ReloadSavedProductAsync` reads the product and its detail first, at once on the thread pool, and hands the detail to the selection's load (`_preloadedDetail`), which shows it without awaiting: the form closes and opens again in one pass. Nothing is left to arrive once Save returns, so it no longer clears `AreDetailsReady`. Delete's confirmation now says the product's sub-assemblies stay on the Sub-Assemblies screen, and nothing but a packaging link refuses it.
    - Linking, copying and Make a Copy of a sub-assembly are recipe edits, saved by Save. The packaging link and Clone stay immediate: Link and Unlink Packaging reload only the link and the packaging lines with their baseline, so unsaved edits elsewhere survive. Clone copies the product as saved — its own copy of every sub-assembly. Delete's confirmation says the product's own sub-assemblies go with it, and it's refused while another product links one of them or its packaging.
    - Covered by `ProductSavePersistenceTests`, and by a `ProductsViewModelRefreshTests` test that fills in a new product and saves it with one Save.
  - **Three layout fixes came out of checking it in the app:**
    - **`MouseWheelForwarding`** (`controls:MouseWheelForwarding.IsEnabled="True"`): an `SfDataGrid` with no height shows every row and has nothing to scroll, yet its own ScrollViewer still takes the wheel, so the tab stopped scrolling whenever the pointer was over a section. It passes the wheel on to the grid's parent.
  - **Verified in the app** on the secondary monitor, against `TinapayanRMS_Demo` (restored from a `COPY_ONLY` backup and seeded with two products, one linked to the other). Each section's total equals its header. The doughnut's shares add up to 100%. The breakdown's total equals the header's price, administrative cost included. The wheel scrolls the tab over a table. A new product filled in, with its sub-assembly form still open, was saved by one Save. A usage added to a saved product was saved, Last Updated moved, and the packaging was left alone. A linked section is read-only with its badge. A raw material repriced on Raw Materials reached the sections while an unsaved labor cost was kept.
  - **Verified again after sub-assemblies became owned (2026-09-14)**, on the secondary monitor against a migrated copy of `TinapayanRMS_Demo`, with real clicks and keystrokes:
    - the Sub-Assemblies tab is gone;
    - Cream Loaf Mini's former Dough-tab link shows as "Sweet Dough · From Cream Loaf", and its material cost matched the one recorded before migrating (₱10.3229);
    - Cream Loaf's Sweet Dough shows "Also used by Cream Loaf Mini, Ube Cheese Roll";
    - a new sub-assembly made in the form (a line added in grams, a yield of 100 typed, 50 g per piece worked out) was saved by Save as a typed yield, and the material cost rose by its ₱2.60;
    - removing the shared Sweet Dough and deleting Cream Loaf were both refused, naming the linking products;
    - Make a Copy on the Mini's linked dough saved it as the Mini's own at the same weight and cost;
    - linking and unlinking the Mini's packaging added and then kept Cream Loaf's box;
    - a role was added in Settings → Roles;
    - Butter Icing's BOM card shows Component and Grams only.
  - The small secondary monitor (1,366 × 768) leaves little height for the sub-assembly form under the header; it scrolls with the tab, but the lines grid's fixed height (200 px since the lines became typed into it) takes the wheel while the pointer is over it.
- **The Settings dialog holds the lookups every screen picks from: units of measure, categories, and (since 2026-09-14) sub-assembly roles** (2026-09-13, at the user's request: the embedded Manage categories panels were hard to find, and Units of Measure took a whole tab). Since 2026-09-17 it has two category tabs: **Material Categories** (the raw materials' list, `CategoriesView`, formerly just "Categories") and **Product Categories** (`ProductCategoriesView`/`ProductCategoriesViewModel`, the same shape over `IProductCategoryRepository`).
  - **It opens from the gear at the bottom of the navigation drawer.** Until 2026-09-22 that was a `FooterItems` entry with `ItemType="Button"` — Syncfusion's docs say a Button-type item "is not selectable and only ItemClicked and Command actions can be performed", so opening Settings left the current page highlighted, and Syncfusion's own `DisplayMode.xaml` demo puts Settings in `FooterItems` too. With the drawer now in `Default` mode, `FooterItems` isn't rendered at all: Settings is a `ButtonAdv` pinned below the menu's `ListBox`, **outside** it, which is what keeps it from taking the menu's selection — the same guarantee by a different route. Verified in the app: opening Settings closes the menu and leaves the highlight on the page showing.
  - **`SettingsWindow`** is a modal `Window` owned by `MainWindow`, with fixed `TabControlExt` tabs (like Products' sub-tabs: no close buttons or context menus) for `UnitsOfMeasureView`, `CategoriesView` and `SubAssemblyRolesView` (and `ProductCategoriesView` and `CostingDefaultsView` since), and a Close button (`IsCancel`, so Esc closes it too). It takes the main window's theme (`SfSkinManager.SetTheme(settings, SfSkinManager.GetTheme(this))`) and sets `SizeMode="Default"` itself (`Touch` until 2026-09-15), since a new window is a new visual tree.
  - It's **built afresh each time it opens, over singleton ViewModels** (`UnitsOfMeasureViewModel`, `CategoriesViewModel`), so a tab keeps its selection and unsaved edits between visits. Anything a view hooks onto its longer-lived ViewModel is undone with it: `SettingsWindow` registers the delete confirmations and disposes them when it closes (`RegisterNativeMessageBoxHandler` returns the registration for this), and each view's focus-the-Name-box subscription lives from `Loaded` to `Unloaded`.
    - **Its controls' own ties to what outlives it are undone on `Closed` too (`ReleaseControls`, 2026-09-25)**. Before, every closed Settings stayed in memory for the rest of the session, and its grids went on taking in each reload of the ViewModels' lists. Found while checking the app against Infragistics' "15 WPF Performance Tips"; the other tips were already met or don't apply. Three Syncfusion behaviours, each confirmed by decompiling 34.2.9 and by heap dumps (`dotnet-dump` `gcroot`):
      - An `SfDataGrid`'s view subscribes strongly to its `ItemsSource`'s `CollectionChanged` and holds the grid. Only `Dispose` unhooks it; `OnUnloaded` doesn't.
      - An editable `ComboBoxAdv` in `Suggest` mode (every picker, via `App.ApplyPickerDefaults`) combines its filter into the list's shared default view, and takes it off only while it's loaded.
      - An `SfTextBoxExt` that loads before its template has found its window hooks `Application.Current.Windows[0]`'s `MouseDown` (the main window), then unhooks from its own window instead, so only its `Dispose` removes it. In Settings that's the Name box of every tab but the first. `TabControlExt` loads every tab's content when the window opens, and a tab not showing isn't templated until it's first shown, so this happened on every open, whether or not those tabs were visited.
      - So the window disposes each grid and text box, and takes each picker's filter back off its view, walking the logical tree to reach the tabs not showing. A grid's `SelectedItem` binding and a text box's bindings are cleared first: `SfTextBoxExt.Dispose` sets `Text` to empty, which would reach the ViewModel through the two-way binding. A tab still keeps its selection and unsaved edits between visits.
      - Measured off-screen against the dev database, with a probe that opens and closes Settings visiting every tab under a themed stand-in main window: 5 of 5 closed dialogs survived a full GC before, 0 of 5 after. The new-category prompt (`TextPromptWindow`) never leaked. Any new window built per use over long-lived ViewModels needs the same care.
  - Each opening loads both lists. When it closes, `MainWindow` refreshes the screen on show so its pickers include anything added or renamed; the other tabs pick changes up when they're next shown, as for any change.
  - **Both tabs reset their form after Save and Delete**, as New does, and title it "New Unit"/"New Category" or "Edit Unit"/"Edit Category". Before, Save and Delete cleared the list selection but left the form pointing at the last record, so the next Save silently renamed it (or failed, after a Delete) instead of adding one. The user ran into this while asking how to add a category.
- **Migrations that carry data over use hand-added SQL, each verified up and down on a restored copy of the dev database** — the three multi-unit migrations and the raw materials merge (`BACKUP … COPY_ONLY` / `RESTORE … WITH MOVE`, then `dotnet ef database update --connection` on the copy):
  - `IngredientAlternativeUnits`: each purchase unit + `PurchaseUnitQty` becomes an `IngredientUnit`, prices are linked to it, `CostPerGram` is recomputed from the true latest price (healing the same-day bug), then the old columns are dropped. The scaffold dropped the columns first; the operations were reordered so the SQL runs in between.
  - `RecipeLinesInAlternativeUnits`: `Quantity = Grams` for existing lines.
  - `PackagingMaterialAlternativeUnits`: rewritten by hand — the scaffold took `PurchaseUnitQty → CostPerBaseUnit` for a rename (both decimals). A purchase unit other than the base unit becomes a `PackagingMaterialUnit` with prices linked to it; a purchase unit equal to the base unit turns prices into per-base-unit prices; `CostPerBaseUnit` comes from the latest price. `Down` restates everything in the old one-purchase-unit shape, including packaging lines entered in an alternative unit.
  - `MergeRawMaterials` (the raw materials merge): rewritten by hand — the scaffold dropped the eight old tables before creating the four new ones. It creates `RawMaterialCategories`, `RawMaterials`, `RawMaterialUnits` and `RawMaterialPriceHistories` first, copies everything across in one SQL batch (table variables map old packaging ids to new ones), then drops the old tables. Ingredient rows keep their ids (`IDENTITY_INSERT`), so recipe lines, BOM lines and their units still point at them; packaging materials, units and prices get new ids after them, and packaging lines follow. A packaging category joins the ingredient category of the same name (case-insensitively, like the unique index), and a packaging material named like an ingredient gets " (packaging)" appended, since names are now unique across both. `Down` splits the tables back by type with every id kept, each category going to both old category tables. Verified on a restored copy of the dev database — the user's 4 ingredients, 5 categories, 5 units and 5 prices came through with identical ids and costs — then with packaging data seeded into the old schema (a category differing only in case, a material named like an ingredient, units, prices per unit and per base unit, and packaging lines in both): up, down and up again. The dev database was backed up (`COPY_ONLY`) before the migration was applied to it.
  - `AddCostBuffer`: adds `CostBufferPercent` and `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit` (both default 0), sets `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit = CostPerBaseUnit` for every material (no buffer yet, so no cost changes), then adds `CK_RawMaterials_CostBufferPercent`. Verified up, down and up on a restored copy (every row's buffered cost matched its cost; the constraint rejected 101%), then applied to the dev database after a `COPY_ONLY` backup.
  - `ProductDescriptionUpdatedAtAndAdministrativeCost` (2026-09-14) needed no hand SQL: it only adds columns, `Description` (`nvarchar(500)`) and `UpdatedAt` (`datetime2`), both nullable, on Products, and `AdministrativeCost` (`decimal(18,4) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`) on CostingParameters. Applied to the dev database after a `COPY_ONLY` backup.
  - `ProductCategories` (2026-09-17) needed no hand SQL: it adds the `ProductCategories` table (unique `Name`) and a nullable `Products.CategoryId` with a Set Null FK, so existing products stay uncategorized. Applied to the dev database.
  - `ProductCurrentPrice` (2026-09-19) needed no hand SQL: it only adds a nullable `Products.CurrentPrice` (`decimal(18,4)`), so existing products have none entered. Applied to the dev database.
  - `ProductOwnedSubAssemblies` (2026-09-14): rewritten by hand — the scaffold dropped `ProductTabLinks` outright and added `OwnerProductId` as 0. In order:
    1. **Role-tab links become sub-assembly links.** A recursive CTE follows each role-tab link (`TabKind = 0`) to the end of its chain; the linking product's own, hidden usages in that role are deleted, and the end product's usages in that role are copied to it at the same weights. Then the role-tab rows go.
    2. **Sub-assemblies no product uses are deleted** (with their lines): they can't have an owner.
    3. **Owners**: `OwnerProductId` is added nullable, set to the lowest product id using each sub-assembly, then made NOT NULL with its Cascade FK; the unique `Name` index becomes `(OwnerProductId, Name)`.
    4. **`ProductTabLinks` → `PackagingLinks`** (`RenameTable`, keeping the Packaging-tab rows): `TabKind`, `SubAssemblyRoleId`, their FK and the filtered indexes go; the PK and FKs are re-created under the new name, with a plain unique index on `ProductId`.
    5. **Grams-only lines**: `Quantity` and `UnitId` (FK, index) leave `SubAssemblyLines` and `IngredientBomLines`; `Grams` already held every line's amount.
    6. **Piece size**: `WeightPerPiece` is renamed `AuthoredValue` and `AuthoredField` added at 0 (weight per piece), so every usage keeps its weight; then `CK_ProductSubAssemblyUsages_AuthoredValue`, and later `ShrinkagePercent` at 0 with `CK_ProductSubAssemblyUsages_ShrinkagePercent` (`UsageShrinkagePercent`), so every existing usage keeps its yield.
    - `Down` resolves a typed yield back to a weight (`TotalWeight / yield`), gives lines `Quantity = Grams` with no unit, restores `ProductTabLinks` with every row a Packaging tab, and appends " (owner name)" to a sub-assembly name that another owner also uses before restoring the global unique index. It can't restore role-tab links (they stay the shared usages they became) or the units lines were entered in; the migration's comment says so.
    - Verified up, down and up on a restored copy of `TinapayanRMS_Demo`: every product's sub-assembly cost per piece matched before and after each step (22.567141, 10.322941, 14.028705); Cream Loaf came to own Sweet Dough, Butter Cream, Custard Filling and Crumb Topping, and Cream Loaf Mini's Dough-tab link became a link to Sweet Dough at 60 g; the two lines entered in units kept their grams. Then, seeded into the new schema, a typed yield (100 pieces → 1 g a piece down), a sub-assembly name shared by two owners (renamed down) and a packaging link (kept both ways). Applied to the dev database (no products or sub-assemblies yet; its one BOM line kept its 500 g) after a `COPY_ONLY` backup.
  - `CostingPercentagesOfMaterialCost` (2026-09-15) needed no hand SQL: EF scaffolded three `RenameColumn`s on CostingParameters (`LaborCost` → `LaborPercent`, `OverheadCost` → `OverheadPercent`, `AdministrativeCost` → `AdministrativePercent`), same `decimal(18,4)`. **The numbers carry over unchanged, so a saved ₱4 labor cost reads as 4%.** Converting them would mean working out every product's material cost in SQL (sub-assembly piece sizes, buffered costs, packaging through its link chain), and the dev database had no costing rows to convert; only `TinapayanRMS_Demo`'s three products have old peso amounts. Verified up, down and up on a restored copy of `TinapayanRMS_Demo` (the values survived each step), then applied to the dev database after a `COPY_ONLY` backup. In the app on that copy: Cream Loaf's ₱23.2754 material cost at 25%, 10% and 5% came to ₱5.8189, ₱2.3275 and ₱1.1638, a ₱32.5856 subtotal and ₱47.44 with a 30% markup and 12% VAT; Save stored 25, 10 and 5, and Reports showed the same ₱32.59 and ₱47.44.
  - `RawMaterialCostPrecision` (2026-09-19) needed no hand SQL: two `AlterColumn`s take `RawMaterials.CostPerBaseUnit` and `BufferedCostPerBaseUnit` from `decimal(18,4)` to `decimal(28,20)` (`HasPrecision` in `RawMaterialConfiguration`), so a buffered cost typed per bag or kilo is kept as typed once divided down to the base unit. EF warns of possible data loss because 8 integer digits replace 14; no cost per base unit comes near ₱100,000,000. 28 digits in all is what a .NET `decimal` holds, so any stored value reads back without overflowing (a larger scale, such as `decimal(38,28)`, would overflow on reading anything from ₱8 up). Existing values carry over unchanged. Applied to the dev database.
